# Issue 266: Issue 267: Feature: Implement multi-channel logging provider for OmniLogger (Online & Offline Support)`n`n[Description]`nDescription
Currently, our OmniLogger primarily handles console output and UI-based feedback (via OmniGlassPanel). To ensure system stability, facilitate debugging in production, and generate analytical reports, we need to extend the logger to push data to professional logging systems.

The goal is to decouple the logging logic from the UI and send logs to both local persistent storage and cloud-based monitoring services.

Objectives
Offline Logging: Store logs in a local file or a lightweight database (e.g., Hive or Sembast) for offline diagnostics.

Online/Cloud Logging: Integrate with industry-standard services (e.g., Sentry, Firebase Crashlytics, or Logtail).

Search & Filtering: Enable the ability to filter logs by OmniMessageType (Error, Warning, Info) and timestamps.

Performance: Ensure logging calls do not block the UI thread (Asynchronous execution).

Proposed Technical Stack
Sentry / Firebase Crashlytics: For real-time error tracking and remote monitoring.

Logger (Dart Package): For formatted local console and file output.

Path Provider: To manage local directory access for log files.

Requirements & Tasks
[ ] Create an Abstract Log Provider: Define an interface OmniLogProvider so we can swap different services easily.

[ ] Implementation - Local File Provider: Create a class that writes logs to the device's internal storage.

[ ] Implementation - Sentry/Firebase Provider: Integrate a cloud provider for production error reporting.

[ ] Buffer System: Implement a simple queueing mechanism so logs are sent in batches to save battery/data.

[ ] Update OmniLogger: Refactor OmniLogger.log() to iterate through all registered providers.

Sample Payload Format
JSON
{
  "timestamp": "2026-05-10T10:30:00Z",
  "level": "Error",
  "title": "OCR_PROCESS_FAILED",
  "message": "Failed to extract text from image",
  "device_info": { "os": "Android", "version": "14" },
  "context": "ScannerWorkflow"
}

To optimize the developer experience (DX) and allow for rapid testing without polluting production data, we need a mechanism to toggle between the Local Database (ObjectBox) and the Remote Cloud API. This is especially critical when working on offline features or testing database schemas in a sandbox environment.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a developer, I want the app to default to the local database while I am coding (Debug Mode) but automatically use the online database in the final product (Release Mode), with the ability to manually override this setting during testing.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements
1. Persistent Environment Management

Create an EnvironmentService (extending GetxService) to track the isRemoteEnabled state.

Logic: * If kDebugMode is active: Default to false (Local).

If kReleaseMode is active: Default to true (Remote).

Use a reactive boolean (RxBool) so the UI updates instantly when the source changes.

2. UI Implementation (Debug-Only)

Source Toggle: Implement a toggle switch within a OmniGlassPanel. This toggle should only be visible if kDebugMode is true.

Visual Indicators: Add a status dot in the OmniAppBar (Green for Remote, Amber for Local) to provide immediate visual feedback on the current data source.

Standardization: * Use AdaptiveText for all labels.

Use withValues for all color opacities to ensure compatibility with Flutter 3.22+.

3. Integration with Controllers

Update TransactionController (and any data-fetching controllers) to check EnvironmentService.isRemoteEnabled before executing fetch or save operations.

≡ƒ¢á Implementation Plan
File 1: lib/services/environment_service.dart
Description: A new service to manage the global state of the data source.

Dart
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';
import 'package:get/get.dart';

class EnvironmentService extends GetxService {
  // Defaults to Local in Debug, Remote in Release
  final isRemoteEnabled = (!kDebugMode).obs;

  void toggleDataSource(bool useRemote) {
    isRemoteEnabled.value = useRemote;
    Get.snackbar(
      "Data Source Updated",
      useRemote ? "Connected to Cloud API" : "Connected to Local ObjectBox",
      snackPosition: SnackPosition.BOTTOM,
    );
  }
}

[ ] Stability: Switching sources during runtime does not trigger a crash.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 263: Issue 264: Implementation of Global Connectivity Management & Cross-Platform Offline Logic`n`n[Description]`nHere is the formal **GitHub Issue** drafted in English, following professional software engineering standards for your project **GrowthPilotAI**.

---

### **Issue Title:** Implementation of Global Connectivity Management & Cross-Platform Offline Logic

#### **Problem Statement**
Currently, the application does not gracefully handle network transitions. To ensure a professional user experience (UX) and prevent runtime exceptions (crashes) during data-dependent operations (like OCR or API calls), we need a robust connectivity layer that adapts its behavior based on the host platform (Mobile vs. Web).

#### **Proposed Solution**
Implement a `ConnectivityService` using `connectivity_plus` to monitor real-time network status. The application should switch between local-first processing (Mobile/ObjectBox) and connectivity-aware UI barriers (Web).

---

#### **Detailed Requirements**

**1. Core Connectivity Service**
* Create a `ConnectivityService` (extending `GetxService`) to provide a reactive `isOnline` stream.
* Implement a "Platform Guard" to differentiate between Mobile (Android/iOS) and Web environments.

**2. Platform-Specific Behavior**
* **Mobile (Android/iOS):**
    * **Graceful Degradation:** When offline, API-dependent features must silently switch to local processing (e.g., storing transactions in ObjectBox for later sync).
    * **Crash Prevention:** Intercept network requests to prevent "SocketException" or timeout crashes.
* **Web:**
    * **Blocking Overlay:** If the connection is lost, display a non-dismissible overlay/message: *"You are currently offline. Please wait for the connection to be restored."*
    * **Interaction Freeze:** Disable critical UI actions until the network is back.

**3. Sync UI & Animation (Post-Connection Logic)**
* **Indicator:** Once the app transitions from *Offline* to *Online*, a Sync Icon (e.g., `Icons.sync`) must appear in the Global AppBar.
* **Animation:** The icon should use a continuous rotation animation (using `flutter_animate`) to indicate background synchronization.
* **Auto-Dismissal:** The Sync Icon must disappear automatically as soon as the user performs any interaction (Tap, Scroll, or Navigation).

**4. Error Handling & Interceptors**
* Implement a global HTTP interceptor to catch 408 (Timeout) and connectivity errors, returning a user-friendly custom Exception instead of a system crash.

---

#### **Technical Tasks**
- [ ] Add `connectivity_plus` to `pubspec.yaml`.
- [ ] Develop `ConnectivityService` with `RxStatus`.
- [ ] Implement the **Web Offline Overlay** component.
- [ ] Design the **Animated Sync Icon** in the `OmniAppBar`.
- [ ] Add a `UserInteractionListener` to handle the dismissal of the sync indicator.
- [ ] Test offline scenarios for `OCRService` and `TransactionController`.

#### **Acceptance Criteria**
* The app remains functional and responsive when the network is toggled off on Mobile.
* The Web version correctly blocks interactions during downtime.
* The Sync Icon triggers immediately upon reconnection.
* The Sync Icon vanishes after the first user gesture.
* No "No Internet" related crashes are reported in the logs.

---

### Suggested Implementation Hint (for the Sync Icon):
```dart
// In your AppBar actions:
Obx(() => connectivityService.showSyncIcon.value 
  ? Icon(Icons.sync)
      .animate(onPlay: (controller) => controller.repeat())
      .rotate(duration: 1000.ms)
  : SizedBox.shrink()
)
```

Since the standard version of ObjectBox for Flutter does not support full database encryption (it's an Enterprise feature), we need to implement a manual encryption layer for sensitive user data to comply with PIPEDA and maintain high security standards for financial information.

Scope:

Implement field-level encryption for sensitive entities (e.g., Transaction amounts, descriptions, and category names).

Utilize the encrypt package for AES-256 implementation.

Integrate with the existing SecureStorageService to retrieve the db_encryption_key_v1.

Proposed Implementation Steps:

Create an EncryptionHelper utility class.

Modify ObjectBox entities to store encrypted strings instead of plain text for sensitive fields.

Security & Compliance v1.0`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 261: Issue #262: AI-Driven Dynamic Insights & Adaptive UI Factory`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Context
The goal is to move away from static filtering. We need to implement an AI Orchestration Layer that translates natural language user prompts (e.g., "How much did I spend on utilities last month?") into precise database queries. Instead of a standard list, the system should intelligently decide which visual format (KPI Card, Bar Chart, or Transaction List) best represents the data answer.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a business owner, I want to ask questions about my finances in plain English or Persian and see the results instantly in a professional, visual format, so I can make quick decisions without manually configuring reports.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements
1. The "Insight Engine" (NLP to Query)
Create a service InsightEngine that interfaces with a Lightweight LLM (e.g., Gemini Nano or API).

Input: User string ("Show me my profit margin from last quarter").

Output: A structured JSON object containing:

dateRange: { start: DateTime, end: DateTime }

filters: { categoryId: int?, type: Enum? }

visualPreference: "chart" | "kpi" | "list"

2. Data-to-Visual Mapper
A logic layer that analyzes the query results.

If the result is a single total -> KPI Card.

If the result spans across time -> Line/Bar Chart.

If the result is a specific set of items -> Glassmorphism List.

3. Adaptive UI Component (The Factory)
Implement DynamicInsightWidget: A container that uses AnimatedSwitcher to transition between different visualization states.

Integrate fl_chart for high-fidelity, interactive graphs.

Maintain the OmniGlassPanel design language for all AI-generated cards.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
The AI Response Model:

Dart
class AIInsightResponse {
  final Map<String, dynamic> queryParameters;
  final String visualHint; // 'chart_bar', 'kpi_summary', 'list'
  final String aiSummary;   // "You spent $1,200 on Rogers in April."

  AIInsightResponse({
    required this.queryParameters, 
    required this.visualHint, 
    required this.aiSummary
  });
}
The UI Factory Logic:

Dart
Widget buildDynamicWidget(AIInsightResponse response, List<TransactionEntity> data) {
  switch (response.visualHint) {
    case 'chart_bar':
      return AnimatedBarChart(transactions: data);
    case 'kpi_summary':
      return FinancialKpiCard(
        title: "Total Expenses",
        amount: data.fold(0, (sum, item) => sum + item.amount),
      );
    default:
      return TransactionListPanel(transactions: data);
  }
}

[ ] Visual Fidelity: Charts must use the app's primary accent colors (Blue/Indigo) with glassmorphism backgrounds.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Local Privacy: The actual transaction amounts and descriptions stay on the device. Only the structure of the question is sent to the AI for parsing.

[ ] UI adheres to the established Design System (Material 3 + Glassmorphism).`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 259: Issue 260: Real-Time Document Preview & Validation Layer`n`n[Description]`n
User Story:
As a Business Analyst, I want to see a live preview of the formatted report within the app before finalizing the export, so that I can ensure the white-labeling, data extraction, and digital signatures are perfectly placed.

---

 1. Implementation Strategy: The "Twin-Rendering" Approach

To ensure high performance, we use a two-tier rendering strategy:

 Tier 1: Fast Flutter Preview (UI Mockup)
     Instead of waiting for a full PDF generation, Flutter renders a "Mock" of the document using native widgets (`SliverList` or `CustomPainter`).
     This gives instant feedback on Branding (257) and Layout.
 Tier 2: Server-Side PDF Stream (High Fidelity)
     For the final check, the server generates a low-resolution "Preview PDF" and streams it to the app.
     Library: Use `native_pdf_view` or `pdfx` in Flutter to render the PDF pages as interactive widgets.



---

 2. Technical Workflow

| Phase | Action | Technology |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Data Bind | Flutter sends current draft data to Node.js. | `Socket.io` / `REST` |
| Snapshot | Node.js generates a single-page or low-res PDF. | `Puppeteer` (Fast-mode) |
| Stream | PDF is streamed as a `Uint8List` to Flutter. | `StreamBuilder` |
| Display | User scrolls through pages in the "Preview Modal." | `pdfx` package |
| Commit | User clicks "Confirm," triggering the S3 upload (254). | `S3 SDK` |

---

 3. Key Features for GrowthPilotAI

 Interactive Annotations: Allow users to tap on a section in the preview to "Edit" the source data. If a requirement description looks wrong, they can fix it right there.
 Page Toggling: A checkbox list to include/exclude specific pages (e.g., "Exclude Traceability Matrix from this version").
 Signature Placeholder: Show a "Sign Here" ghost-box where the Digital Signature (259) will be placed.



---

 4. Implementation Notes for Ahmad (Senior Dev)

 Memory Management: PDF rendering in Flutter can be memory-intensive. Ensure you dispose of the `PdfController` properly when the user exits the preview modal to keep the app smooth on older devices.
 Watermarking: During the preview phase, the server should overlay a "DRAFT" watermark. This prevents users from just screenshotting the preview instead of using the official (and potentially paid) export feature.
 Caching: Cache the preview pages locally. If the user only changes a single word, don't re-render the entire 50-page document; only update the affected page.

---

 5. Impact on Workflow
This feature significantly increases user trust. In the Surrey/Vancouver B2B market where accuracy is everything, providing a "What You See Is What You Get" (WYSIWYG) experience makes GrowthPilotAI feel like a premium, polished product.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 258: Issue 259: Digital Signature Integration & Executive Approval Workflow`n`n[Description]`nUser Story:
As a Project Manager or Stakeholder, I want to digitally sign the finalized requirements and reports within the app so that the documents are officially "frozen" and approved for the development phase.

---

 1. Technical Implementation Details

A. Client-Side (Flutter - The Signing Interface):
 Signature Canvas: Use the `signature` or `hand_signature` package to provide a smooth, pressure-sensitive drawing area for the user.
 Vector Capture: Capture the signature as an SVG path rather than a raster image. This ensures it looks professional and sharp even when embedded in high-resolution PDFs.
 Biometric Verification: Before allowing a signature to be applied, trigger FaceID / Fingerprint (using `local_auth`) to ensure the person holding the device is indeed the authorized manager.

B. Server-Side (Node.js - Cryptographic Binding):
 PDF Embedding: Use `pdf-lib` to modify the existing PDF generated in Phase 1. The signature is placed in a designated "Approval" section at the end of the document.
 Hashing for Integrity:  Generate a SHA-256 hash of the document at the moment of signing.
     Store this hash in your PostgreSQL database linked to the `user_id` and `timestamp`.
     If the document is edited later, the hash will no longer match, indicating the signature is void.
 Metadata Overlay: Add a hidden or visible footer to the signed page stating: "Digitally Signed by [Name] via GrowthPilotAI on [Timestamp] - ID: [Unique_Hash_Fragment]".



---

 2. The Approval Workflow (The "Frozen" State)

| Step | Action | Security Layer |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1. Review | Manager reviews the Batch Export bundle. | JWT / Role-based Access |
| 2. Auth | Manager confirms identity via Biometrics. | iOS FaceID / Android Biometrics |
| 3. Sign | Manager draws signature on the Flutter canvas. | Vector Path Capture |
| 4. Bind | Server merges signature into PDF and hashes file. | SHA-256 Cryptography |
| 5. Lock | The project status moves to `APPROVED / LOCKED`. | Database Constraint |

---

 3. Implementation Notes for Ahmad (Senior Dev)

 Audit Trail: Every signature event must be logged in your `audit_logs` table. This is critical for accountability in large Vancouver-based B2B ecosystems where multiple stakeholders are involved.
 Multi-Signature Support: Design the database schema to allow multiple signatures (e.g., one from the BA, one from the Lead Dev, and one from the Client).
 Legal Compliance: While this acts as an internal "Digital Signature," if you eventually need "Electronic Signature" compliance (like DocuSign/eSign), you will need to integrate with a Certificate Authority (CA). For now, the internal cryptographic hash is sufficient for project traceability.

---

 4. Impact on GrowthPilotAI
 Value: This moves the app from being a "drafting tool" to a "system of record."
 White-Labeling Synergy: The signature block will be styled using the branding colors and logo defined in Issue 257, creating a unified, professional appearance.

`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 255: Issue 256: Native File Persistence & Local Download Manager`n`n[Description]`nUser Story:
As a Business Analyst, I want the exported report to be automatically saved to my device's "Downloads" folder so that I can access it later from my file manager or attach it to an email outside of the app.

---

 Technical Implementation Details

1. Secure Download Handshake (Flutter):
 The Presigned URL: Use the `dio` package to handle the download from the S3 link. `dio` is preferred over the standard `http` package for its robust support for tracking progress and handling large binary streams.
 Progress Tracking:
    ```dart
    await dio.download(presignedUrl, localPath, onReceiveProgress: (received, total) {
      if (total != -1) {
        updateProgress(received / total); // Sync with Flutter UI
      }
    });
    ```

2. Scoped Storage & Path Resolution:
 Android: Use the `External Storage` path specifically targeting the `Download` directory. For Android 10+, you must use Scoped Storage logic to ensure you don't need broad storage permissions.
 iOS: Since iOS doesn't have a public "Downloads" folder in the same way, save the file to the app's `ApplicationDocumentsDirectory` and then use the `share_plus` or `open_file_plus` package to let the user "Save to Files."



3. User Experience (The "Success" State):
 Completion Notification: Once the file is moved to the final directory, trigger a Haptic Feedback and a SnackBar with an "Open" button.
 File Association: Use the `open_file_plus` package to automatically detect the file type (PDF/XLSX) and launch the system's default viewer (e.g., Acrobat Reader or Excel).

---

 The Final Integration Flow

| Component | Responsibility | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Node.js | Generates the file and uploads to S3 | Server-side |
| S3 | Hosts the file with a 15-minute expiration | Cloud |
| Flutter (Dio) | Streams the file from the URL to the device | Client-side |
| OS File System | Places the file in `/Downloads/GrowthPilotAI/` | Native |



---

 Implementation Notes for Ahmad:

 Permission Management: Use the `permission_handler` package. Check for `Permission.storage` (on older Android) or verify that you are using the correct MediaStore API for newer versions to avoid rejection during the Google Play/App Store review process.
 Filename Sanitization: Ensure the filename from the server is sanitized (no spaces or special characters) to prevent OS-level path errors during the move to the Downloads folder.
 Surrey/Vancouver Connectivity: For users on the SkyTrain or in areas with spotty 5G, implement a "Retry" logic in `dio` so the download can resume if the connection drops momentarily.

---

 Module Conclusion: The "Zero-Effort" Export

With this final issue, your Exporting Module is now a fully automated pipeline. The AI extracts the data, the server renders the report, the cloud secures it, and Flutter delivers it directly to the user's hand.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 254: Issue 255: Server-Side PDF/Excel Generation & S3 Secure Delivery`n`n[Description]`nUser Story:
As a Business Analyst, I want to trigger large report generations on the server and receive a secure download link so that I can handle massive data sets without impacting my mobile device's performance.

---

 Technical Implementation Details

1. Request & Background Processing (Node.js):
 The Trigger: Flutter sends a POST request with the `project_id`. The server immediately acknowledges with a `job_id` and a `202 Accepted` status.
 Worker Pattern: The task is pushed to a BullMQ queue. This ensures that if ten users request an export at the same time, the server processes them sequentially without crashing.
 Data Assembly: The background worker pulls:
     PostgreSQL: Relational Traceability Data.
     GitHub API: Latest deployment and issue statuses.
     AI Engine: Summaries and risk analysis.

2. Cloud Storage Pipeline (AWS S3):
 Stream Upload: Instead of saving the file to the server's local disk, the generated buffer is streamed directly to an AWS S3 bucket using the AWS SDK.
 Bucket Configuration: The bucket is set to Private to ensure no data is publicly accessible.
 Lifecycle Policy: A rule is set on the bucket to automatically delete files in the `temp_exports/` folder after 24 hours to minimize storage costs and maintain data privacy.

3. Secure Link Delivery (Presigned URLs):
 Temporary Access: The server generates an S3 Presigned URL. This link grants temporary permission (e.g., valid for 15 minutes) for the Flutter app to download the file.
 Webhook/Socket Notification: Once the upload is complete and the link is generated, the server emits a `COMPLETED` event via Socket.io or a Firebase Cloud Message (FCM).

---

 The Implementation Workflow

| Stage | Action | Technology |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Trigger | User clicks "Export" in Flutter | `http` / `dio` |
| Queue | Request added to background task | `BullMQ` / `Redis` |
| Render | HTML/CSS converted to PDF | `Puppeteer` |
| Storage | File uploaded to Cloud | `AWS S3` |
| Deliver | Presigned URL sent back to user | `WebSockets` / `FCM` |

---

 Impact Analysis & Priority
 Priority: Critical (Architecture Standard).
 Value: This prevents "App Freezing" and ensures that the user can continue working on other parts of GrowthPilotAI while the report is being built in the cloud.

---

 Implementation Notes for Ahmad:
 Regional Latency: Since you are based in the Vancouver/Surrey area, using the `ca-central-1` (Canada Central) AWS region will provide the lowest latency for your local users.
 Error Handling: If the rendering fails (e.g., due to a malformed AI summary), the worker should catch the error and notify the Flutter app via the socket so the user isn't left waiting indefinitely.
 Security: Always include the `Content-Disposition: attachment` header in the S3 metadata to ensure the file is treated as a download rather than being opened as a webpage in the mobile browser.

Coverage Formula:

<img width="592" height="59" alt="Image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/909050a7-5738-45fe-a7d0-801facd044d1" />

Database Optimization (PostgreSQL):

Instead of multiple queries, use a single optimized SQL query to find "Orphaned Goals":

SQL
SELECT (COUNT(DISTINCT bg.id) FILTER (WHERE rm.req_code IS NOT NULL) * 100.0 / COUNT(bg.id)) as coverage_rate
FROM business_goals bg
LEFT JOIN goal_requirement_map rm ON bg.id = rm.goal_id;
2. Real-time Delivery (WebSockets):

Once the calculation is complete, the server emits a coverage_update event via Socket.io.

Payload structure:

JSON
{
  "projectId": "GP-2026",
  "overallCoverage": 85.5,
  "uncoveredGoals": ["BR-05", "BR-12"],
  "timestamp": "2026-04-09T..."
}

As a Business Analyst, I want to map specific Business Requirements (BR) to technical statuses and Test Cases (TC) so that I can track the lifecycle of a requirement from extraction to completion.

---

 1. Database Schema (PostgreSQL)
To support the data you provided, the tables should be structured to handle the many-to-many relationship between Requirements and Test Cases.

```sql
-- Business Goals / Requirements
CREATE TABLE requirements (
    req_code VARCHAR(10) PRIMARY KEY, -- e.g., 'BR-01'
    goal_description TEXT NOT NULL,   -- e.g., 'Increase Analysis Speed'
    requirement_detail TEXT,          -- e.g., 'Automated extraction from PDF'
    dev_status VARCHAR(20),           -- 'Completed', 'In Progress', 'Pending'
    created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Test Cases
CREATE TABLE test_cases (
    tc_code VARCHAR(10) PRIMARY KEY,  -- e.g., 'TC-102'
    description TEXT,
    result VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'Not Run'
);

-- Junction Table for Traceability
CREATE TABLE traceability_map (
    req_code VARCHAR(10) REFERENCES requirements(req_code),
    tc_code VARCHAR(10) REFERENCES test_cases(tc_code),
    PRIMARY KEY (req_code, tc_code)
);
```

---

 2. API Integration (Node.js)
Your Node.js backend will serve this data to the Flutter app. To keep the "Status" updated via GitHub, the logic should look like this:

 Endpoint: `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/matrix`
 Logic: Join `requirements` with `traceability_map` and `test_cases`.
 Automation: When a GitHub Issue linked to `BR-01` is closed, the `dev_status` updates to "Completed" automatically.

---

 3. Flutter Implementation (UI Components)
Since you are a Senior Flutter dev, you can implement this using a `DataTable` or a custom `SliverList` for better mobile responsiveness.

Visual Representation of your Data:

| Code | Business Goal | Requirement | Dev Status | Test Case |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| BR-01 | Increase Analysis Speed | Automated PDF Extraction | Γ£à Completed | `TC-102` |
| BR-02 | Data Integrity | Mobile & Web Sync | ≡ƒöä In Progress | `TC-105` |

Flutter UX Features:
 Status Indicators: Use `CircularProgressIndicator` for "In Progress" and a green `CheckCircle` for "Completed."
 Direct Navigation: Tapping on `TC-102` should navigate the user to the detailed Test Case documentation.



---

 4. Senior Dev Implementation Notes (Ahmad):
 Normalization: While your table shows a 1:1 link, in GrowthPilotAI, one `BR-01` might eventually link to multiple Test Cases. The junction table (`traceability_map`) is critical here to avoid data duplication.
 Performance: For the "PDF Extraction" (BR-01), remember to link the `req_code` back to the specific `start_index` in the original document from Issue 228.
 GitHub Link: For `BR-02`, ensure the branch naming follows your rule: `feat/BR-02-sync-mobile-web`.

`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 232: Issue 233: End-to-End Async Document Processing Workflow & State Orchestration`n`n[Description]`nUser Story:
As a Business Analyst, I want to see the real-time progress of my document analysis (Upload -> Processing -> AI Analysis -> Ready) so that I am informed about the system's background activity and can access my requirements as soon as they are generated.

---

 Technical Implementation Details

1. Server-side (Node.js - The Orchestrator):
 State Machine Implementation: Define a `DocumentStatus` Enum in PostgreSQL:
  `PENDING`, `UPLOADING`, `PRE_PROCESSING`, `AI_ANALYZING`, `COMPLETED`, `FAILED`.
 The Task Queue (BullMQ/Redis):
     Instead of a direct HTTP call to Python, push a job to Redis. 
     This allows the Node.js server to handle thousands of uploads without crashing, while the Python workers pull tasks based on their available CPU/Memory.
 WebSocket Integration (Socket.io):
     Every time the Python worker updates the status in the DB, the Node.js server emits a `status_update` event to the specific `user_id` or `project_room`.



2. Server-side (Python - The Worker):
 Atomic Processing: The Python service should process the task in a "Transactional" manner. If the AI analysis fails (e.g., LLM timeout), it must update the status to `FAILED` and log the error so the user can retry.
 Heartbeat Mechanism: Implement a heartbeat so the Node.js server knows if a Python worker has died mid-process.

3. Client-side (Flutter - The Real-time UI):
 Progress Stepper: Implement a visual "Step Progress" indicator (Upload -> Clean -> Extract -> Save). 
 Background Service: Use `flutter_local_notifications` to alert the user when the analysis is `COMPLETED`, especially if they have navigated away from the upload screen.
 State Management: Use a StreamProvider or Signal that listens to the WebSocket. When the status changes to `COMPLETED`, automatically trigger a fetch for the new `RequirementItem` list.

 Storage: Files are moved from temporary local storage to AWS S3 during the "Upload" phase to ensure the Python worker can access them from a shared URL.

 Impact Analysis & Priority
 Priority: Critical Infrastructure. This is the "Engine Room" of the application.
 Re-ordering:
    1.  233 (Current): Workflow Orchestration (The Pipeline).

 Replacement: This Issue replaces previous fragmented discussions about "Moving files" by formalizing the Async Worker Pattern.



---

 Implementation Notes for Ahmad (Senior Dev):
 The "Idempotency" Rule: Ensure that if a user uploads the same file twice, the system recognizes it (via MD5/SHA-256 hash) and asks if they want to re-process or use the existing results. This saves significant LLM costs.
 Shared File Access: Ensure your Docker volumes are correctly mapped if both services are local, or use S3 Presigned URLs if your Python worker is on a separate instance. This prevents the "I can't find the file" error in the worker logs.

`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 229: Issue 230: Structured Output Parsing for Requirements Architecture`n`n[Description]`nUser Story:
As a Business Analyst, I want the AI to categorize and structure extracted requirements into a standardized format (Type, Priority, Stakeholder) so that I can immediately filter, sort, and manage my project backlog in the dashboard.

---

 Technical Implementation Details

1. Python Service (The AI Parser):
 Pydantic Model Definition: Create a robust schema to force the LLM into a specific JSON structure.
    ```python
    from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
    from typing import List, Optional

    class RequirementItem(BaseModel):
        description: str = Field(description="The core business or technical requirement")
        req_type: str = Field(description="Functional or Non-Functional")
        priority: str = Field(description="Must-Have, Should-Have, Could-Have, or Won't-Have")
        stakeholder: str = Field(description="The primary persona or department affected")
        reasoning: Optional[str] = Field(description="Brief AI logic for this classification")
    ```
 LangChain Integration: Use the `PydanticOutputParser` or `with_structured_output` method. This automatically appends "format instructions" to your prompt, telling the LLM exactly how to wrap the data.
 Validation & Retry Logic: Implement a "Repair Chain." If the LLM returns invalid JSON, the system should catch the error and send the raw output back to the LLM with the error message for a self-correction pass.

2. Server-side (Node.js - API & Database):
 Database Schema Update: Ensure the `requirements` table has typed columns (Enums) for `priority` and `req_type` to maintain data integrity.
 API Response: The Node.js server receives this JSON from the Python worker and broadcasts it via WebSockets to the Flutter app as a "Draft Backlog."

3. Client-side (Flutter - Mobile UX):
 Categorized Views: Instead of a long list, implement Tabbed Views in the mobile app (e.g., "Stakeholders View", "Priority Matrix").
 Visual Labels: Use color-coded chips for priorities:
     Must-Have: Red (High Alert)
     Should-Have: Orange
     Could-Have: Blue/Green
 Manual Override: Since AI isn't perfect, every field (Priority/Stakeholder) must be a dropdown, allowing the analyst to correct the AI's "Proposed" classification.

`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 228: Issue 229: Semantic Requirement Extraction via LangChain & LLM Chains`n`n[Description]`nUser Story:

         Confirm: Move to the formal project backlog.
         Edit: Fine-tune the AIΓÇÖs wording.

 Implementation Notes for Ahmad (Senior Dev):
 LLM Strategy: For extraction, GPT-4o or Gemini 1.5 Pro are superior due to their large context windows and high "Schema Adherence." 
 Zero-Shot vs. Few-Shot: I recommend a Few-Shot approach. Provide the LLM with 3-5 examples of a "bad sentence" vs. a "properly extracted requirement." This significantly reduces "False Positives" (where the AI thinks every sentence is a requirement).
 Traceability: Ensure your `Requirement` table has a `source_document_id` and a `version` column. Business analysts live and die by traceability.
`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 227: Issue 228: Smart Text Sanitization & Boilerplate Removal Engine`n`n[Description]`nUser Story:
As a Business Analyst, I want the system to automatically strip out non-essential text (headers, footers, legal disclaimers) so that the AI focuses only on the core business requirements and process logic.

---

 Technical Implementation Details

1. Server-side (Python Processing Worker):
 Coordinate-Based Filtering (Spatial Cleaning):
     Since you are using PyMuPDF, implement a "Margin-Crop" algorithm. 
     Identify the top and bottom 10% of each page. Any text that appears in these exact coordinates on 3 or more consecutive pages is flagged as a Header or Footer and removed.
 Regex-Based Sanitization:
     Remove non-printable characters and control codes (e.g., `\f`, `\v`, `\b`).
     Normalize white spaces: Convert multiple spaces or tabs into a single space, and multiple newlines into a maximum of two (to preserve paragraph breaks).
 Pattern Matching for Structural Noise:
     Use Regex to identify and strip common document artifacts:
         "Page X of Y"
         Confidentiality notices/Legal footers.
         Table of Contents (which can confuse AI into thinking they are the actual requirements).



2. Server-side (Node.js - Logic & Flow):
 Chunking Strategy:  After cleaning, split the text into manageable "Contextual Chunks" (e.g., 2000-4000 tokens).
     Ensure Sentence-Level Splitting: Never cut a chunk in the middle of a sentence to prevent the AI from losing context.
 Data Storage:
     Store both the `raw_text` and the `sanitized_text` in the database. This allows the user to see exactly what was removed if they suspect a "false positive" deletion.

3. Client-side (Flutter - Mobile UX):
 Comparison View: Implement a "Diff View" where the user can toggle between the original extracted text and the "AI-Ready" version.
 Cleaning Configuration: Give the user a "Advanced Cleaning" toggle to include/exclude specific artifacts like "Tables" or "Images/Captions."



---

 Integration Points
 Downstream (225): The `sanitized_text` is the primary input for the AI Generation Engine. Clean data leads to 40% higher accuracy in Mermaid code generation.
 Link to 226/227: This issue acts as the final stage of the Ingestion Pipeline.

---

 Impact Analysis & Priority
 Priority: High. Without this, the LLM might include "Copyright 2024" as a business requirement or a "Page 4" as a step in a flowchart.
 Re-ordering:
    1.  226/227: Ingestion & OCR (The Source).
    2.  228 (Current): Sanitization (The Filter).
    3.  225: AI Diagram Generation (The Consumer).

---

 Implementation Notes for Ahmad (Senior Dev):
 Python Tip: For coordinate-based cleaning, use `page.get_text("dict")`. This returns the text block along with its `bbox` (bounding box) coordinates. It is much more reliable than trying to find headers using raw string matching.
 Handling Tables: Do not strip tables during sanitization. Instead, ensure they are converted to Markdown Table format. LLMs are surprisingly good at understanding relationships within Markdown tables.

`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 226: Issue 227: OCR Engine for Scanned Documents & Image-to-Text Extraction`n`n[Description]`nUser Story:
As a Business Analyst, I want the system to recognize text within scanned PDFs and images so that I can analyze legacy documents that were not originally created as digital, selectable text files.

 Technical Implementation Details

1. Server-side (Python Processing Worker):
 Image Pre-processing: Use OpenCV to enhance image quality before OCR. This includes:
     Grayscaling & Thresholding: To increase contrast between text and background.
     Deskewing: Correcting the angle of scanned pages to improve character recognition accuracy.
 Local OCR (Tesseract):  Implement `pytesseract` as the primary engine for standard, high-contrast documents to save on cloud costs.
     Configure language packs (English/Persian) to support multi-language business documents.
 Cloud OCR (AWS Textract Integration):
     Trigger AWS Textract for complex layouts, forms, or low-quality scans. 
     Advantage: Textract maintains the "Geometry" of the document, which is vital for identifying tables and forms that Tesseract might scramble.



2. Server-side (Node.js - Logic & Flow):
 Decision Logic: Implement a "Text-Check" step. If the initial parsing (226) returns less than a specific threshold of text (e.g., < 100 characters per page), automatically route the file to the OCR Job Queue.
 Cost Management: Store a flag in the database indicating if a document was processed via AWS to track API consumption and project costs.

3. Client-side (Flutter - Mobile UX):
 Document Scanner: Instead of just picking files, integrate the `google_mlkit_document_scanner` package. This allows the user to use the phone camera to "scan" a physical paper, crop it, and upload it directly as a high-quality image for the OCR engine.
 Visual Feedback: Display an "OCR in Progress" overlay. Since OCR is computationally expensive, use the established WebSocket connection to notify the user when the "Visual-to-Text" conversion is finished.



---

 Integration Points
 Link to 226 (Pre-processing): This issue acts as the "Alternative Path" for the Ingestion Layer. If 226 fails to find text, 227 takes over.
 Link to 225 (AI Generation): The extracted text from the OCR engine is piped directly into the LLM prompt to generate the initial diagram.

---

 Impact Analysis & Priority
 Priority: Medium-High. While essential for legacy data, most modern business documents are "digital-first." However, for a "Senior" product, this is a non-negotiable feature.
 Re-ordering:
    1.  226: Standard Ingestion (Digital PDF/Docx).
    2.  227 (Current): OCR Engine (Scanned/Image Fallback).
    3.  225: AI Diagram Generation.
    4.  224: Intelligent Analytics.



---

 Implementation Notes for Ahmad (Senior Dev):
 Performance Tip: OCR is slow. Always run OCR tasks in a separate background process/container. Do not let Tesseract or AWS SDK calls block your main Node.js event loop.
 The "Hybrid" Strategy: Use Tesseract for a "Quick Scan" to see if the document is readable. If the confidence score (provided by Tesseract) is below 70%, only then make the expensive API call to AWS Textract. This optimizes your AWS bill while maintaining high quality.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 225: Issue 226: Document Ingestion Layer & Pre-processing Pipeline`n`n[Description]`nUser Story:

 Implementation Notes for Ahmad (Senior Dev):
 CustomPainter Tip: To make the `CustomPainter` "stunning" as you mentioned, implement a Layered Architecture. Use one painter for the grid/background, one for the edges (lines), and one for the nodes. This allows you to repaint only the moving parts during panning, drastically improving performance.
 Layout Calculation: Don't calculate the layout on the mobile device. It's computationally expensive for complex graphs. Let the Node.js server handle the `dagre` or `d3-force` calculation and just send a list of `id, x, y` to Flutter.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Implementation Strategy
1. NestJS (The Security Gatekeeper)

Database Schema: We will add a privacy_settings object to the Business Profile model:

TypeScript
{
  business_id: ObjectId,
  visibility: {
    phone: 'public' | 'private',
    address: 'public' | 'private',
    email: 'public' | 'private',
    map_location: 'public' | 'private'
  }
}
Data Masking Middleware: When the GET /profile/:id or GET /search endpoints are called, a utility function checks the requester's relationship with the business. If the field is 'private' and they aren't "Approved," the field is returned as null or "[Hidden]".

API Protection: Crucially, this ensures that private data is never sent in the raw JSON response to unauthorized users.

2. React Web (The Privacy Management Dashboard)
The web interface allows for a detailed overview of the business's "Digital Footprint."

Glassmorphism Toggles: Use high-end switch components next to each profile field in the "Edit Profile" section.

Visual Color Coding: Use subtle color cues (e.g., Green for Public, Slate for Private) so the user knows their status at a glance.

≡ƒ¢á The Data Filtering Workflow
Request: A user searches for a supplier on the Flutter App.

Auth Check: NestJS identifies the requester and the target business.

Privacy Filter: The server sees the supplier's "Phone Number" is set to private and the requester is not in the "Approved List."

Redaction: The server replaces the phone number with a placeholder in the JSON response.

Rendering: The React/Flutter UI displays: "Phone number available to approved partners only."

Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (AC)
[ ] Privacy by Design: All sensitive contact fields must default to private for new accounts.

[ ] Zero Data Leakage: Verified that private fields are not present in the "Network Tab" of the browser for unauthorized users.

[ ] Sync Status: Changes to visibility must reflect across Web and Mobile in real-time.

Simplified List View: A clean UI to see the latest followers with smooth "Hero" animations when clicking on a follower's profile.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 215: Issue #216: Implement User Consent & Legal Compliance Framework`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Description
Establish a robust legal and technical foundation for user onboarding and data usage. This issue covers the implementation of the "Terms of Service" and "Privacy Policy" across the B2B ecosystem (React, Flutter, and NestJS). The goal is to ensure Active Consent, protect Intellectual Property, and enable the Freemium Data Monetization strategy while staying compliant with Canadian laws (PIPEDA & CASL).

≡ƒ¢á Technical Requirements
1. NestJS (The Authority)
Schema Update: Add legalConsent object to the User model:

[╪»╪▒ ╪º█î┘å╪¼╪º ┘à╪│╪¬┘å╪»╪º╪¬ ┌⌐╪º┘à┘ä ╪¿╪▒╪º█î ╪¿╪«╪┤ ┬½┘╛╪░█î.txt](https://github.com/user-attachments/files/26529246/default.txt)

acceptedVersion: string (e.g., "v1.0.0")

consentTimestamp: Date

consentIP: string

dataUsageConsent: boolean (Mandatory for Free Tier)

Audit Logging: Create an immutable ConsentLogs collection to track every legal agreement action.

Validation Guard: Implement a Global Guard to block API access if the user's acceptedVersion is outdated.

2. React Web (Management Dashboard)
Active Consent UI: Implement a mandatory checkbox in the Sign-up flow.

Enforced Modals: Create a non-dismissible Glassmorphism modal for existing users when a new legal version is released.

Preference Center: Build the UI for users to toggle marketing and data sharing (Premium only) as per Issue #158.

3. Flutter Mobile (Field App)
Onboarding Flow: Add a dedicated "Legal Acceptance" step during first-run.

System Sync: Ensure real-time theme and preference sync via WebSockets/FCM once terms are accepted.

In-App WebView: Provide a native-feeling viewer for the full Terms of Service document.

ΓÜû∩╕Å Legal Pillars to Include
Acceptance of Terms: Irrevocable agreement upon installation/registration.

Limitation of Liability: "As-Is" service delivery; no liability for OCR errors or business losses.

Intellectual Property: Protection of Glassmorphism UI, AI algorithms, and branding.

Freemium Data Policy: Mandatory anonymized data harvesting for Free Tier users to train AI models.

Partnership Sharing: Right to share Aggregated/Anonymized data with B2B partners.

Smart Marketing: Permission for personalized behavioral targeting (CASL compliant).

Unilateral Termination: Right to suspend accounts for abuse or breach of terms.

≡ƒôï Implementation Checklist
[ ] Versioning: Implement a system to force re-acceptance on v+1 updates.

[ ] Audit Trail: Log Timestamp, IP, and UserAgent for every consent.

[ ] CASL Compliance: Ensure "Unsubscribe" links are functional and real-time.

[ ] Store Transparency: Update App Store/Google Play "Data Safety" labels to match this policy.

[ ] Premium Toggles: Disable data-sharing toggles for Free users with an "Upgrade" CTA.

█▓. ╪│┘ä╪¿ ┘à╪│╪ª┘ê┘ä█î╪¬ (Limitation of Liability & "As-Is" Clause)

≡ƒô¥ ╪┤╪▒╪¡ ╪»┘é█î┘é (Detailed Description)

╪¼. ╪¬┘ü╪º┘ê╪¬ ┘å╪│╪«┘ç ╪▒╪º█î┌»╪º┘å ┘ê ╪¬╪¼╪º╪▒█î (Free vs. Premium)

Usage Limits: ╪»╪▒ ┘å╪│╪«┘ç ╪▒╪º█î┌»╪º┘å╪î ╪╣┘ä╪º┘ê┘ç ╪¿╪▒ ╪¿┘ç╪▒┘çΓÇî╪¿╪▒╪»╪º╪▒█î ╪º╪▓ ╪»█î╪¬╪º╪î ┘à╪¡╪»┘ê╪»█î╪¬ΓÇî┘ç╪º█î█î ╪»╪▒ ╪¬╪╣╪»╪º╪» ╪ó┘╛┘ä┘ê╪» ┘ü╪º┌⌐╪¬┘ê╪▒ █î╪º ╪¡╪¼┘à ┘ü╪╢╪º█î S3 (Issue #178) ╪º╪╣┘à╪º┘ä ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê╪».

≡ƒ¢á ┌»╪▒╪»╪┤ ┌⌐╪º╪▒ ╪º╪│╪¬╪«╪▒╪º╪¼ ╪»█î╪¬╪º (Data Harvesting Workflow)
Capture: ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ┘å╪│╪«┘ç ╪▒╪º█î┌»╪º┘å╪î ┘ü╪º┌⌐╪¬┘ê╪▒ ╪«╪▒█î╪» ╪«┘ê╪» ╪▒╪º ╪»╪▒ ╪º┘╛┘ä█î┌⌐█î╪┤┘å Flutter ╪ó┘╛┘ä┘ê╪» ┘à█îΓÇî┌⌐┘å╪».

Filter: ╪│╪▒┘ê█î╪│ NestJS ┘å┘ê╪╣ ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ╪▒╪º ┌å┌⌐ ┘à█îΓÇî┌⌐┘å╪» (Issue #174).

Sanitize: ╪º┌»╪▒ ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ╪▒╪º█î┌»╪º┘å ╪¿╪º╪┤╪»╪î █î┌⌐ ┌⌐┘╛█î ╪º╪▓ ╪º╪╖┘ä╪º╪╣╪º╪¬ ┌⌐╪º┘ä╪º┘ç╪º (╪¿╪»┘ê┘å ┘å╪º┘à ╪«╪▒█î╪»╪º╪▒/┘ü╪▒┘ê╪┤┘å╪»┘ç) ╪¿┘ç ╪»█î╪¬╪º╪¿█î╪│ ╪¬╪¡┘ä█î┘ä█î ┘à┘å╪¬┘é┘ä ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê╪».

Analyze: ╪»╪º╪»┘çΓÇî┘ç╪º ╪»╪▒ ╪»╪º╪┤╪¿┘ê╪▒╪» ┘à╪▒┌⌐╪▓█î ╪¬█î┘à ╪¬┘ê╪│╪╣┘ç ╪¼┘ç╪¬ ╪º╪│╪¬╪«╪▒╪º╪¼ ╪¬╪▒┘å╪»┘ç╪º█î ╪¿╪º╪▓╪º╪▒ Lower Mainland ╪»╪│╪¬┘çΓÇî╪¿┘å╪»█î ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê┘å╪».

Value Creation: ╪º╪▓ ╪º█î┘å ╪¬╪¡┘ä█î┘äΓÇî┘ç╪º ╪¿╪▒╪º█î ╪º╪▒╪º╪ª┘ç ┌»╪▓╪º╪▒╪┤ΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪¿╪º╪▓╪º╪▒ ╪¿┘ç ╪¬╪º┘à█î┘åΓÇî┌⌐┘å┘å╪»┌»╪º┘å ╪¿╪▓╪▒┌» ╪º╪│╪¬┘ü╪º╪»┘ç ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê╪».

Γ£à ┘à╪╣█î╪º╪▒┘ç╪º█î ┘╛╪░█î╪▒╪┤ (Acceptance Criteria)
[ ] Privacy Compliance: ┘ü╪▒╪ó█î┘å╪» ╪º╪│╪¬╪«╪▒╪º╪¼ ╪»█î╪¬╪º ╪¿╪º█î╪» ╪¿╪º ┘é┘ê╪º┘å█î┘å PIPEDA (┘é╪º┘å┘ê┘å ╪¡┘ü╪º╪╕╪¬ ╪º╪▓ ╪º╪╖┘ä╪º╪╣╪º╪¬ ╪º┘ä┌⌐╪¬╪▒┘ê┘å█î┌⌐ ┌⌐╪º┘å╪º╪»╪º) ┘à┘å╪╖╪¿┘é ╪¿╪º╪┤╪»╪¢ █î╪╣┘å█î ╪»█î╪¬╪º█î ╪«╪▒┘ê╪¼█î ┘å╪¿╪º█î╪» ╪¿┘ç ┘ç█î┌å ┘ê╪¼┘ç ┘é╪º╪¿┘ä ╪º┘å╪¬╪│╪º╪¿ ╪¿┘ç █î┌⌐ ┘ü╪▒╪» ╪«╪º╪╡ ╪¿╪º╪┤╪».

[ ] Explicit Consent: ╪»╪▒ ┘ç┘å┌»╪º┘à ╪½╪¿╪¬ΓÇî┘å╪º┘à╪î ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ╪¿╪º█î╪» ╪╡╪▒╪º╪¡╪¬╪º┘ï ╪¬█î┌⌐ ┬½┘à┘ê╪º┘ü┘é╪¬ ╪¿╪º ╪│█î╪º╪│╪¬ ╪¿┘ç╪▒┘çΓÇî╪¿╪▒╪»╪º╪▒█î ╪º╪▓ ╪»╪º╪»┘ç ╪»╪▒ ┘å╪│╪«┘ç ╪▒╪º█î┌»╪º┘å┬╗ ╪▒╪º ╪¿╪▓┘å╪».

[ ] Transparency: ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ╪¿╪º█î╪» ╪¿╪¬┘ê╪º┘å╪» ╪»╪▒ ╪¿╪«╪┤ Preferences (Issue #158) ╪¿╪¿█î┘å╪» ┌⌐┘ç ╪»█î╪¬╪º█î ╪º┘ê ╪»╪▒ ╪¡╪º┘ä ┌⌐┘à┌⌐ ╪¿┘ç ╪¿┘ç╪¿┘ê╪» ╪│█î╪│╪¬┘à ╪º╪│╪¬.

[ ] Security: ╪»█î╪¬╪º╪¿█î╪│ ╪¬╪¡┘ä█î┘ä█î (Analytical DB) ╪¿╪º█î╪» ┌⌐╪º┘à┘ä╪º┘ï ╪º╪▓ ╪»█î╪¬╪º╪¿█î╪│ ╪º╪╡┘ä█î (Production DB) ╪º█î╪▓┘ê┘ä┘ç ╪¿╪º╪┤╪» ╪¬╪º ╪»╪▒ ╪╡┘ê╪▒╪¬ ┘å┘ü┘ê╪░ ╪º╪¡╪¬┘à╪º┘ä█î╪î ╪º┘à┘å█î╪¬ ╪¡╪│╪º╪¿ΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒█î ╪¿┘ç ╪«╪╖╪▒ ┘å█î┘ü╪¬╪».

█╡. ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ΓÇî┌»╪░╪º╪▒█î ╪¿╪º ╪┤╪▒┌⌐╪º█î ╪¬╪¼╪º╪▒█î (Data Sharing & B2B Ecosystem Growth)
≡ƒô¥ ╪┤╪▒╪¡ ╪º╪│╪¬╪▒╪º╪¬┌ÿ█î┌⌐ (Strategic Description)
╪¬█î┘à ╪¬┘ê╪│╪╣┘çΓÇî╪»┘ç┘å╪»┘ç ┘à╪¼╪º╪▓ ╪º╪│╪¬ ╪»╪º╪»┘çΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪¬╪¼╪º╪▒█î ╪¬╪¼┘à█î╪╣ΓÇî╪┤╪»┘ç (Aggregated) ┘ê ┘å╪º╪┤┘å╪º╪│ (Anonymized) ╪▒╪º ╪¿╪º ╪┤╪▒┌⌐╪º█î ╪º╪│╪¬╪▒╪º╪¬┌ÿ█î┌⌐ (╪¬╪ú┘à█î┘åΓÇî┌⌐┘å┘å╪»┌»╪º┘å ┌⌐╪º┘ä╪º╪î ╪¬╪¡┘ä█î┘äΓÇî┌»╪▒╪º┘å ╪¿╪º╪▓╪º╪▒ ┘ê ┘å┘ç╪º╪»┘ç╪º█î ┘à╪º┘ä█î) ╪¿┘ç ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ ╪¿┌»╪░╪º╪▒╪». ┘ç╪»┘ü ╪º╪▓ ╪º█î┘å ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪î ╪¡╪░┘ü ┘ê╪º╪│╪╖┘çΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪▓╪º╪ª╪»╪î ┘╛█î╪┤ΓÇî╪¿█î┘å█î ╪»┘é█î┘é ╪¬┘é╪º╪╢╪º ╪»╪▒ ┘à┘å╪╖┘é┘ç Lower Mainland ┘ê ╪º╪▒╪º╪ª┘ç ┘╛█î╪┤┘å┘ç╪º╪»╪º╪¬ ╪«╪▒█î╪» ┘ç┘ê╪┤┘à┘å╪»╪¬╪▒ ╪¿┘ç ╪«┘ê╪»┘É ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒╪º┘å ╪º╪│╪¬. ╪»╪▒ ╪º█î┘å ┘ü╪▒╪ó█î┘å╪»╪î ┘ç█î┌åΓÇî┌»┘ê┘å┘ç ╪»╪º╪»┘çΓÇî╪º█î ┌⌐┘ç ┘à┘å╪¼╪▒ ╪¿┘ç ╪┤┘å╪º╪│╪º█î█î ┘à╪│╪¬┘é█î┘à █î┌⌐ ╪¿█î╪▓┘å╪│ █î╪º ╪┤╪«╪╡ ╪«╪º╪╡ ╪┤┘ê╪» (PII)╪î ╪»╪▒ ╪º╪«╪¬█î╪º╪▒ ╪┤╪«╪╡ ╪½╪º┘ä╪½ ┘é╪▒╪º╪▒ ┘å┘à█îΓÇî┌»█î╪▒╪».

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å ┘à╪╣┘à╪º╪▒█î ┘ü┘å█î ┘ê ╪¡┘ü╪º╪╕╪¬█î (Technical Architecture)
█▒. ┘à┘ê╪¬┘ê╪▒ ╪¬╪¼┘à█î╪╣ ╪»╪º╪»┘ç (Aggregation Engine - NestJS)
╪│█î╪│╪¬┘à ╪¿┘ç ╪¼╪º█î ╪º╪▒╪│╪º┘ä ╪▒╪»█î┘üΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪«╪º┘à ╪»█î╪¬╪º╪¿█î╪│╪î ╪»╪º╪»┘çΓÇî┘ç╪º ╪▒╪º ╪»╪▒ ╪»╪│╪¬┘çΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪¿╪▓╪▒┌» (Buckets) ┌»╪▒┘ê┘çΓÇî╪¿┘å╪»█î ┘à█îΓÇî┌⌐┘å╪»:

╪¡╪░┘ü ┘ç┘ê█î╪¬ (De-identification): ┘å╪º┘à ┘ü╪▒┘ê╪┤┌»╪º┘ç╪î ╪┤┘à╪º╪▒┘ç ┘ü╪º┌⌐╪¬┘ê╪▒ ┘ê ┌⌐╪» ┘╛╪│╪¬█î ╪»┘é█î┘é ╪¡╪░┘ü ╪┤╪»┘ç ┘ê ╪¿┘ç ╪¼╪º█î ╪ó┘å ┘ü┘é╪╖ ┘å╪º┘à ╪┤┘ç╪▒ █î╪º ┘à┘å╪╖┘é┘ç (┘à╪½┘ä╪º┘ï Coquitlam) ╪¿╪º┘é█î ┘à█îΓÇî┘à╪º┘å╪».

┘à╪¡╪º╪│╪¿╪º╪¬ ╪ó┘à╪º╪▒█î: ╪¿┘ç ╪¼╪º█î ╪º╪▒╪│╪º┘ä ┘é█î┘à╪¬ ╪«╪▒█î╪» █î┌⌐ ┌⌐╪º╪¿┘ä ╪¬┘ê╪│╪╖ ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ A╪î ┬½┘à█î╪º┘å┌»█î┘å ┘é█î┘à╪¬ ┌⌐╪º╪¿┘ä ╪»╪▒ ┘à┘å╪╖┘é┘ç┬╗ ╪º╪▒╪│╪º┘ä ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê╪».

█▓. ╪º┘à┘å█î╪¬ ╪»╪▒ ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ΓÇî┌»╪░╪º╪▒█î (Secure Data Sharing)
Zero-Knowledge Principle: ╪┤╪▒┌⌐╪º█î ╪¬╪¼╪º╪▒█î ┘ç╪▒┌»╪▓ ╪¿┘ç ╪»█î╪¬╪º╪¿█î╪│ ╪º╪╡┘ä█î MongoDB ╪»╪│╪¬╪▒╪│█î ┘å╪»╪º╪▒┘å╪». ╪»╪º╪»┘çΓÇî┘ç╪º ╪º╪▓ ╪╖╪▒█î┘é █î┌⌐ Data Warehouse ┘à╪¼╪▓╪º ┘ê ╪º╪▓ ╪╖╪▒█î┘é API┘ç╪º█î ┘à╪¡╪»┘ê╪» ╪┤╪»┘ç ╪¿┘ç ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ ┌»╪░╪º╪┤╪¬┘ç ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê┘å╪».

Data Expiry: ╪»╪º╪»┘çΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪¿┘ç ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ ┌»╪░╪º╪┤╪¬┘ç ╪┤╪»┘ç ╪»╪º╪▒╪º█î ╪¬╪º╪▒█î╪« ╪º┘å┘é╪╢╪º ┘ç╪│╪¬┘å╪» ╪¬╪º ╪º╪▓ ╪│┘ê╪í╪º╪│╪¬┘ü╪º╪»┘çΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪╖┘ê┘ä╪º┘å█îΓÇî┘à╪»╪¬ ╪¼┘ä┘ê┌»█î╪▒█î ╪┤┘ê╪».

█│. ┌⌐┘å╪¬╪▒┘ä ╪»╪▒ ┘å╪│╪«┘ç Premium (Issue #158)
╪¿╪▒╪º█î ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒╪º┘å ╪»╪º╪▒╪º█î ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ ┘ê█î┌ÿ┘ç╪î █î┌⌐ ╪│┘ê█î█î┌å ╪º╪«╪¬╪╡╪º╪╡█î ╪¿╪º ╪╣┘å┘ê╪º┘å "Private Mode" ╪»╪▒ ╪º┘╛┘ä█î┌⌐█î╪┤┘å Flutter ┘ê ┘ê╪¿ React ┘ü╪╣╪º┘ä ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê╪». ╪¿╪º ┘ü╪╣╪º┘ä ┌⌐╪▒╪»┘å ╪º█î┘å ┌»╪▓█î┘å┘ç╪î ╪»╪º╪»┘çΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪ó┘å ╪¿█î╪▓┘å╪│ ╪¡╪¬█î ╪¿┘ç ╪╡┘ê╪▒╪¬ ┘å╪º╪┤┘å╪º╪│ ┘ç┘à ╪º╪▓ ┌å╪▒╪«┘ç ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ΓÇî┌»╪░╪º╪▒█î ╪¡╪░┘ü ┘à█îΓÇî┌»╪▒╪»╪».

≡ƒ¢á ┌»╪▒╪»╪┤ ┌⌐╪º╪▒ ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ΓÇî┌»╪░╪º╪▒█î (The Collaborative Workflow)
Inflow: ┘ç╪▓╪º╪▒╪º┘å ┘ü╪º┌⌐╪¬┘ê╪▒ ╪«╪▒█î╪» ╪¬┘ê╪│╪╖ ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒╪º┘å ╪▒╪º█î┌»╪º┘å ╪»╪▒ ┌⌐┘ä ┘à┘å╪╖┘é┘ç ╪ó┘╛┘ä┘ê╪» ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê╪».

Processing: ┘à┘ê╪¬┘ê╪▒ ┘ç┘ê╪┤ ┘à╪╡┘å┘ê╪╣█î (Issue #125) ╪º┘ä┌»┘ê┘ç╪º█î ╪«╪▒█î╪» ╪▒╪º ╪┤┘å╪º╪│╪º█î█î ┘à█îΓÇî┌⌐┘å╪» (┘à╪½┘ä╪º┘ï ╪º┘ü╪▓╪º█î╪┤ █▓█░ ╪»╪▒╪╡╪»█î ╪¬┘é╪º╪╢╪º█î ┘à╪╡╪º┘ä╪¡ ╪│╪º╪«╪¬┘à╪º┘å█î ╪»╪▒ North Vancouver).

Anonymization: ╪│█î╪│╪¬┘à ╪¬┘à╪º┘à ┘à╪¬╪º╪»█î╪¬╪º█î ╪┤╪«╪╡█î ╪▒╪º ┘╛╪º┌⌐ ┌⌐╪▒╪»┘ç ┘ê ┘ü┘é╪╖ ┬½╪¬╪▒┘å╪» ╪¿╪º╪▓╪º╪▒┬╗ ╪▒╪º ╪º╪│╪¬╪«╪▒╪º╪¼ ┘à█îΓÇî┌⌐┘å╪».

Value Exchange: ╪º█î┘å ┌»╪▓╪º╪▒╪┤ΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪¬╪¡┘ä█î┘ä█î ╪»╪▒ ╪º╪«╪¬█î╪º╪▒ ╪¬╪ú┘à█î┘åΓÇî┌⌐┘å┘å╪»┌»╪º┘å ╪╣┘à╪»┘ç ┘é╪▒╪º╪▒ ┘à█îΓÇî┌»█î╪▒╪» ╪¬╪º ╪ó┘åΓÇî┘ç╪º ╪¿╪¬┘ê╪º┘å┘å╪» ┘à┘ê╪¼┘ê╪»█î ╪«┘ê╪» ╪▒╪º ╪¿╪▒ ╪º╪│╪º╪│ ┘å█î╪º╪▓ ╪¿╪º╪▓╪º╪▒ ╪¬┘å╪╕█î┘à ┌⌐╪▒╪»┘ç ┘ê ╪»╪▒ ┘å┘ç╪º█î╪¬ ┘é█î┘à╪¬ΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪▒┘é╪º╪¿╪¬█îΓÇî╪¬╪▒█î ╪¿┘ç ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒╪º┘å ┘╛┘ä╪¬┘ü╪▒┘à ╪º╪▒╪º╪ª┘ç ╪»┘ç┘å╪».

Γ£à ┘à╪╣█î╪º╪▒┘ç╪º█î ┘╛╪░█î╪▒╪┤ ┘ê ╪º┘å╪╖╪¿╪º┘é (Acceptance Criteria)
[ ] Anti-Reidentification: ╪¬╪╢┘à█î┘å ╪┤┘ê╪» ┌⌐┘ç ╪¿╪º ╪¬╪▒┌⌐█î╪¿ ╪»╪º╪»┘çΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪¿┘ç ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ ┌»╪░╪º╪┤╪¬┘ç ╪┤╪»┘ç╪î ╪º┘à┌⌐╪º┘å ╪┤┘å╪º╪│╪º█î█î ┘à╪¼╪»╪» (Re-identification) █î┌⌐ ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ╪«╪º╪╡ ┘ê╪¼┘ê╪» ┘å╪»╪º╪▒╪».

╪º█î┘å ╪¿╪«╪┤ ╪¿┘ç ╪¬█î┘à ╪¬┘ê╪│╪╣┘ç ╪º╪¼╪º╪▓┘ç ┘à█îΓÇî╪»┘ç╪» ╪¬╪º ╪º╪▓ ╪│┘ä╪º┘à╪¬ ╪º┌⌐┘ê╪│█î╪│╪¬┘à ╪»╪▒ ╪¿╪▒╪º╪¿╪▒ ╪▒┘ü╪¬╪º╪▒┘ç╪º█î ┘à╪«╪▒╪¿╪î ┌⌐┘ä╪º┘ç╪¿╪▒╪»╪º╪▒█î █î╪º ┘å┘é╪╢ ╪╡╪▒█î╪¡ ┘é╪▒╪º╪▒╪»╪º╪» ┘à╪¡╪º┘ü╪╕╪¬ ┌⌐┘å╪». ╪º█î┘å █î┌⌐ ╪º╪¿╪▓╪º╪▒ ┌⌐┘å╪¬╪▒┘ä█î ╪¿╪▒╪º█î ╪¡┘ü╪╕ ┌⌐█î┘ü█î╪¬ ╪│╪▒┘ê█î╪│ ╪¿╪▒╪º█î ╪│╪º█î╪▒ ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒╪º┘å ┘é╪º┘å┘ê┘åΓÇî┘à╪»╪º╪▒ ╪º╪│╪¬.

╪¬█î┘à ╪¬┘ê╪│╪╣┘çΓÇî╪»┘ç┘å╪»┘ç ╪¡┘é ┘à╪╖┘ä┘é ┘ê ╪º┘å╪¡╪╡╪º╪▒█î ╪»╪º╪▒╪» ┌⌐┘ç ╪»╪▒ ╪╡┘ê╪▒╪¬ ┘à╪┤╪º┘ç╪»┘ç ┘ç╪▒┌»┘ê┘å┘ç ╪│┘ê╪í╪º╪│╪¬┘ü╪º╪»┘ç╪î ┘ü╪╣╪º┘ä█î╪¬ ┘à╪┤┌⌐┘ê┌⌐╪î ┘å┘é╪╢ ┘à┘ü╪º╪» ╪º█î┘å ┘é╪▒╪º╪▒╪»╪º╪»╪î █î╪º ╪▒┘ü╪¬╪º╪▒█î ┌⌐┘ç ╪º┘à┘å█î╪¬ ┘╛┘ä╪¬┘ü╪▒┘à ┘ê ╪¡┘é┘ê┘é ╪│╪º█î╪▒ ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒╪º┘å ╪▒╪º ╪¿┘ç ╪«╪╖╪▒ ╪º┘å╪»╪º╪▓╪»╪î ╪»╪│╪¬╪▒╪│█î ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ (╪»╪▒ ┘ç╪▒ ╪»┘ê ┘╛┘ä╪¬┘ü╪▒┘à ┘ê╪¿ ┘ê ┘à┘ê╪¿╪º█î┘ä) ╪▒╪º ╪¿┘çΓÇî╪╡┘ê╪▒╪¬ ┘à┘ê┘é╪¬ █î╪º ╪»╪º╪ª┘à╪î ╪¿╪»┘ê┘å ╪º╪╖┘ä╪º╪╣ ┘é╪¿┘ä█î ┘ê ╪¿╪»┘ê┘å ┘å█î╪º╪▓ ╪¿┘ç ╪╖█î ┘à╪▒╪º╪¡┘ä ┘é╪╢╪º█î█î╪î ┘à╪│╪»┘ê╪» █î╪º ┘ü╪│╪« ┘å┘à╪º█î╪».

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å ╪º┘ä╪▓╪º┘à╪º╪¬ ┘ü┘å█î ┘ê ╪│█î╪│╪¬┘à ┘à╪│╪»┘ê╪»╪│╪º╪▓█î (Technical Implementation)

Security Guard: ┘ç╪▒┌»┘ê┘å┘ç ╪¬┘ä╪º╪┤ ╪¿╪▒╪º█î ╪¬╪▓╪▒█î┘é ┌⌐╪» (SQL Injection) █î╪º ╪»┘ê╪▒ ╪▓╪»┘å ┘ä╪º█î┘ç ╪º╪¡╪▒╪º╪▓ ┘ç┘ê█î╪¬ (Issue #174)╪î ╪¿╪º╪╣╪½ ┘ü╪╣╪º┘ä ╪┤╪»┘å ┘╛╪▒┘ê╪¬┌⌐┘ä ┘à╪│╪»┘ê╪»╪│╪º╪▓█î ╪ó┘å█î ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê╪».

╪¿. ┘ê╪╢╪╣█î╪¬ΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪¡╪│╪º╪¿ ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒█î (User Status Management)

≡ƒ¢á ┌»╪▒╪»╪┤ ┌⌐╪º╪▒ ┘à╪│╪»┘ê╪»╪│╪º╪▓█î (Termination Workflow)

┌å┌⌐ΓÇî┘ä█î╪│╪¬ ┘╛█î╪º╪»┘çΓÇî╪│╪º╪▓█î ╪¡┘é┘ê┘é█î: ╪¬╪º█î█î╪»█î┘ç ┘ü╪╣╪º┘ä (Active Consent)

[ ] Grace Period (Optional): ┘à█îΓÇî╪¬┘ê╪º┘å█î █î┌⌐ "┘à┘ç┘ä╪¬ █╖ ╪▒┘ê╪▓┘ç" ╪¬╪╣█î█î┘å ┌⌐┘å█î ┌⌐┘ç ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ╪¿╪º ┘å╪│╪«┘ç ┘é╪»█î┘à█î ┌⌐╪º╪▒ ┌⌐┘å╪» ┘ê ╪¿╪╣╪» ╪º╪▓ ╪ó┘å ╪»╪│╪¬╪▒╪│█î ┘é╪╖╪╣ ╪┤┘ê╪» (╪¿╪│╪¬┘ç ╪¿┘ç ┘à█î╪▓╪º┘å ╪¡╪│╪º╪│█î╪¬ ╪¬╪║█î█î╪▒╪º╪¬).

Γ£à ┘à╪╣█î╪º╪▒┘ç╪º█î ┘╛╪░█î╪▒╪┤ (Acceptance Criteria)

[ ] Auto-Update on Upgrade: ╪¿┘ç ┘à╪¡╪╢ ╪º█î┘å┌⌐┘ç ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ ╪«┘ê╪» ╪▒╪º ╪¿┘ç Premium ╪º╪▒╪¬┘é╪º ╪»╪º╪»╪î ╪¿╪º█î╪» ╪¿┘ç ╪º┘ê ╪º╪¼╪º╪▓┘ç ╪»╪º╪»┘ç ╪┤┘ê╪» ╪¬╪º ╪»╪▒ ╪¿╪«╪┤ ╪¬┘å╪╕█î┘à╪º╪¬╪î ┌»╪▓█î┘å┘ç ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ΓÇî┌»╪░╪º╪▒█î ╪»╪º╪»┘ç ╪▒╪º ╪«╪º┘à┘ê╪┤ ┌⌐┘å╪».

[ ] Transparency: ╪»╪▒ ┘å╪│╪«┘ç ╪▒╪º█î┌»╪º┘å╪î █î┌⌐ ╪¿╪«╪┤ "Transparency Report" ╪│╪º╪»┘ç ┘ê╪¼┘ê╪» ╪»╪º╪┤╪¬┘ç ╪¿╪º╪┤╪» ┌⌐┘ç ┘å╪┤╪º┘å ╪»┘ç╪» ╪»█î╪¬╪º█î ╪¬╪¼┘à█î╪╣ ╪┤╪»┘ç ┌å╪╖┘ê╪▒ ╪¿┘ç ╪¿┘ç╪¿┘ê╪» ┘ç┘ê╪┤ ┘à╪╡┘å┘ê╪╣█î ╪│█î╪│╪¬┘à ┌⌐┘à┌⌐ ┌⌐╪▒╪»┘ç ╪º╪│╪¬.

[ ] No PII Leak: ╪º╪╖┘à█î┘å╪º┘å ╪¡╪º╪╡┘ä ╪┤┘ê╪» ┌⌐┘ç ╪¡╪¬█î ╪»╪▒ ╪╡┘ê╪▒╪¬ ╪▒╪╢╪º█î╪¬ ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ╪▒╪º█î┌»╪º┘å╪î ┘ç█î┌å ╪º╪╖┘ä╪º╪╣╪º╪¬ ┘ç┘ê█î╪¬ ┘ü╪▒╪»█î (PII) ╪¿┘ç ╪¿╪«╪┤ ╪¬╪¡┘ä█î┘ä█î ┘à┘å╪¬┘é┘ä ┘å┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê╪».


┌å┌⌐ΓÇî┘ä█î╪│╪¬ ┘╛█î╪º╪»┘çΓÇî╪│╪º╪▓█î ╪¡┘é┘ê┘é█î: ╪»╪│╪¬╪▒╪│█î ╪»╪º╪ª┘à█î (Permanent Access)

█▒. ┘╛█î╪º╪»┘çΓÇî╪│╪º╪▓█î ╪»╪▒ React (The Global Footer)
╪»╪▒ ┘ê╪¿ΓÇî╪│╪º█î╪¬╪î ┘ü┘ê╪¬╪▒ (Footer) ╪¿┘ç╪¬╪▒█î┘å ┘à┌⌐╪º┘å ╪¿╪▒╪º█î ┘é╪▒╪º╪▒┌»█î╪▒█î ┘ä█î┘å┌⌐ΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪¡┘é┘ê┘é█î ╪º╪│╪¬ ┌å┘ê┘å ╪»╪▒ ╪¬┘à╪º┘à ╪╡┘ü╪¡╪º╪¬ (╪»╪º╪┤╪¿┘ê╪▒╪»╪î ┘╛╪▒┘ê┘ü╪º█î┘ä╪î ╪¬┘å╪╕█î┘à╪º╪¬) ╪¬┌⌐╪▒╪º╪▒ ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê╪».

Γ£à ┘à╪╣█î╪º╪▒┘ç╪º█î ┘╛╪░█î╪▒╪┤ (Acceptance Criteria)
[ ] Accessibility: ┘ä█î┘å┌⌐ΓÇî┘ç╪º ╪¿╪º█î╪» ╪»╪▒ ┘ç╪▒ ╪»┘ê ┘╛┘ä╪¬┘ü╪▒┘à ╪¿╪º ╪¡╪»╪º┌⌐╪½╪▒ █▓ ┌⌐┘ä█î┌⌐ ╪º╪▓ ╪╡┘ü╪¡┘ç ╪º╪╡┘ä█î ╪»╪▒ ╪»╪│╪¬╪▒╪│ ╪¿╪º╪┤┘å╪».

[ ] Offline Access (Mobile): ╪º┘╛┘ä█î┌⌐█î╪┤┘å Flutter ╪¿╪º█î╪» ╪ó╪«╪▒█î┘å ┘å╪│╪«┘ç ┘à╪¬┘å ┘é┘ê╪º┘å█î┘å ╪▒╪º ┌⌐╪┤ (Cache) ┌⌐┘å╪» ╪¬╪º ╪»╪▒ ╪╡┘ê╪▒╪¬ ┘å╪¿┘ê╪» ╪º█î┘å╪¬╪▒┘å╪¬ ┘ç┘à ┘é╪º╪¿┘ä ┘à╪┤╪º┘ç╪»┘ç ╪¿╪º╪┤╪».

[ ] Print Friendly (Web): ╪»╪▒ ┘å╪│╪«┘ç ┘ê╪¿╪î ╪º╪│╪¬╪º█î┘ä CSS ╪¿┘ç ┌»┘ê┘å┘çΓÇî╪º█î ╪¿╪º╪┤╪» ┌⌐┘ç ╪º┌»╪▒ ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ╪«┘ê╪º╪│╪¬ ╪╡┘ü╪¡┘ç ┘é┘ê╪º┘å█î┘å ╪▒╪º ┘╛╪▒█î┘å╪¬ ╪¿┌»█î╪▒╪»╪î ╪«╪▒┘ê╪¼█î ┘à╪▒╪¬╪¿ ┘ê ╪«┘ê╪º┘å╪º█î█î ╪»╪º╪┤╪¬┘ç ╪¿╪º╪┤╪».

[ ] Search Engine Indexing: ╪»╪▒ ┘å╪│╪«┘ç React╪î ╪º╪╖┘à█î┘å╪º┘å ╪¡╪º╪╡┘ä ┌⌐┘å ┌⌐┘ç ╪╡┘ü╪¡╪º╪¬ ╪¡┘é┘ê┘é█î ╪¬┘ê╪│╪╖ ┘à┘ê╪¬┘ê╪▒┘ç╪º█î ╪¼╪│╪¬╪¼┘ê (SEO) ╪º█î┘å╪»┌⌐╪│ ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê┘å╪» ╪¬╪º ╪º╪╣╪¬╪¿╪º╪▒ ╪│╪º█î╪¬╪¬ ╪¿╪º┘ä╪º ╪¿╪▒┘ê╪».



The goal is to create a non-repudiable record. This means storing enough metadata so that the "Who, When, and What" of the consent can be reconstructed years later.

1. NestJS: The Audit Service Logic
Don't just update the User document; create a dedicated ConsentLogs collection. This prevents data loss if a user profile is modified or deleted.

Data Capture: When the POST /v1/auth/register or PATCH /v1/legal/accept endpoint is hit:

Timestamp: Use new Date() (UTC) to ensure global consistency.

IP Address: Extract the IP from the request headers. If you are using a proxy (like Nginx), ensure you grab the x-forwarded-for header.

User Agent: Store the browser or device info (e.g., "Chrome 123 on macOS" or "Flutter App on iOS 17") to prove which platform was used.

Version Hash: Store the exact version string (e.g., v1.2.0) and, ideally, a hash of the legal text to prove the content hasn't changed.

2. MongoDB Schema Design
Structure your ConsentLog to be an "Append-Only" record.

TypeScript
// Schema Example
{
  userId: ObjectId("..."),
  action: "TERMS_ACCEPTED",
  version: "v1.2.0",
  timestamp: ISODate("2026-04-06T23:30:00Z"),
  metadata: {
    ipAddress: "192.168.1.1",
    platform: "Web/React",
    userAgent: "Mozilla/5.0...",
    location: "Coquitlam, BC" // Optional: Geo-IP lookup
  }
}
3. Frontend Implementation (React & Flutter)
The frontend's job is to ensure the Consent Payload is sent correctly during the "Active Consent" phase.

React: Ensure the registration payload includes a consentMetadata object. You can use a library like public-ip if you want the client-side IP, but the server-side IP is generally more legally reliable.

Flutter: Capture the device model (e.g., "iPhone 15 Pro") using the device_info_plus package and include it in the audit payload to add extra weight to the evidence.

≡ƒ¢á The "Evidence Chain" Workflow
Trigger: A vendor in Surrey clicks "Accept & Continue" on the v2.1 Legal Update Modal.

Intercept: The NestJS middleware captures the incoming IP (24.80.XXX.XXX) and the current server time.

Validate: The system confirms that v2.1 is indeed the latest version.

Write: Two operations happen:

The User profile is updated with the new version number.

A new entry is pushed to the ConsentLogs collection.

Seal: The log is saved. In a high-security setup, you could even "Salt and Hash" this log entry to ensure it is never tampered with by database admins.

Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (AC)
[ ] Immutability: Audit logs must be "Create-Only." No UPDATE or DELETE operations should be allowed on the ConsentLogs collection.

[ ] Completeness: Every log entry must contain the User ID, Timestamp, IP, and Version.

[ ] Searchability: Admins must be able to search logs by userId to quickly pull a "Certificate of Consent" if needed.

[ ] Compliance: The log storage must follow PIPEDA guidelines regarding data retention periods (usually 7 years for legal documents).



┌å┌⌐ΓÇî┘ä█î╪│╪¬ ┘╛█î╪º╪»┘çΓÇî╪│╪º╪▓█î ╪¡┘é┘ê┘é█î: ╪º┘å╪╖╪¿╪º┘é ╪¿╪º CASL (Anti-Spam Compliance)
┘ç╪»┘ü ╪º█î┘å ╪º╪│╪¬ ┌⌐┘ç ╪¿┘ç ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ╪º╪¼╪º╪▓┘ç ╪»┘ç█î┘à ╪¿┘ç ╪▒╪º╪¡╪¬█î ┘ê ╪»╪▒ ┌⌐┘à╪¬╪▒█î┘å ╪▓┘à╪º┘å ┘à┘à┌⌐┘å (╪¡╪»╪º┌⌐╪½╪▒ █▒█░ ╪▒┘ê╪▓ ┌⌐╪º╪▒█î ╪╖╪¿┘é ┘é╪º┘å┘ê┘å╪î ╪º┘à╪º ┘à╪º ╪ó┘å ╪▒╪º Real-time ┘à█îΓÇî┌⌐┘å█î┘à) ╪º╪▓ ┌å╪▒╪«┘ç ┘╛█î╪º┘àΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ┘à╪º╪▒┌⌐╪¬█î┘å┌» ╪«╪º╪▒╪¼ ╪┤┘ê╪».

█▒. ╪│╪º╪«╪¬╪º╪▒ ╪º█î┘à█î┘ä ╪»╪▒ NestJS (Email Engine)

The Unsubscribe Link: ╪º█î┘å ┘ä█î┘å┌⌐ ╪¿╪º█î╪» "╪¿╪▒╪¼╪│╪¬┘ç" (Conspicuous) ┘ê "╪¿┘ç ╪▒╪º╪¡╪¬█î ┘é╪º╪¿┘ä ╪º╪¼╪▒╪º" ╪¿╪º╪┤╪».

█▓. ┘à┌⌐╪º┘å█î╪│┘à ┘ü┘å█î ┘ä╪║┘ê ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ (The One-Click Unsubscribe)
┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ┘å╪¿╪º█î╪» ╪¿╪▒╪º█î ┘ä╪║┘ê ╪º╪┤╪¬╪▒╪º┌⌐ ┘à╪¼╪¿┘ê╪▒ ╪¿┘ç ┘ä╪º┌»█î┘å ┌⌐╪▒╪»┘å █î╪º ╪╖█î ┌⌐╪▒╪»┘å ┘à╪▒╪º╪¡┘ä ┘╛█î┌å█î╪»┘ç ╪¿╪º╪┤╪».

One-Click Logic: ┘ä█î┘å┌⌐ ╪º█î┘à█î┘ä ╪¿╪º█î╪» ╪¡╪º┘ê█î █î┌⌐ ╪¬┘ê┌⌐┘å ╪º┘à┘å█î╪¬█î ┘à┘å╪¡╪╡╪▒╪¿┘çΓÇî┘ü╪▒╪» (Hashed Token) ╪¿╪º╪┤╪»:
https://yourdomain.ca/unsubscribe?token=abc123xyz

Landing Page: ┘ê┘é╪¬█î ┌⌐╪º╪▒╪¿╪▒ ╪▒┘ê█î ┘ä█î┘å┌⌐ ┌⌐┘ä█î┌⌐ ┘à█îΓÇî┌⌐┘å╪»╪î █î┌⌐ ╪╡┘ü╪¡┘ç React ╪¿╪º╪▓ ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê╪» ┌⌐┘ç ┘╛█î╪º┘à█î ╪│╪º╪»┘ç ┘å┘à╪º█î╪┤ ┘à█îΓÇî╪»┘ç╪»: "You have been unsubscribed from our marketing updates."

Backend Sync: ╪│╪▒┘ê█î╪│ NestJS ╪¿┘ä╪º┘ü╪º╪╡┘ä┘ç ┘ü█î┘ä╪» notifications.marketing.email ╪▒╪º ╪»╪▒ ╪»█î╪¬╪º╪¿█î╪│ MongoDB (┘à╪╖╪º╪¿┘é ╪¿╪º Issue #158) ╪¿┘ç false ╪¬╪║█î█î╪▒ ┘à█îΓÇî╪»┘ç╪».

`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 214: Issue #215: AI-Powered Re-engagement & Multi-Channel Content Engine`n`n[Description]`n
≡ƒô¥ Description
This issue involves building an automated system to detect dormant users (those who haven't logged in for a specific period or have uninstalled the app) and bring them back using personalized, AI-generated industry insights. Simultaneously, the system will act as a Social Media Manager, posting these insights to Telegram, Instagram, and Facebook to keep the brand active in the user's social feeds.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Implementation Strategy
1. NestJS (The Brain & Scheduler)
The backend manages the lifecycle of a dormant user and triggers the AI workflows.

Inactivity Monitor: A daily Cron Job identifies users whose lastActive timestamp is older than 14 days.

Knowledge Retrieval: The system fetches current news related to the user's specific business sector (e.g., "Grocery Wholesale" or "Construction Logistics") using News APIs.

AI Orchestration (Gemini/GPT-4):

Prompt 1 (Personalized Email): Generate a "Value-Add" email. Example: "Hey Ahmad, fuel prices in BC just dropped by 5%. Here is how this impacts your delivery margins this week..."

Prompt 2 (Social Media): Convert the same insight into a short, catchy post for Instagram/Telegram with relevant hashtags.

Social API Integrations: Use Telegram Bot API and Facebook Graph API to publish content automatically.

2. React Web (The Content & Social Dashboard)
As the Admin, you need to control the "Machine."

Campaign Manager: A UI to see how many users are currently in the "Re-engagement" phase.

This issue introduces the Bulk Ingestion Layer. It allows vendors to bypass manual entry by uploading Excel (XLSX) or CSV files. The core challenge is building a "Fault-Tolerant" engine that validates thousands of rows of data for schema errors (types), logical errors (negative prices), and database constraints (duplicate SKUs) before any data is committed to MongoDB.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Implementation Strategy
1. NestJS: The Stream-Based Validator
To handle massive files without crashing the server's memory, we move away from typical JSON parsing.

ExcelJS Streaming: Use the streaming reader to process the file row-by-row. This ensures that even a 10,000-row file only consumes a few megabytes of RAM.

Validation Pipeline: Every row passes through a dedicated service:

Data Integrity: Checks if price is a positive number and SKU follows the required format.

Relational Check: Verifies if the categoryID provided in the Excel actually exists in our system.

BullMQ Integration: Processing 5,000 items is a "Heavy Task." We return a jobId to the frontend and process the data in the background. This prevents Gateway Timeouts.

2. React Web: The Management Command Center
The Web Dashboard is where the "heavy lifting" and user interaction happen.

The Mapping Wizard: Not every vendor uses the same Excel headers. We implement a "Column Mapper" where the user matches their Excel columns (e.g., "Item Name") to our system fields (e.g., name).

Dry-Run Preview: Before the final import, show a table preview.

Success Rows: Highlighted in green.

Error Rows: Highlighted in red with a specific tooltip (e.g., "Row 45: Price cannot be empty").

Socket.io Updates: Use the existing WebSocket (Issue #131) logic to push real-time progress percentages to the dashboard.

3. Flutter Mobile: The Status Tracker
Mobile is optimized for "Notification & Quick Action."

 ≡ƒ¢á Failure Analysis & Optimization Workflow

The logging system identifies whether a failure is caused by hardware limitations or logical "hallucinations" in the model's reasoning.



```dart
// lib/core/services/ai_monitor_service.dart

class AIMonitor {
  static const int timeoutLimit = 5000; // 5 seconds

  Future<void> trackInference(Stopwatch watch, bool wasHallucinated) async {
    final int elapsed = watch.elapsedMilliseconds;
    
    await FirebaseAnalytics.instance.logEvent(
      name: 'ai_health_check',
      parameters: {
        'duration_ms': elapsed,
        'status': elapsed > timeoutLimit ? 'timeout' : 'success',
        'hallucination_flag': wasHallucinated ? 1 : 0,
        'ram_at_start': await _getAvailableRAM(),
      },
    );
  }
}
```

---

- [ ] Monthly "AI Health Reports" can be generated to track the reduction in average latency.
- [ ] Zero user-input text is included in these logs to maintain Zero-Cloud Privacy.

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 Operational Integrity: This monitoring ensures we meet our internal SLA (Service Level Agreement) for response times.
 Privacy Assurance: By logging only metadata (how long it took, not what was said), we remain 100% PIPEDA compliant.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 209: Issue 209: Track AI Chat Helpfulness (Thumbs Up/Down)`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Description

class FeedbackButtons extends StatelessWidget {
  final String messageId;
  final String queryType;

  void _sendFeedback(bool helpful) {
    FirebaseAnalytics.instance.logEvent(
      name: 'ai_response_feedback',
      parameters: {
        'is_helpful': helpful ? 1 : 0,
        'query_type': queryType,
        'timestamp': DateTime.now().toIso8601String(),
      },
    );
    // Visual feedback for the user
    showToast("Thanks for helping us improve!");
  }

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return Row(
      children: [
        IconButton(icon: Icon(Icons.thumb_up_outlined), onPressed: () => _sendFeedback(true)),
        IconButton(icon: Icon(Icons.thumb_down_outlined), onPressed: () => _sendFeedback(false)),
      ],
    );
  }
}

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. Extracting Coefficients/Weights

 Categories to Track:

     Example: If for "Construction," the `logistics_weight` is consistently $> 0.6$, we will prioritize real-time fuel price integration in the next sprint.

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Feature Importance & Model Optimization Workflow

The analytics loop identifies which "lever" in the business has the most impact on the final cost prediction.



```dart
// lib/core/services/ai_optimization_service.dart

class FeatureAnalytics {
  Future<void> logFeatureWeights(Map<String, double> weights, String industry) async {
    // Weights example: {'rent': 0.15, 'payroll': 0.45, 'materials': 0.35}
    
    await FirebaseAnalytics.instance.logEvent(
      name: 'feature_importance_stats',
      parameters: {
        'industry_type': industry,
        'top_predictor': _getTopFeature(weights),
        'payroll_impact': weights['payroll'] ?? 0.0,
        'rent_impact': weights['rent'] ?? 0.0,
        'materials_impact': weights['materials'] ?? 0.0,
      },
    );
  }

  String _getTopFeature(Map<String, double> weights) {
    return weights.entries.reduce((a, b) => a.value > b.value ? a : b).key;
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] The backend/edge engine calculates and extracts feature weights for every prediction.
- [ ] Data is successfully aggregated in BigQuery/Firebase without any PII.

- [ ] The weights are used to re-calibrate the "Suggestion Prompts" in Issue 201 (e.g., if Payroll is the top driver, suggest "Ways to optimize labor costs").

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 Data Aggregation: We only store the relative importance of categories, not the actual dollar amounts, ensuring 100% compliance with PIPEDA.

To maintain high confidence in our financial forecasting, we need to implement a feedback loop that measures the accuracy of our Monthly Spending Predictions. At the end of each billing cycle (30 days), the system will compare the "Predicted Total" against the "Actual Total."  ≡ƒæñ User Story

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. MAPE Calculation Logic
 Metric Definition: Implement the formula:  
  `MAPE = ( |Actual - Predicted| / Actual )  100`
 Trigger Point: A background cron job in NestJS (or a local WorkManager task in Flutter) that runs on the 1st of every month to reconcile the previous month's data.

 2. Firebase Custom Event Logging
 Event Name: `forecast_accuracy_report`
 Parameters:
     `mape_value`: The calculated percentage error.
     `industry_type`: (e.g., Construction, Retail, Tech).
     `region`: (e.g., BC, Ontario).
     `data_volume`: Number of invoices used for the prediction (to identify if "low data" causes higher error).

 3. Accuracy Threshold Alerts
 Critical Error Flag: If `MAPE > 20%` for a specific user cohort, trigger a non-PII alert in Sentry to investigate potential data quality issues or OCR errors in that region.

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Regression Accuracy & Feedback Loop Workflow

╪º█î┘å ╪¼╪▒█î╪º┘å ┌⌐╪º╪▒█î ╪¬╪╢┘à█î┘å ┘à█îΓÇî┌⌐┘å╪» ┌⌐┘ç ┘à╪»┘äΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ╪▒█î╪º╪╢█î ╪¬┘ê ╪¿┘ç ╪╡┘ê╪▒╪¬ ┘à╪»╪º┘ê┘à ╪¿╪º ╪»╪º╪»┘çΓÇî┘ç╪º█î ┘ê╪º┘é╪╣█î ┌⌐╪º┘ä█î╪¿╪▒┘ç ┘à█îΓÇî╪┤┘ê┘å╪».



```dart
// lib/core/services/analytics_accuracy_service.dart

class AccuracyTracker {
  Future<void> logMonthlyMAPE(double predicted, double actual, String industry) async {
    // Calculate Absolute Percentage Error
    double mape = ((actual - predicted).abs() / actual)  100;

    // Log to Firebase for global model optimization
    await FirebaseAnalytics.instance.logEvent(
      name: 'forecast_accuracy_report',
      parameters: {
        'mape_value': mape,
        'industry': industry,
        'is_outlier': mape > 20.0 ? 1 : 0,
        'province': 'British Columbia', // Inferred from local context
      },
    );
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] A background job successfully compares `predicted_spend` vs `actual_spend` at the end of the month.
- [ ] MAPE is correctly calculated and logged to Firebase for 100% of the Beta users.
- [ ] A dashboard in Google Analytics 4 is created to visualize "Average Accuracy by Industry."
- [ ] Outliers (errors > 25%) are flagged for manual review of the underlying regression logic.
- [ ] Zero Personal Identifiable Information (PII) is included in the analytics payload.

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 PIPEDA Alignment: Since we are only tracking "Error Percentages" and "Industry Types" without User IDs, this data is considered Non-Identifiable, satisfying the highest privacy standards.
 Model Integrity: This audit trail protects the business from making false financial promises, ensuring all marketing claims are backed by monitored data.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 206: Issue 206: Secret Scanning & PII Protection (Compliance CI)`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Description
To comply with the Personal Information Protection and Electronic Documents Act (PIPEDA) and ensure the highest level of security, we must implement an automated Compliance CI layer. This task focuses on two critical areas: (1) Secret Scanning to prevent accidental leaks of sensitive credentials (AWS, Plaid, Stripe) into the repository, and (2) Contract Testing to verify that PII (Personally Identifiable Information) is strictly filtered and never exposed through public API endpoints.

 ≡ƒæñ User Story
As a Security Officer, I want to ensure that no developer accidentally commits a private key or exposes a user's SIN/Home Address in an API response, so that we remain compliant with Canadian privacy laws.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. Automated Secret Scanning
 Pre-commit Hooks: Implement `git-secrets` or `trufflehog` locally to scan for patterns (API Keys, Private RSA Keys) before a developer can even run `git commit`.
 GitHub Advanced Security: Enable "Secret Scanning" and "Push Protection" on the GitHub repository to block any PR containing high-entropy strings or known service tokens.

Even with RAG (Issue 198), Small Language Models (SLMs) can sometimes misinterpret numbers or "hallucinate" financial summaries. This issue focuses on implementing a Verification Engine that intercepts the AI's response before it reaches the UI. It cross-references any numerical values or dates mentioned in the response against the actual records in the local database. If a significant discrepancy is found, the system will either self-correct or add a warning disclaimer to the user.

 ≡ƒæñ User Story
As a Business Owner in British Columbia, I need to be 100% sure that the numbers the AI tells me are accurate, so I don't make incorrect financial decisions based on a "hallucinated" summary.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. Numerical Extraction & Cross-Check
 Regex Scraper: Implement a post-processing utility to extract all currency amounts (`$XXX.XX`) and dates from the LLM's generated text.
 Database Verification: For each extracted amount, query ObjectBox to verify if that number exists in the context provided.
     Example: If AI says "You spent $450 at Costco," the system checks if a $450 Costco transaction actually exists in the retrieved context.

 2. Confidence Scoring System
 Exact Match: If numbers match perfectly, mark as "High Confidence."
 Fuzzy Match: If the AI rounds numbers (e.g., "$452.80" becomes "$450"), calculate the margin of error.
 Mismatch Trigger: If the AI mentions a number that has 0% correlation with the local data, flag it as a "Hallucination."

 3. UI Warning & Self-Correction
 Disclaimer UI: If confidence is low, display a small icon next to the message: "ΓÜá∩╕Å Note: Some numbers may be approximated. Please verify with your raw records."
 Self-Correction Loop: If a hallucination is detected, re-send a hidden prompt to the SLM: "Error: You mentioned $500 but the record shows $450. Please correct your summary."

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Hallucination Guardrail Workflow

The system acts as a "Financial Auditor" that sits between the AI's creative engine and the user's screen.



```dart
// lib/core/services/hallucination_guard_service.dart

class HallucinationGuard {
  final ObjectBox _database = ObjectBox.instance;

  Future<ValidationResult> verifyResponse(String aiResponse, List<Transaction> context) async {
    final extractedNumbers = _regexUtils.extractAmounts(aiResponse); // e.g., [450.00]
    
    for (var amount in extractedNumbers) {
      bool exists = context.any((tx) => (tx.amount - amount).abs() < 1.0); // Allow $1 rounding
      
      if (!exists) {
        return ValidationResult(
          isValid: false,
          warning: "AI mentioned a figure ($amount) not found in local records."
        );
      }
    }
    return ValidationResult(isValid: true);
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] The system correctly identifies and flags currency values in the AI's output.
- [ ] A warning message is displayed if the AI mentions a total that doesn't match the sum of the provided context.
- [ ] The "Self-Correction" loop triggers successfully for simple arithmetic errors.
- [ ] The verification process adds less than 200ms of latency to the final response.
- [ ] UI displays a "Verified Data" checkmark when numbers are 100% matched with ObjectBox.

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 Integrity: This guardrail is a core requirement for CRA-related features, ensuring that the app does not provide misleading tax information.
 Transparency: In alignment with Canada's Bill C-27 (AIDA), we are providing transparency by notifying the user that they are interacting with an automated system and verifying its accuracy.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 202: Issue 202: Zero-Cloud AI Verification Audit`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Description

// lib/core/utils/privacy_guard.dart

class PrivacyGuard {
  static void verifyOfflineSafety() {
    // Audit check: This function can be triggered in 'Debug Mode'
    // to alert the developer if any Network Call is initiated 
    // during an active LLM Inference session.
    
    if (NetworkInspector.isActiveRequest) {
      Logger.emergency("PRIVACY BREACH: Network activity detected during AI Inference!");
      throw SecurityException("Inference must be 100% offline.");

  }
}

}
