The Context Provider Service acts as the bridge between the User's natural language query and the local ObjectBox Vector Database (Issue 198). Its primary role is to perform "Intent Analysis" and "Context Window Management." It ensures that only the most relevant, high-density financial data is injected into the SLM's limited context window, preventing hallucinations and reducing token processing time on the device.

 ≡ƒæñ User Story
As a Business User, I want the AI to precisely reference my March expenses when I ask about them, so that I can get accurate financial insights without the AI mixing up data from other months.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. Hybrid Intent Analysis
 Entity Extraction: Use a lightweight Regex or a dedicated NER (Named Entity Recognition) layer to extract:
     Timeframes: (e.g., "March", "Last Quarter").
     Categories: (e.g., "Fuel", "Rent", "Office Supplies").
     Locations: (e.g., "Surrey", "Burnaby").
 Query Pre-processing: Convert relative terms like "Last month" into absolute dates based on the current system time (April 2026).

 2. Context Window Management (Sliding Window)
 Token Budgeting: The SLM (Gemma/Phi) has a limited context window (e.g., 2048 or 4096 tokens). The service must limit the injected context to ~1000 tokens to leave room for the response.
 Prioritization Logic:
    1. Direct Matches: Exact dates/categories requested.
    2. Semantic Matches: Results from ObjectBox Vector Search.
    3. Metadata Summary: A brief summary of the user's total balance to give the AI "General Awareness."

 3. Privacy-Safe Prompt Construction
 Template Injection: Format the retrieved data into a clean, structured string (JSON or Markdown) inside the prompt.
 System Instruction: Prefix the context with instructions: "Use the following verified local data to answer. If the data is not present, say you don't know."

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Context Injection & Token Management Workflow

The Context Provider filters the "Ocean" of user data into a "Glass" of relevant information for the AI.



```dart
// lib/core/services/context_provider_service.dart

class ContextProviderService {
  final VectorSearchService _vectorSearch = VectorSearchService();

  Future<String> buildAugmentedPrompt(String userQuery) async {
    // 1. Extract Entities (March, Fuel, etc.)
    final intent = _intentAnalyzer.parse(userQuery);

    // 2. Fetch relevant data from ObjectBox
    final List<Transaction> matches = await _vectorSearch.search(
      query: userQuery,
      dateRange: intent.dateRange,
      category: intent.category,
    );

    // 3. Convert to Markdown Table (Dense & Token-efficient)
    String contextData = "| Date | Merchant | Amount | Category |\n|---|---|---|---|\n";
    for (var tx in matches) {
      contextData += "| ${tx.date} | ${tx.merchant} | \$${tx.amount} | ${tx.category} |\n";
    }

    // 4. Wrap in System Prompt
    return """
      [LOCAL DATA CONTEXT]
      ${contextData}
      
      [USER QUESTION]
      ${userQuery}
      
      Answer based ONLY on the data above. Use CAD currency.
    """;
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] The service can successfully parse date-related queries (e.g., "Last 30 days").
- [ ] Only the top 5-10 most relevant transactions are injected into the prompt.
- [ ] The generated context does not exceed 1000 tokens (monitored via a local tokenizer).
- [ ] All PII (Names, Addresses) is formatted clearly so the SLM can read it.
- [ ] The system correctly handles "No Data Found" scenarios without crashing.

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 Zero Leakage: The "Augmented Prompt" exists only in the app's volatile memory and is never written to disk or sent to a cloud server.
 Context Isolation: Ensure that context from User A can never be retrieved or injected into a session for User B (Multitenancy Safety).`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 198: Issue 198: Implement Local Vector Database (ObjectBox Vector Search)`n`n[Description]`n

 ≡ƒô¥ Description
To enable the On-Device AI to answer specific questions about the user's financial data (e.g., "How much did I spend on logistics in Surrey last quarter?"), we must implement a Local Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) pipeline. This involves converting local documents and transactions stored in ObjectBox into high-dimensional numerical vectors (Embeddings) and using ObjectBox Vector Search to retrieve relevant context for the LLM.

 ≡ƒæñ User Story
As a Business Owner, I want to ask the AI assistant questions about my own business data so that I can get instant insights without manually searching through dozens of old invoices.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. Local Embedding Generation
 Model Selection: Integrate a lightweight embedding model (e.g., `all-MiniLM-L6-v2` via ONNX or MediaPipe) that runs entirely on the mobile CPU/GPU.
 Data Sanitization: Before vectorizing, clean the text from invoices (Merchant Name, Date, Line Items, GST/HST) to ensure high-quality search results.

 2. ObjectBox Vector Store Integration
 Vector Indexing: Configure ObjectBox to store `float32` arrays (vectors) alongside the existing transaction entities.
 HNSW Algorithm: Utilize ObjectBoxΓÇÖs implementation of Hierarchical Navigable Small World (HNSW) to perform sub-millisecond similarity searches across thousands of local records.

 3. RAG Orchestration (The "Context Provider")
 Semantic Retrieval: When a user asks a question, generate an embedding for the query and find the top-k (e.g., Top 5) most relevant transaction fragments in ObjectBox.
 Prompt Injection: Append the retrieved data as "Context" to the LLM prompt:
  > "Context: [Invoice A: $500 from ABC Logistics, Surrey]. Question: What was my last logistics spend?"

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Local RAG & Vector Search Architecture

The RAG process bridges the gap between the static knowledge of the LLM and the dynamic, private data of the user.



```dart
// lib/core/services/vector_search_service.dart

@Entity()
class TransactionEntity {
  int id = 0;
  String description;
  double amount;
  
  // The magic field: Vector representation of the transaction
  @Property(type: PropertyType.floatVector)
  List<double>? embedding;

  TransactionEntity({required this.description, required this.amount, this.embedding});
}

class RAGService {
  final Box<TransactionEntity> _box = ObjectBox.instance.box<TransactionEntity>();

  Future<List<TransactionEntity>> getContext(String userQuery) async {
    // 1. Convert query to vector
    final queryVector = await _embeddingModel.encode(userQuery);
    
    // 2. Perform nearest neighbor search in ObjectBox
    final query = _box.query(
      TransactionEntity_.embedding.nearestNeighbors(queryVector, 5)
    ).build();
    
    return query.find();
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] The local embedding model successfully generates 384-dimension vectors on-device.
- [ ] ObjectBox is configured to store and index these vectors without bloating app size (limit to essential fields).
- [ ] Similarity search returns relevant transactions for a query (e.g., "Surrey invoices" returns records with "Surrey" in the metadata).
- [ ] Memory usage during vector search + LLM inference remains within the 1.5GB limit (Issue 197).
- [ ] The RAG pipeline works 100% offline, with zero data sent to external servers.

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 Absolute Privacy: Since the Vector DB is local, even the "embeddings" (which are mathematical representations of PII) never leave the device.
 Data Integrity: Ensure that if a user deletes an account (Issue 182), the ObjectBox Vector Index is also fully purged.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 197: Issue 197: Memory & Asset Management for SLM`n`n[Description]`n ≡ƒô¥ Description
Running LLMs on mobile devices poses a significant challenge for memory stability. This issue focuses on optimizing the RAM footprint of our on-device AI engine to ensure the main Flutter UI thread remains responsive and other app services (like OCR or Networking) are not terminated by the OS. We will implement 4-bit Quantization (INT4) to compress the model size and utilize a strict Lifecycle-based Memory Management strategy.

 ≡ƒæñ User Story
As a User with a mid-range smartphone, I want the AI assistant to work smoothly without slowing down the rest of the app or causing it to crash due to high memory usage.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. 4-bit (INT4) Quantization Strategy
 Weight Compression: Use MediaPipe Model Maker or TensorFlow Lite Converter to convert the base model (e.g., Gemma 2B) from FP32/FP16 to INT4.
 Impact: Reduce the model's disk and RAM footprint by ~70% (from ~4GB down to ~1.2GB) while maintaining ~90-95% of the original reasoning capabilities.
 Hardware Support: Ensure the quantized model is optimized for Apple Neural Engine (ANE) and Android NNAPI.

 2. Advanced RAM Management (Flutter/Native)
 On-Demand Loading: Do not load the model into RAM at app startup. Load it only when the user enters the "AI Assistant" screen.
 Lazy Unloading: Implement a timer-based or navigation-based disposal. If the user leaves the AI screen for more than 2 minutes, the `InferenceEngine` must be destroyed to free up memory.
 Background Isolate: Move the inference heavy-lifting to a Flutter Isolate or a dedicated Native Background Thread to prevent UI jank.

 3. Crash Prevention & Monitoring
 Pre-Flight Memory Check: Use `system_info_plus` to check available RAM before model initialization. If available RAM is < 500MB, alert the user and suggest closing other apps.
 Low Memory Callbacks: Listen to `onLowMemory()` in Android and `didReceiveMemoryWarning` in iOS to gracefully terminate the AI engine before the OS kills the app.

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Model Quantization & Memory Lifecycle

The quantization process reduces the precision of weights, allowing the model to fit into the limited cache and RAM of mobile processors.



```dart
// lib/core/services/memory_aware_ai.dart

class MemoryAwareAIService {
  static final MemoryAwareAIService _instance = MemoryAwareAIService._internal();
  bool _isModelLoaded = false;

  Future<void> initializeEngine() async {
    final availableRam = await _getAvailableRam(); // Custom Method
    
    if (availableRam < 1024) { // Threshold for 2B INT4 Model
      throw LowMemoryException("Insufficient RAM to start AI engine.");
    }

    // Initialize MediaPipe with INT4 Quantized Model
    await _loadQuantizedModel("gemma-2b-int4.bin");
    _isModelLoaded = true;
  }

  void disposeEngine() {
    if (_isModelLoaded) {
      // Explicitly call native C++ cleanup to purge RAM
      NativeAIBinding.freeMemory(); 
      _isModelLoaded = false;
    }
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] The model file (Gemma/Phi) is compressed to under 1.5GB using INT4 quantization.
- [ ] RAM usage of the entire app during active AI inference does not exceed 1.2GB on average.
- [ ] The Flutter UI maintains 60 FPS while the AI engine is processing a query in the background.
- [ ] The app does not crash on devices with 4GB Total RAM (Standard for older Androids).
- [ ] The model is successfully purged from memory within 30 seconds of the user navigating away from the AI screen.

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 Data Safety: Quantization happens during development (Pre-build). No raw/sensitive weights are ever sent over the network during this process.
 Performance Stability: By preventing crashes, we maintain the "Reliability" pillar of our SLA for business users in Canada.

```dart
class AnalyticsService {

  // Track the most important conversion event for SMBs

        'currency': 'CAD',
      },
    );
  }

  // Segment users by Canadian Province

    await _analytics.setUserProperty(name: 'user_province', value: province);
  }
}
```

```dart
class SupportService {

        'industry': user.industryType,
        'is_founding_member': user.tags.contains('FOUNDING_MEMBER'),
      },
    );
  }

  static void showChat() {
    Intercom.instance.displayMessenger();
  }
}
```

```typescript
async registerUser(dto: RegisterDto) {
  const userCount = await this.userModel.countDocuments();
  const isFoundingMember = userCount < 100;

  const newUser = await this.userModel.create({
    ...dto,
    isPremium: isFoundingMember,
    premiumExpiry: isFoundingMember ? addMonths(new Date(), 6) : null,
    tags: isFoundingMember ? ['FOUNDING_MEMBER'] : [],
  });

  if (isFoundingMember) {
    await this.notificationService.sendWelcomeBetaEmail(newUser.email);
  }
  
  return newUser;
}
```

 ≡ƒ¢á CI/CD Automation Workflow

Fastlane acts as the bridge between your Flutter code and the global distribution platforms, ensuring every "Release" is identical in quality.



```ruby
 ios/fastlane/Fastfile

platform :ios do
  desc "Capture screenshots and upload to App Store"
  lane :screenshots do
    capture_screenshots(
      scheme: "Runner",
      languages: ["en-CA", "fr-CA"],  Supporting both Canadian languages
      devices: ["iPhone 13 Pro Max", "iPad Pro (12.9-inch)"]
```

`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 187: Issue 187: Develop Data Breach Notification System`n`n[Description]`n
 ≡ƒô¥ Description
In strict adherence to PIPEDA's Mandatory Breach Reporting regulations, we must implement a standardized protocol and technical infrastructure for Data Breach Notifications. This system ensures that if a security safeguard is compromised, the affected users and relevant Canadian authorities are notified without "unreasonable delay." This includes a dedicated notification engine, a breach incident log, and pre-approved communication templates.

 ≡ƒæñ User Story
As a User in British Columbia, I want to be immediately notified if my sensitive financial or personal data is compromised, so that I can take proactive steps (like changing passwords or monitoring my bank account) to protect myself.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. Breach Detection & Logging Trigger
 Incident Log: Create a restricted-access database table `SecurityIncidents` to document the circumstances of the breach, the data involved, and the steps taken to reduce harm.
 Audit Integration: Link this system with Issue 186 (Audit Logs) to automatically flag mass data export attempts or unauthorized access to Plaid tokens as "Potential Breach Events."

 2. Multi-Channel Notification Engine (NestJS)
 Email Service: Integrate with SendGrid or AWS SES using pre-verified "Security Alert" templates.
 Push Notifications: Use FCM (Firebase Cloud Messaging) to send high-priority alerts to the Flutter app.
 Queue Management: Use BullMQ to ensure that thousands of notifications are sent out immediately and reliably without crashing the server.

 3. Legal Disclosure Templates
Maintain a set of "Ready-to-Use" templates that satisfy PIPEDA requirements:
 What happened: Description of the breach.
 When it happened: Timing and duration.
 What data was involved: (e.g., Names, Bank account numbers).
 Steps taken: What the company is doing to fix it.
 Steps for the user: What the user should do (e.g., "Reset your password now").
 Contact Info: How to reach our Privacy Officer.

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Incident Response & Notification Workflow

The system acts as an emergency broadcast network, ensuring information moves faster than the threat.



```typescript
// backend/src/security/breach-notification.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class BreachNotificationService {
  async triggerEmergencyAlert(incidentId: string, affectedUsers: string[]) {
    const incident = await this.incidentModel.findById(incidentId);
    
    // 1. Log the start of the notification process for legal compliance
    await this.auditService.logBreachResponse(incidentId);

    // 2. Dispatch Bulk Notifications via BullMQ
    for (const userId of affectedUsers) {
      await this.mailQueue.add('send-breach-alert', {
        userId,
        template: 'pipeda_compliant_alert',
        incidentDetails: incident.summary,
      }, { priority: 1 }); // Highest Priority
    }
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] A dedicated "Emergency Notification" pipeline is functional and tested.
- [ ] Breach emails satisfy all legal disclosure requirements of the OPC (Office of the Privacy Commissioner).
- [ ] The system can send 10,000+ notifications within 15 minutes of trigger.
- [ ] All breach notifications are recorded in an immutable "Compliance Log" for 24 months (minimum legal requirement).
- [ ] A "Security Advisory" page is ready on the website to host public notices if individual notification is not possible.

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 PIPEDA Mandatory Reporting: Failure to report a breach can result in fines up to $100,000 CAD per violation. This system is our primary insurance against such penalties.
 Privacy by Design: Ensure the notification process itself does not expose more data (e.g., don't include the stolen password in the email).
 Direct Notification: Under Canadian law, you must notify users directly (email, letter, or phone) unless it would cause further harm or cost is prohibitive.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 186: Issue 186: Setup Security Audit Logs`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Description
To ensure full accountability and support future security forensics, we need to implement a Write-Once, Read-Many (WORM) style Audit Logging system. This system will track all "High-Sensitivity" actions across the platform. Unlike standard application logs (Sentry/Winston), these Audit Logs are strictly for security auditing and must be immutable, searchable, and stored separately from general debug data.

 ≡ƒæñ User Story
As a Compliance Auditor, I want to see a chronological record of every sensitive action (e.g., password changes, data exports, or logins from new IPs), so that I can investigate potential insider threats or external breaches effectively.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. High-Sensitivity Event Triggers
Capture and log the following events across NestJS and Flutter:
 Authentication: Successful logins, failed attempts (Issue 181), and account lockouts.
 Identity Management: Password changes, 2FA enablement/disablement, and Email updates.
 Data Access: Bulk exports of financial data (Issue 141) and access to Plaid banking tokens.
 System Changes: Changes to User Roles (e.g., promoting a user to Admin).
 Geo-Anomalies: Logins from an IP address or City (e.g., outside of BC/Canada) that differs significantly from the user's history.

 2. Audit Log Schema (Immutable)
 Storage: Use a dedicated MongoDB collection `SecurityAuditLogs` or a dedicated service like AWS CloudWatch Logs.
 Schema Fields:
     `timestamp`: ISO 8601 UTC time.
     `userId`: The actor performing the action.
     `actionType`: (e.g., `PASSWORD_CHANGE`, `DATA_EXPORT`).
     `ipAddress`: The originating IP.
     `userAgent`: Device/Browser info.
     `status`: SUCCESS or FAILURE.
     `metadata`: (e.g., "Exported 500 records"). Note: NEVER log actual PII or Passwords.

 3. Implementation (NestJS Interceptors)
 Interceptor Pattern: Create a `SecurityAuditInterceptor` that can be applied as a decorator `@Audit(ActionType.DATA_EXPORT)` to specific controller methods.
 Async Processing: Use BullMQ to write logs asynchronously so the user's request performance is not impacted.

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Security Audit & Incident Response Workflow

The audit system creates a permanent trail that can be used to reconstruct the sequence of events during a security incident.



```typescript
// backend/src/common/decorators/audit.decorator.ts

@Injectable()
export class AuditInterceptor implements NestInterceptor {
  intercept(context: ExecutionContext, next: CallHandler): Observable<any> {
    const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();
    const user = request.user;

    return next.handle().pipe(
      tap(() => {
        // Record the event after successful execution
        this.auditService.log({
          userId: user.id,
          action: 'SENSITIVE_DATA_ACCESS',
          ip: request.ip,
          timestamp: new Date(),
        });
      }),
    );
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] A dedicated `SecurityAuditLogs` collection is active in MongoDB Atlas (Canada Region).
- [ ] Every password change event is successfully logged with IP and Timestamp.
- [ ] Failed login attempts (Brute-force) trigger an audit entry for monitoring.
- [ ] Exporting a CSV/Excel file (Issue 141) creates a corresponding audit log.
- [ ] Audit logs are "Append-Only" (the API must not have an endpoint to Update or Delete these logs).

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 PIPEDA Requirement: Maintaining an audit trail is a key "Safeguard" for protecting personal information under Canadian law.
 Retention Policy: Set a TTL (Time-To-Live) index of 7 years on audit logs to comply with Canadian business record-keeping standards.
 Tamper-Evidence: Ensure that even an Admin user cannot modify the audit trail to hide their activities.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 185: Issue 185: Audit Encryption-at-Rest & In-Transit`n`n[Description]`n
 ≡ƒô¥ Description

 SSL/TLS Certificates: Ensure certificates are issued by a trusted CA (e.g., Let's Encrypt or ACM) and are set to auto-renew.
 Certificate Pinning (Optional): Evaluate the use of SSL Pinning in the Flutter app to prevent Man-in-the-Middle (MitM) attacks.

This issue focuses on protecting the supply chain of your software. We will integrate Snyk and GitHub Advanced Security tools to scan for "Vulnerable Dependencies" and "Leaked Secrets." This ensures that every line of code in NestJS, React, and Flutter is verified against the latest global security databases (CVEs) before deployment.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Implementation Strategy
1. NestJS & React (Dependency Vulnerability Scanning)
The JavaScript ecosystem is vast, and sub-dependencies can often hide security holes.

Snyk Integration: Add snyk test to your GitHub Actions. Snyk has a more comprehensive database than the standard npm audit and can even suggest "Remediation Patches" automatically.

Dependabot: Configure a dependabot.yml file. It will monitor your package.json and automatically open a Pull Request to update a library (like axios or express) the moment a security fix is released.

2. Flutter Mobile (Pub Security Audit)
Mobile security is often overlooked, but the Dart/Pub ecosystem needs the same vigilance.

Dart Fix & Audit: Integrate dart pub deps --json analysis into the CI to check for outdated or insecure packages.

ProGuard/R8: (For Android) Ensure that the build process obfuscates the code, making it harder for malicious actors to reverse-engineer your Plaid integration logic.

3. Secret Scanning (The Global Guardrail)
Accidentally committing an AWS Secret Key or a Stripe Private Key can lead to catastrophic financial loss within minutes.

GitHub Secret Scanning: Enable this for the entire organization. If a developer pushes a commit containing a string that looks like a high-entropy key, GitHub will block the push or immediately alert the Owner (Issue #174).

Environment Variable Masking: Ensure that the CI/CD Pipeline (Issue #182) never prints sensitive variables to the logs.

≡ƒ¢á The "Secure-by-Default" Workflow
Develop: You add a new charting library to the React Dashboard to help with Issue #176.

Push: You push the code to a branch.

Scan: GitHub Actions triggers the Snyk scan. It finds that the charting library uses an old version of lodash with a "Prototype Pollution" vulnerability.

Block: The PR is marked with a Red X. You are prohibited from merging until the dependency is updated.

Remediate: You update the library, the scan passes (Green Check), and the "Lead Developer" can safely merge.

Success: Your production environment in the Lower Mainland remains hardened against known exploits.

Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (AC)
[ ] Zero High-Severity Vulnerabilities: The CI/CD pipeline must fail if any "High" or "Critical" vulnerability is detected in NestJS, React, or Flutter.

[ ] Secret Protection: A test commit with a "Fake" AWS key format must be successfully caught and blocked by the pre-commit or CI hooks.

[ ] Audit Trail: A security report is generated for every build, providing a "Paper Trail" for future compliance audits (like SOC2 or BC PIPA).`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 182: Issue #182: Infrastructure as Code (IaC) & Containerization`n`n[Description]`n
≡ƒô¥ Description
This issue focuses on the "Containerization" of your entire ecosystem. We will create Multi-Stage Dockerfiles to ensure small, secure production images. By using Docker Compose for orchestration and GitHub Actions for CI/CD, we automate the path from "Code Commit" to "Live Production," ensuring that your React dashboard and NestJS API are always deployed together in a verified state.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Implementation Strategy
1. NestJS & React (Optimized Dockerization)
We want our containers to be lean and fast to pull from a registry.

Multi-Stage Builds: * Stage 1 (Build): Install all devDependencies and compile TypeScript (NestJS) or bundle the React app (Vite).

Stage 2 (Production): Copy only the compiled /dist folder and node_modules into a tiny Alpine Linux image.

Nginx for React: The React container will use Nginx to serve static files and handle internal routing, providing better performance and security than a raw Node.js server for the frontend.

2. Docker Compose (Local & Staging Orchestration)
Managing four different services manually is impossible.

The Blueprint: Create a docker-compose.yml that defines how the API, Web, MongoDB, and Redis interact.

Networking: Use a private Docker network so that the React app and NestJS API can communicate securely without exposing the database to the public internet.

Volumes: Use Docker Volumes to ensure your MongoDB data persists even if the container is restarted or updated.

3. GitHub Actions (The Deployment Engine)
Automation is the key to scaling your business in the Lower Mainland.

Container Registry (GHCR): On every merge to main, GitHub Actions builds the Docker images and pushes them to the GitHub Container Registry.

Rolling Updates: The pipeline then connects to your production server and triggers a docker compose pull && docker compose up -d. This replaces the old containers with the new ones one-by-one, ensuring Zero Downtime.

≡ƒ¢á The "One-Command" Workflow
Develop: You finish Issue #181 (Testing) on your local machine in Coquitlam.

Verify: You run docker-compose up to see exactly how the API, Database, and Web Dashboard will behave in production.

Push: You push your code to GitHub.

Automate: GitHub Actions runs your tests, builds the optimized Docker images, and pushes them to the registry.

Deploy: The production server receives the "Update" signal, pulls the new images, and restarts the services.

Success: Within 2 minutes, your changes are live for every vendor in Vancouver, with zero manual intervention.

Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (AC)
[ ] Portability: The entire stack (Backend + Frontend + DB) must launch successfully on a fresh machine with only Docker installed.

[ ] Image Optimization: The final production NestJS image must be under 200MB to ensure fast deployment cycles.

[ ] Security: No secrets (AWS Keys, DB Passwords) are baked into the Docker images; they must be provided via an .env file or a Secret Manager at runtime.

[ ] Health Checks: Docker Compose must include healthcheck directives to ensure the API is fully "Ready" before the Nginx frontend starts sending traffic to it.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 181: Issue #181: Implementation of Cross-Platform Integration Testing`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Description
This issue establishes a triple-layer testing strategy. We will implement Contract Testing to ensure the API matches the frontend expectations, E2E Testing to simulate real user journeys on React and Flutter, and Integration Testing in NestJS using isolated database containers. This ensures that a change in one repository doesn't cause a "Silent Failure" in another.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Implementation Strategy
1. NestJS (Isolated Integration Testing)
We need to test the backend against a real database without polluting our development data.

Testcontainers: Use testcontainers to spin up a "disposable" MongoDB and Redis instance in Docker during the test run.

Supertest: Perform actual HTTP requests to your controllers.

Example: "Post a new invoice -> Check if the BullMQ worker (Issue #125) starts -> Verify the OCR status in the DB."

Contract Testing (Pact): NestJS acts as the "Provider." It publishes a "Contract" that defines exactly what every response looks like.

2. React Web (User Flow Automation)
On the web, we need to ensure the Admin Dashboard (Issue #143) remains functional after every update.

Playwright: Use Playwright for cross-browser testing (Chrome, Safari, Firefox).

Test Case: "Log in as an Owner -> Navigate to Team Management -> Invite a new Staff member -> Verify the invitation email was 'sent' (mocked)."

Component Testing: Use Cypress Component Testing to verify that the "Glassmorphism" cards (Issue #175) render correctly across different screen resolutions.

3. Flutter Mobile (Device-Specific UI Testing)
Mobile testing must account for different screen aspect ratios and OS behaviors.

This issue replaces traditional server-side file handling with a Client-to-Cloud model. ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Implementation Strategy

Logic: When a user wants to upload, NestJS generates a unique key (e.g., org_123/products/uuid.jpg) and returns a PUT URL that is valid for only 60 seconds.

Metadata Persistence: Once the client finishes the upload, they call a second endpoint POST /v1/storage/confirm to tell NestJS the file is ready. Only then is the file record saved to MongoDB.

ACL Management: * Public Folder: Product photos (accessible via CDN).

Private Folder: Invoices/Contracts (accessible only via another temporary "Get" signed URL for security).

Axios Parallel Uploads: Use axios to send the File object directly to the S3 URL.

Progress Tracking: Implement the onUploadProgress callback to show a real-time progress bar for each individual file in the dashboard.

XHR vs. Fetch: Use XHR (Axios) because it provides better progress tracking for large binary blobs than the native Fetch API.

3. Flutter Mobile (Resilient Mobile Uploads)
Mobile uploads are prone to network drops (e.g., driving through the Massey Tunnel).

Dio Client: Use the dio package for the upload.

Background Uploading: (Optional) If the file is large, use flutter_uploader to ensure the upload continues even if the user minimizes the app.

CDN Integration: Update the CachedNetworkImage configuration from Issue #139 to point to your CloudFront domain (e.g., cdn.yourbusiness.ca) instead of the raw S3 bucket URL.

≡ƒ¢á The Direct Upload Workflow

Authorize: React asks NestJS: "Can I upload this 5MB JPEG to the products folder?"

Sign: NestJS verifies the user's session and returns a Presigned URL.

Upload: React sends the file directly to S3. No data passes through your NestJS server.

Confirm: Once S3 returns a 200 OK, React tells NestJS: "The file is at [Path]. Please link it to Product #99."

Deliver: When a customer in Surrey opens the Flutter App, the app fetches the image from the CloudFront Edge Node in Seattle/Vancouver, ensuring a 50ms load time.

[ ] Security: Attempting to upload a file to a folder that doesn't belong to the user's orgId (from Issue #174) must be blocked by the backend.

Queue: NestJS receives the request and adds it to the BullMQ "Reporting" queue.

Generation: A background worker fetches 1,500 rows from MongoDB, renders the HTML template, and converts it to a PDF.

Storage: The PDF is uploaded to a Private S3 Bucket. NestJS generates a Presigned URL valid for only 10 minutes.

Delivery: The Socket.io gateway sends a message to the React client: { status: 'complete', url: 'https://s3.aws...signature...' }.

Action: The React UI changes from a loading spinner to a "Download Now" button.

Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (AC)
[ ] Branding: Every PDF report must include the company logo, date of generation, and professional headers/footers.

[ ] Tax Accuracy: Excel exports must include a "Tax Summary" sheet specifically breaking down GST and PST for BC businesses.

[ ] Security: Download links must expire after 10 minutes; files must be deleted from S3 after 24 hours to save cost and maintain privacy.

[ ] Capacity: The system must successfully generate a 5,000-row Excel file in under 30 seconds.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 171: Issue 171: Build "Refer-a-Business" Program`n`n[Description]`n ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Implementation Strategy

 1. NestJS (The Financial Orchestrator)
The backend manages the secure communication between your database and Stripe's API.

 In-App WebView: Use the `url_launcher` or `webview_flutter` package. When the user taps "Billing Settings" in the app, fetch the Portal URL from NestJS and open it in a secure browser view.

 ≡ƒ¢á Unified Billing Workflow



1.  Request: User clicks "Update Plan" on the React Dashboard.

4.  Redirect: The user is sent to the Stripe Customer Portal (branded with your logo/colors).

This issue focuses on performing a comprehensive Security Audit and Penetration Test (Pentest) across the entire ecosystem (Flutter, NestJS, and MongoDB Atlas).  ≡ƒæñ User Story
As a Compliance Officer, I want to ensure that all financial data and private communications are encrypted and inaccessible to unauthorized parties, so the company remains compliant with Canadian data protection laws and maintains user trust.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. API Penetration Testing (NestJS)
 Injection Attacks: Test for SQL/NoSQL injection in search queries and invoice metadata fields.
 Broken Object Level Authorization (BOLA): Ensure a user in Coquitlam cannot access the spending dashboard of a user in Burnaby by simply guessing an `ID` in the URL.
 Rate Limiting: Verify that Express Rate Limit or Throttler (NestJS) effectively prevents Brute Force attacks on the login and banking sync endpoints.

 2. Chat & Real-time Security (WebSockets)
 Socket Hijacking: Test if a non-participant can "listen in" on a private negotiation between a buyer and a vendor.
 Input Sanitization: Ensure the chat prevents XSS (Cross-Site Scripting) attacks via malicious links or scripts sent in messages.

 3. Financial Data & Token Safety
 Secret Management: Audit GitHub Actions and Heroku/AWS environments to ensure no API keys (Plaid Secret, QB Client ID) are hardcoded or exposed in logs.
 Token Rotation: Verify that Refresh Tokens are stored securely and that Access Tokens have a short TTL (Time-to-Live) of 15ΓÇô30 minutes.

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Vulnerability Mapping & Audit Flow

The audit follows a "Red Team" approach, simulating an external attack to harden the internal infrastructure.



```typescript
// backend/src/common/guards/security-audit.decorator.ts

/
  STRATEGY: Implement Strict Ownership Checks (Anti-BOLA)
  This guard ensures that the 'Requested Resource' strictly belongs 
  to the 'Authenticated User' before the Controller logic executes.
 /

@Injectable()
export class OwnershipGuard implements CanActivate {
  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {
    const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();
    const user = request.user;
    const resourceId = request.params.id;

    const resource = await this.db.findById(resourceId);
    if (!resource || resource.ownerId !== user.id) {
      // Log Attempted Unauthorized Access to Sentry (Issue 165)
      throw new ForbiddenException('Unauthorized access attempt logged.');
    }
    return true;
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] No "Critical" or "High" severity vulnerabilities remain in the OWASP Top 10 report.
- [ ] SSL Labs Report: The API domain scores an A+ for TLS configuration.
- [ ] JWT Audit: Tokens are verified to be signed with `RS256` and cannot be tampered with.
- [ ] Socket Security: Chat messages are confirmed to be delivered only to authorized members of a specific Room ID.

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 PIPEDA/PIPA Compliance: Confirm that all personal data is encrypted at rest (AES-256 via MongoDB Atlas) and in transit (TLS 1.3).
 Audit Trail: Ensure every access to sensitive banking data is logged in a "Read-Only" audit log for future forensics.
 Disclosure Policy: Prepare a Security.txt file for the website to allow white-hat hackers to report vulnerabilities safely.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 166: Issue 166: Implementation of Health Check Endpoints & Service Monitoring`n`n[Description]`n ≡ƒô¥ Description
This issue involves developing a standardized Health Check System within the NestJS backend using the `@nestjs/terminus` library. We need to create a dedicated `/health` endpoint that performs real-time status checks on our core infrastructure and third-party integrations (Plaid, QuickBooks, MongoDB Atlas). This system will integrate with our monitoring stack (Sentry/UptimeRobot) to provide immediate alerts if a critical dependency goes offline.

 ≡ƒæñ User Story
As a Lead Engineer, I want to receive an automated alert the moment our connection to the Plaid API fails, so I can investigate the token status or API downtime before a user attempts to sync their bank account and receives an error.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. The `/health` Endpoint (NestJS Terminus)
 Library: Install `@nestjs/terminus` and `godaddy/terminus` for standardized JSON health responses.
 Checks to Implement:
     Database: Verify active connection to MongoDB Atlas.
     Disk/Memory: Ensure the server isn't running out of storage or RAM.
     Third-Party APIs: Implement custom `HealthIndicator` classes for:
         Plaid: A simple ping to the `/item/get` or `/categories/get` endpoint.
         QuickBooks: Check the validity of the stored OAuth2 tokens and API reachability.

 2. Status Dashboard & Response Structure
 JSON Output: The endpoint should return a `200 OK` if all systems are up, and a `503 Service Unavailable` if a critical dependency (like the DB) is down.
 Structure:
  ```json
  {
    "status": "error",
    "info": { "database": { "status": "up" } },
    "error": { "plaid": { "status": "down", "message": "API Timeout" } }
  }
  ```

 3. External Uptime Monitoring
 UptimeRobot/Pingdom: Set up an external monitor to ping the `/health` endpoint every 60 seconds.
 Alerting: If the endpoint returns anything other than `200`, trigger an emergency notification via Sentry or a dedicated Slack Webhook.

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Monitoring & Dependency Flow

The health check acts as a diagnostic gateway, verifying each link in the data chain.



```typescript
// backend/src/health/health.controller.ts

@Controller('health')
export class HealthController {
  constructor(
    private health: HealthCheckService,
    private db: MongooseHealthIndicator,
    private api: HttpHealthIndicator,
  ) {}

  @Get()
  @HealthCheck()
  check() {
    return this.health.check([
      () => this.db.pingCheck('database'),
      () => this.api.pingCheck('plaid-api', 'https://production.plaid.com/categories/get'),
      () => this.api.pingCheck('quickbooks-api', 'https://sandbox-quickbooks.api.intuit.com/v3/health'),
    ]);
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] The `/health` endpoint returns a detailed status of all internal and external dependencies.
- [ ] A failure in MongoDB Atlas correctly triggers a `503 Service Unavailable` status code.
- [ ] Third-party API pings (Plaid/QuickBooks) are lightweight and do not exhaust rate limits.
- [ ] High-priority alerts are successfully delivered to the team when a service is "down."

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 Endpoint Protection: While the health status is useful, the detailed error messages should be protected. Use a Secret Header or IP Whitelisting so only your monitoring tools (and not the public) can see the full diagnostic data.
 Rate Limiting: Ensure the health check itself doesn't become a vector for a DoS attack; limit pings to authorized monitoring IPs.
 Privacy (PIPEDA): No user-specific data or PII should ever be returned in a health check response; it is strictly for infrastructure status.
`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 165:  Issue 165: Expand Sentry Monitoring for Web (React)`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Description
This issue involves integrating Sentry SDK into the React frontend to capture JavaScript runtime errors, API failures, and performance bottlenecks. Unlike mobile, web monitoring needs to handle Source Maps (to see original code instead of minified production code) and User Feedback loops.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Implementation Strategy

 1. NestJS (The Sentry Relay & Security)
The backend doesn't just track its own errors; it provides context for the frontend.
 Environment Configuration: Ensure the `SENTRY_DSN` is securely provided to the React build via environment variables.
 Trace Propagation: Enable Distributed Tracing. When React makes a request to NestJS, Sentry attaches a `sentry-trace` header. This allows you to see the entire journey of a failed request from the browser through the API to the Database.
 Release Management: Use Sentry's CLI in your GitHub Actions (Issue 182) to notify Sentry every time a new version of the API and Web app is deployed.

 2. React Web (The Error Boundary & Session Replay)
This is where the heavy lifting happens for the web.
 Error Boundaries: Wrap your main layout in a `Sentry.ErrorBoundary`. If a specific component crashes (e.g., the Analytics Chart), it won't crash the entire dashboard; instead, it shows a "Fallback UI" (Glassmorphism style) and reports the error.
 Session Replay: Enable Sentry Replay. This records a "video-like" recreation of the user's session (masking private data) so you can see exactly what the user clicked before the crash.
 Source Map Uploads: Configure your build tool (Vite or Webpack) to upload `.map` files to Sentry. This ensures that when an error happens in `main.abc123.js`, Sentry shows you the exact line in `InventoryPage.tsx`.



 3. Flutter Mobile (Cross-Platform Consistency)
While this issue focuses on Web, we must ensure parity:
 Tagging: Both platforms must use consistent tags like `user_id`, `org_id` (Issue 174), and `app_version`.
 Unified Alerting: Set up a single Slack or Discord channel where both Flutter and React errors are routed, so your "DevOps" view is centralized.

---

 ≡ƒ¢á The "Bug-to-Fix" Workflow



1.  Event: A user in Surrey tries to upload a corrupted Excel file (Issue 141) on the React Dashboard.
2.  Capture: React catches the `TypeError`. Sentry captures the browser version, the user's ID, and a Session Replay of the last 30 seconds.
3.  Tracing: Because of Distributed Tracing, Sentry shows that the error was actually caused by a `500 Internal Server Error` in NestJS due to a database timeout.
4.  Alert: You receive a notification. You see the exact line of code in both the Frontend and Backend.
5.  Resolution: You fix the bug, deploy, and Sentry automatically marks the issue as "Resolved in Version 1.2.0."

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (AC)
- [ ] Zero Performance Impact: Sentry must load asynchronously to ensure it doesn't slow down the initial React page load.
- [ ] Privacy Compliance: All "Sensitive" fields (Passwords, Credit Cards, PII) must be blacklisted from Session Replays.
- [ ] Breadcrumbs: Sentry must record the last 20 console logs and UI clicks leading up to an error.
- [ ] User Feedback UI: If a fatal error occurs, a Sentry-powered popup should ask the user: "What happened?" and send that report directly to the ticket.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 164: Issue #164: Develop Contextual Help System (Tooltips)`n`n[Description]`n ≡ƒô¥ Description

```dart
// lib/ui/widgets/shared/info_tooltip.dart

class InfoTooltip extends StatelessWidget {
  final String termKey;
  final Widget child;

  const InfoTooltip({required this.termKey, required this.child});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    final definition = context.read<HelpCubit>().getDefinition(termKey);

    return JustTheTooltip(
      content: Padding(
        padding: const EdgeInsets.all(12.0),
        child: Text(
          definition,
          style: TextStyle(color: Colors.white, fontSize: 13),
        ),
      ),
      backgroundColor: Colors.black.withOpacity(0.85),
      child: Row(
        mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min,
        children: [
          child,
          SizedBox(width: 4),
          Icon(Icons.info_outline, size: 16, color: Colors.blueAccent),
        ],
      ),
    );
  }
}
```

```typescript
    distanceKm: number;
  };
}
```

`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 159: Issue 159: Implement "Quiet Hours" & Frequency Capping`n`n[Description]`n

 ≡ƒô¥ Description
This issue involves developing the server-side logic in NestJS to enforce "Quiet Hours" and "Frequency Capping". The system must intercept all outgoing non-transactional notifications and check them against the user's local timezone settings and daily alert count to ensure we are not intruding during rest periods or overwhelming the user.

 ≡ƒæñ User Story
As a Restaurant Manager in Coquitlam, I want the app to stop sending me market alerts after 10:00 PM and limit the total number of "New Vendor" or "Price Drop" alerts to 5 per day, so I can focus on my operations without constant digital distractions.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. Timezone-Aware Delivery Logic
 User Context: Fetch the user's `timezone` (e.g., `America/Vancouver`) and `quietHours` (from Issue 158) from the MongoDB `UserPreferences` collection.
 Intercept Logic: Use a NestJS `Interceptor` or a dedicated `NotificationGuard` service to compare the `CurrentTime` (in user's local zone) against their `QuietWindow`.
 Priority Levels:  High Priority: (e.g., New Direct Messages, Order Confirmations) bypass Quiet Hours.
     Low Priority: (e.g., Price Drops, Recommendations) are suppressed or queued.

 2. Daily Frequency Capping
 Counter Implementation: Use Redis to maintain a rolling 24-hour counter for each user: `notification_count:{userId}:{category}`.
 Thresholds: Set a global default (e.g., max 5 Market Alerts/day) that can be overridden in user settings.
 Logic: If `count >= threshold`, the notification is dropped or converted into a "Weekly Digest" entry.

 3. Notification Queuing (Delayed Delivery)
 BullMQ Integration: Notifications blocked by Quiet Hours should be moved to a "Delayed" queue.
 Batch Release: Once the user's Quiet Hour ends (e.g., 8:00 AM local time), the system should release the queued notifications as a single "Morning Catch-up" summary.

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Logic & Architecture

The system acts as a smart gateway, filtering alerts based on time and volume before they reach the FCM provider.

```typescript
// backend/src/notifications/delivery-guard.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class DeliveryGuardService {
  constructor(private readonly redis: Redis) {}

  async shouldDeliver(userId: string, category: NotificationCategory): Promise<boolean> {
    const prefs = await this.userPrefsModel.findOne({ userId });
    
    // 1. Check Quiet Hours
    if (this.isWithinQuietHours(prefs)) {
      if (category !== 'TRANSACTIONAL') return false; 
    }

    // 2. Check Frequency Cap (Redis-based)
    const dailyKey = `notify_cap:${userId}:${category}:${new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0]}`;
    const currentCount = await this.redis.incr(dailyKey);
    await this.redis.expire(dailyKey, 86400); // 24 hours

    if (currentCount > prefs.maxDailyAlerts) {
      return false;
    }

    return true;
  }

  private isWithinQuietHours(prefs: any): boolean {
    const userTime = moment().tz(prefs.timezone);
    const now = userTime.format('HH:mm');
    return now >= prefs.quietStart || now <= prefs.quietEnd;
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] Non-transactional notifications are successfully blocked during the user's defined Quiet Hours.
- [ ] The system correctly identifies the user's local time using their stored timezone.
- [ ] Users do not receive more than the "Max Daily Alerts" allowed for non-critical categories.
- [ ] Transactional messages (Direct Messages) bypass these filters to ensure business continuity.

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 Data Integrity: Ensure the Redis counters are accurate and handle edge cases like daylight savings changes in Canada (PDT/PST).
 Performance: The lookup and increment in Redis must happen in `<10ms` to avoid delaying the notification pipeline.
 CRA/Legal: Ensure that legal notices or "Terms of Service" updates bypass all filters as they are legally required communications.

≡ƒô¥ Description

```typescript
// src/modules/orgs/org-permissions.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class OrgPermissionsService {
  async canPerformAction(userId: string, orgId: string, action: string): Promise<boolean> {
    const membership = await this.membershipModel.findOne({ userId, orgId });
    
    if (!membership) return false;

    // Logic for Surrey-specific fiscal limits
    if (action === 'APPROVE_PAYMENT' && membership.role === 'BUYER') {
      const dailyTotal = await this.getDailySpend(userId);
      return dailyTotal < 1000; // Buyers capped at $1k without Manager override
    }

    return this.roleRegistry.hasPermission(membership.role, action);
  }
}
```

A premium, glass-morphic drawer component that allows users to swap professional contexts.

```dart
        ...userOrgs.map((org) => OrgTile(
          title: org.name,
          isSelected: currentOrgId == org.id,
          onTap: () => _contextController.switchTo(org.id),
        )),
        AddOrgButton(),
      ],
    ),
  );
}
```

Develop a logistics tracking module in Flutter and NestJS. This system must allow sellers (or third-party couriers) to share their live location during a delivery, provide "ETA" updates via WebSockets (130), and finalize the transaction using a "Secure Handshake." The handshake requires the buyer to scan a generated QR code or the seller to upload a "Proof of Delivery" photo via the Media Service (139).

 Scope of Work
1.  Background Tracking Service: Implement `flutter_background_geolocation` to track the delivery vehicle's coordinates with minimal battery drain.
2.  Live Map Overlay: Build a frosted-glass map component in the Product Detail (143) using Google Maps API to show the real-time "Last-Mile" progress.
3.  ETA Calculation Engine: A NestJS service that uses the Google Distance Matrix API to provide dynamic arrival times based on Highway 1 traffic.
4.  Secure QR Handshake: Generate a unique, time-sensitive QR code on the BuyerΓÇÖs app that the Seller must scan to trigger the Payment Release (147).
5.  Visual PoD (Proof of Delivery): Allow for a "Photo-First" delivery option where the seller captures the item at the drop-off point, automatically tagged with GPS Metadata and stored in the Private Vault (139).

---

 Conceptual Overview: The Delivery-to-Settlement Loop
The logistics module bridges the gap between the digital agreement and the physical exchange, ensuring the Stripe Escrow (147) is only settled upon verified arrival.



---

 Implementation Strategy (NestJS / Flutter)

 1. The Delivery State Machine (NestJS)
Managing the transition from "In Transit" to "Finalized" with high data integrity.

```typescript
// src/modules/logistics/delivery-tracker.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class DeliveryTrackerService {
  async updateLocation(deliveryId: string, lat: number, lng: number) {
    // 1. Update the 'Live' location in Redis for high-speed retrieval
    await this.redis.set(`delivery:loc:${deliveryId}`, JSON.stringify({ lat, lng }), 'EX', 300);

    // 2. Broadcast to the Buyer via WebSocket (130)
    this.socketGateway.to(`delivery_${deliveryId}`).emit('location_update', { lat, lng });
    
    // 3. (Optional) Update the 'Route History' in MongoDB for Issue 129
    await this.deliveryModel.updateOne({ _id: deliveryId }, { $push: { path: [lat, lng] } });
  }
}
```

 2. The Flutter "PoD" Camera Interface
Ensuring the delivery photo is high-quality and contains the necessary verification metadata.

```dart
// lib/ui/logistics/proof_of_delivery_view.dart

Future<void> captureProof() async {
  final XFile? image = await _picker.pickImage(source: ImageSource.camera);
  if (image != null) {
    // 1. Overlay a Glassmorphism 'Timestamp & GPS' Watermark
    final processedImage = await _mediaService.watermark(image);
    
    // 2. Upload to the Private S3 Bucket (139)
    final fileUrl = await _api.uploadPoD(processedImage);

    // 3. Close the Transaction & Release Funds (147)
    await _api.confirmDelivery(deliveryId, proofUrl: fileUrl);
  }
}
```

`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 154: Issue 154: Voice-to-Action Integration (Gemini Live/Whisper)`n`n[Description]`n

 Description
Implement a high-performance voice interface in the Flutter app. This module must capture ambient audio, convert it to text using Whisper, and then use Gemini's Natural Language Understanding (NLU) to map that text to specific platform actions (e.g., "Post a new product," "Check my messages," or "Generate an invoice").
```typescript
// src/modules/ai/voice-intent.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class VoiceIntentService {
  async parseIntent(transcript: string) {
    const prompt = `Convert this voice command into a JSON action: "${transcript}".
                   Options: { action: "CREATE_ASSET", data: { title, price } } 
                   or { action: "SEND_MESSAGE", data: { body, recipientId } }`;

    const result = await this.gemini.generateContent(prompt);
    return JSON.parse(result.response.text());
  }
}
```

`n`n[Comments]`n- m13v: shipped a voice-to-action pipeline for a macOS agent and learned a few things about the STT-to-intent flow:

the 1.5 second latency target is tight but achievable. the bottleneck is almost always the transcription step, not the intent parsing. with OpenAI Whisper API you are looking at 500ms-1s for a 5-second utterance. if you can do local transcription (whisper.cpp or WhisperKit on Apple Silicon), you cut that to 200-400ms and remove the network dependency entirely.

for the Gemini intent parser, sending raw transcript text works but you get much better accuracy by including context about what screen the user is on. we pass a brief description of the current UI state alongside the transcript, so the model knows "the user said 'post it' while looking at a product form" vs just "the user said post it."

for multi-language (English/Farsi/Mandarin), whisper handles all three well but you need to set the language parameter explicitly rather than relying on auto-detect. auto-detect adds 200-300ms of latency and sometimes gets confused when users code-switch mid-sentence, which happens a lot in multilingual business contexts.

the confirmation toast is essential. we initially shipped without it and users were nervous about the AI executing actions they did not intend. even a 1-second confirmation window with cancel makes the whole experience feel safer.`n- m13v: our voice-to-action transcription service with on-device Whisper, language configuration, and the chunked processing pipeline: https://github.com/m13v/fazm/blob/main/Desktop/Sources/TranscriptionService.swift

and the tool execution layer that maps intents to actions: https://github.com/m13v/fazm/blob/main/Desktop/Sources/Providers/ChatToolExecutor.swift`n- ahmad1368: > our voice-to-action transcription service with on-device Whisper, language configuration, and the chunked processing pipeline: https://github.com/m13v/fazm/blob/main/Desktop/Sources/TranscriptionService.swift
> 
> and the tool execution layer that maps intents to actions: https://github.com/m13v/fazm/blob/main/Desktop/Sources/Providers/ChatToolExecutor.swift

Hi,
Thanks a lot for the insights and for sharing the links. IΓÇÖll go through the TranscriptionService and ChatToolExecutor implementationsΓÇöespecially the onΓÇædevice Whisper setup, language configuration, and the chunked pipeline. IΓÇÖll reach out if I have any questions or suggestions.`n- m13v: glad it's helpful! one thing worth noting when you dig into the TranscriptionService: the chunked processing pipeline is key for keeping latency low on longer dictation. we found that processing in ~3 second chunks with overlap gives the best balance between accuracy and responsiveness.

 Implementation Strategy (NestJS / Flutter)

 1. The Currency Conversion Utility (NestJS)
Handling the "Spread" and "Transaction Fees" before the Stripe Intent (147) is generated.

```typescript
// src/modules/finance/currency-converter.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class CurrencyConverterService {
  async convert(amount: number, from: string, to: string) {
    const rate = await this.redis.get(`rate:${from}:${to}`);
    // Apply a 0.5% platform buffer for exchange rate volatility (Slippage)
    const convertedAmount = (amount  parseFloat(rate))  1.005;
    
    return {
      original: amount,
      converted: Math.round(convertedAmount  100) / 100,
      rate: parseFloat(rate),
    };
  }
}
```

 2. The Flutter "Price Bubble" Widget
A dynamic way to show users exactly what they will pay in their home currency.

```dart
// lib/ui/catalog/widgets/multi_currency_price.dart

  return Column(
    crossAxisAlignment: CrossAxisAlignment.start,
    children: [
      Text("\$${asset.price} ${asset.currency}", style: primaryStyle),
      if (userCurrency != asset.currency)
        Text(
          "Γëê ${_calculateLocal(asset.price)} $userCurrency",
          style: TextStyle(color: Colors.white.withOpacity(0.5), fontSize: 12),
        ),
    ],
  );
}
```

Using OpenAI's `gpt-4o` to extract structured data from unstructured chat.

```typescript
// src/modules/ai/negotiation-copilot.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class NegotiationCopilotService {
  async extractAgreedTerms(messages: ChatMessage[]) {
    const prompt = `Analyze this B2B chat from a Surrey marketplace. 
                   Identify: Price, Quantity, and Pickup Location. 
                   Context: ${JSON.stringify(messages)}`;

    const response = await this.openai.chat.completions.create({
      model: "gpt-4o",
      messages: [{ role: "system", content: prompt }],
      response_format: { type: "json_object" }
    });

    return JSON.parse(response.choices[0].message.content);
  }
}
```

```dart
          label: text,
```

 Conceptual Overview: The Tiered Access Model

---

 Implementation Strategy (NestJS / Flutter)

 1. The Feature-Gate Middleware (NestJS)
A clean way to protect premium logic (like the AI Matching Engine 145).

```typescript
// src/common/guards/feature-gate.guard.ts

@Injectable()
export class FeatureGateGuard implements CanActivate {
  constructor(private reflector: Reflector) {}

  canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): boolean {
    const requiredTier = this.reflector.get<string>('minTier', context.getHandler());
    const { user } = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();

    const tierWeights = { 'STARTER': 1, 'PRO': 2, 'ENTERPRISE': 3 };
    
    if (tierWeights[user.plan] < tierWeights[requiredTier]) {
      throw new ForbiddenException('Upgrade to ' + requiredTier + ' to unlock this feature.');
    }
    return true;
  }
}
```

 2. The Flutter "Upgrade" Prompt
Using a frosted-glass overlay to encourage users to move to the Pro tier.

```dart
// lib/ui/shared/premium_feature_overlay.dart

  return Stack(
    children: [
      content, // The blurred background feature
      BackdropFilter(
        filter: ImageFilter.blur(sigmaX: 5, sigmaY: 5),
        child: GlassUpgradeCard(
          title: "Unlock Market Intelligence",
          onUpgrade: () => Navigator.pushNamed(context, '/pricing'),
        ),
      ),
    ],
  );
}
```

---

 Acceptance Criteria
 [ ] Automated Billing: Subscriptions automatically renew and trigger FCM Notifications (137) 3 days before the charge.
 [ ] Grace Periods: Users have a 48-hour "Grace Period" if a payment fails before their Verified Status (144) is impacted.
 [ ] Seamless Upgrade: Moving from "Starter" to "Pro" instantly unlocks features in the Glassmorphism UI (136) without a logout/login cycle.
 [ ] Privacy Integrity: No billing history or card details are stored in the platform's database; all data is managed via the Stripe Customer Vault.

 Technical Constraints
 Platform: NestJS / Stripe Billing / Flutter.
 Webhooks: Must handle `customer.subscription.updated` and `invoice.payment_failed` events with 100% reliability.
 Taxation: Ensure GST/PST (146) is correctly applied to the subscription fee based on the business's BC Address.

 Implementation Strategy (NestJS / Flutter)

 1. The Aggregation Logic (NestJS)
Using MongoDB's `$facet` and `$bucket` operators to group transactional data without killing database performance.

```typescript
// src/modules/analytics/market-trends.service.ts

async getPriceTrends(categoryId: string, days: number = 30) {
  return this.transactionModel.aggregate([
    { $match: { categoryId, status: 'SUCCESS', createdAt: { $gte: subDays(new Date(), days) } } },
    {
      $group: {
        _id: { $dateToString: { format: "%Y-%m-%d", date: "$createdAt" } },
        avgPrice: { $avg: "$amount" },
        count: { $sum: 1 }
      }
    },
    { $sort: { "_id": 1 } }
  ]);
}
```

```typescript
@Post('create-intent')
@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard)
async createIntent(@Body() dto: CreatePaymentDto) {
  const invoice = await this.invoiceService.getById(dto.invoiceId);
  
  // Create the intent with Stripe
  const intent = await this.stripe.paymentIntents.create({
    amount: Math.round(invoice.total  100), // Stripe uses cents
    currency: 'cad',
    metadata: { invoiceId: invoice.id, buyerId: invoice.buyerId },
    automatic_payment_methods: { enabled: true },
  });

  return { clientSecret: intent.client_secret };
}
```

```dart
      merchantDisplayName: 'Lower Mainland B2B Network',
      style: ThemeMode.dark, // Matches the premium look
      appearance: PaymentSheetAppearance(
        colors: PaymentSheetAppearanceColors(primary: Colors.blueAccent),
```

 Implementation Strategy (NestJS / Puppeteer)

 1. The Tax Logic (BC Specific)
Ensuring compliance with the CRA (Canada Revenue Agency) requirements for small and medium businesses in the Lower Mainland.

```typescript
// src/modules/finance/tax-calculator.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class TaxCalculatorService {
  private readonly GST_RATE = 0.05;
  private readonly PST_RATE = 0.07;

  calculateTotal(subtotal: number, applyPST: boolean) {
    const gst = subtotal  this.GST_RATE;
    const pst = applyPST ? subtotal  this.PST_RATE : 0;
    
    return {
      subtotal,
      gst,
      pst,
      total: subtotal + gst + pst,
    };
  }
}
```

 2. Generating the Branded PDF
Using HTML/Handlebars templates to maintain the "Modern B2B" visual identity.

```typescript
// src/modules/finance/invoice-generator.service.ts

async generateInvoicePdf(data: InvoiceData): Promise<string> {
  const html = this.templateEngine.compile('invoice-template', data);
  
  // Launch headless browser for high-fidelity rendering
  const browser = await puppeteer.launch();
  const page = await browser.newPage();
  await page.setContent(html);
  
  const buffer = await page.pdf({ format: 'A4' });
  await browser.close();

  // Upload to the Media Service (139) and return a Signed URL
  return this.storageService.uploadPrivateFile(`invoices/${data.id}.pdf`, buffer);
}
```

 Implementation Strategy (NestJS / Python Microservice)

 1. Generating Embeddings (NestJS)
Transforming unstructured business descriptions into searchable mathematical vectors.

```typescript
// src/modules/matching/embedding.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class EmbeddingService {
  async generateEmbedding(text: string): Promise<number[]> {
    const response = await this.openai.embeddings.create({
      model: 'text-embedding-3-small',
      input: text,
    });
    return response.data[0].embedding;
  }
}
```

 2. The Vector Search Query (MongoDB)
Performing a "K-Nearest Neighbors" search to find the best regional matches.

```typescript
// src/modules/matching/matching.service.ts

async findTopMatches(requestEmbedding: number[], location: number[]) {
  return this.assetModel.aggregate([
    {
      $vectorSearch: {
        index: "vector_index",
        path: "embedding",
        queryVector: requestEmbedding,
        numCandidates: 100,
        limit: 10
      }
    },
    {
      $addFields: {
        geoScore: { $meta: "searchScore" }, // Placeholder for geo-weighting
      }
    }
  ]);
}
```

---

 Acceptance Criteria

A decorator to protect high-value actions (like starting a $10,000+ negotiation).

```typescript
// src/common/guards/verified-user.guard.ts

@Injectable()
export class VerifiedUserGuard implements CanActivate {
  canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): boolean {
    const { user } = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();
    // Only allow users with a 'VERIFIED' status from Issue 120
    if (user.verificationStatus !== 'VERIFIED') {
      throw new ForbiddenException('Identity verification required for this action.');
    }
    return true;
  }
}
```

```dart
  // 2. Upload Business License
  final bizUrl = await _storage.uploadBusinessDoc(licenseFile);

  // 3. Update status to PENDING in MongoDB
  await _api.updateVerificationStatus({
    'status': 'PENDING',
    'documents': [idUrl, bizUrl],
    'submittedAt': DateTime.now().toIso8601String(),
  });
  
  _uiState.setLoading(false);
  _navigation.goToSuccessScreen();
}
```

 ≡ƒô¥ Description
To accelerate vendor onboarding and product catalog management, we need to implement a Bulk Import System. This feature will allow vendors to upload hundreds of products at once using standard `.csv` or `.xlsx` templates. The system must handle data validation, coordinate mapping for location-based services, and provide a detailed error report for failed rows.

 ≡ƒæñ User Story
As a Large-Scale Vendor, I want to upload my entire inventory using an Excel spreadsheet so that I don't have to manually enter each product into the system one by one.

---

 ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

 1. Multi-Format Parsing (NestJS)
 Libraries: Use `multer` for file uploads and `exceljs` or `xlsx` for parsing spreadsheet data.
 Buffer Processing: Process files in-memory (for small files) or stream to disk (for large files > 10MB) to prevent heap memory exhaustion.
 Worker Threads: Use BullMQ or NestJS `Task Scheduling` to process large imports in the background to avoid blocking the main event loop.

 2. Validation & Transformation Pipeline
 Schema Validation: Use `class-validator` and `Joi` to ensure required fields (Name, SKU, Price, Category) are present and correctly formatted.
 Data Cleaning: Trim whitespace, normalize currency formats (CAD), and validate `Category IDs` against our master list.
 Geo-Coding: If the file contains specific branch addresses, trigger the Google Maps Geocoding API to generate `lat/lng` coordinates for the Matching Engine (Issue 154).

 3. Error Handling & Feedback UI (Flutter)
 Partial Success Logic: If 95 out of 100 rows are valid, import the 95 and return a "Download Error Log" for the remaining 5.
 Real-time Progress: Implement a WebSocket (Socket.io) or SSE listener in Flutter to show a progress bar during the import.
 Preview Step: Show a "Data Preview" table in Flutter before final submission to catch mapping errors.

---

 ≡ƒ¢á Data Ingestion Workflow

The import pipeline ensures data integrity by running files through a multi-stage validation "sieve."



```typescript
// backend/src/catalog/import.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class ImportService {
  async processBulkUpload(file: Express.Multer.File, vendorId: string) {
    const workbook = new ExcelJS.Workbook();
    await workbook.xlsx.load(file.buffer);
    const worksheet = workbook.getWorksheet(1);

    const products = [];
    const errors = [];

    worksheet.eachRow((row, rowNumber) => {
      if (rowNumber === 1) return; // Skip Header

      const rowData = {
        name: row.getCell(1).value,
        sku: row.getCell(2).value,
        price: parseFloat(row.getCell(3).value.toString()),
      };

      // Simple Validation Logic
      if (isNaN(rowData.price)) {
        errors.push({ row: rowNumber, error: "Invalid Price Format" });
      } else {
        products.push({ ...rowData, vendorId });
      }
    });

    // Batch Insert for Performance
    if (products.length > 0) {
      await this.productModel.insertMany(products);
    }

    return { importedCount: products.length, errors };
  }
}
```

---

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] Users can download a `.csv` template directly from the app.
- [ ] The system supports both `.csv` and `.xlsx` file formats.
- [ ] Duplicate SKUs for the same vendor are flagged as errors rather than creating duplicates.
- [ ] The Flutter UI displays a "Success" summary (e.g., "450 items imported, 12 failed").
- [ ] Large files (5,000+ rows) do not crash the backend or time out the client.

 ≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
 File Sanitization: Use `file-type` to verify magic bytes; ensure users cannot upload malicious executables disguised as CSVs.
 Rate Limiting: Limit bulk imports to 3 per hour per vendor to prevent database hammering.
 Storage Privacy: Automatically delete temporary upload files from the server immediately after processing.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 141: Issue 141: Develop Bulk Import Feature (CSV/Excel)`n`n[Description]`n

```typescript
// backend/src/catalog/import.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class ImportService {
  async processBulkUpload(file: Express.Multer.File, vendorId: string) {
    const workbook = new ExcelJS.Workbook();
    await workbook.xlsx.load(file.buffer);
    const worksheet = workbook.getWorksheet(1);

    const products = [];
    const errors = [];

    worksheet.eachRow((row, rowNumber) => {
      if (rowNumber === 1) return; // Skip Header

      const rowData = {
        name: row.getCell(1).value,
        sku: row.getCell(2).value,
        price: parseFloat(row.getCell(3).value.toString()),
      };

      // Simple Validation Logic
      if (isNaN(rowData.price)) {
        errors.push({ row: rowNumber, error: "Invalid Price Format" });
      } else {
        products.push({ ...rowData, vendorId });
      }
    });

    // Batch Insert for Performance
    if (products.length > 0) {
      await this.productModel.insertMany(products);
    }

    return { importedCount: products.length, errors };
  }
}
```

 Γ£à Acceptance Criteria

```dart
Widget buildSpecFields(String category) {
  final specs = CategoryRegistry.getSpecsFor(category); // Linked to Issue 104
  return Column(
    children: specs.map((spec) {
      return GlassTextField(
        label: spec.label,
        onSaved: (val) => _currentAsset.specs[spec.key] = val,
        validator: spec.isRequired ? Validators.required : null,
      );

  );
```

 2. The Asynchronous Media Manager
Handling the S3/GCS Upload (139) without blocking the user from typing.

```dart
// lib/controllers/upload_controller.dart

Future<void> handleImageSelection(List<AssetEntity> assets) async {
  for (var asset in assets) {
    // 1. Show local thumbnail immediately in the Glassmorphism UI (136)
    _uiState.addPlaceholder(asset);

    // 2. Request Pre-signed URL from NestJS (139)
    final uploadUrl = await _api.getUploadUrl(asset.name);

    // 3. Upload in background using Dio with progress tracking
    await _transferService.uploadFile(
      url: uploadUrl,
      file: await asset.file,
      onProgress: (p) => _uiState.updateProgress(asset.id, p),
    );
  }
}
```

`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 139: Issue #139: Unified Image Optimization & CDN Delivery`n`n[Description]`n
≡ƒô¥ Description
This issue addresses the "Media Fragmentation" problem. We are building a centralized processing service that intercepts every upload (from React or Flutter), generates optimized variants (WebP/JPEG), and serves them through a global Content Delivery Network (CDN). This reduces latency for users in different parts of BC and optimizes data usage.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Implementation Strategy
1. NestJS (The Image Processor)
The backend becomes a factory for media assets.

Processing Engine: Use the sharp library. It is extremely fast and low-memory.

The Triple-Stream Logic: For every upload to S3, generate three versions:

Thumbnail (200x200): Optimized for the Flutter "List View" and React "Search Results."

Standard (800x800): For "Product Details" pages.

Original: Untouched, stored in a "Cold" S3 tier for legal/archival purposes.

Format Conversion: Automatically convert all compatible images to WebP, which offers ~30% better compression than JPEG without losing quality.

2. React Web (Progressive Desktop Experience)
Web users expect "Instant" feeling pages even with hundreds of products.

Blur-up Technique: Use the Thumbnail (200x200) as a blurred placeholder while the Standard image loads in the background. This prevents "Layout Shift."

srcset Support: Use the <picture> tag or srcset attribute so the browser automatically chooses the best resolution based on the user's monitor (e.g., 2x for Retina displays).

Lazy Loading: Ensure images below the "fold" are only downloaded when the user scrolls down to them.

3. Flutter Mobile (Memory-Efficient Rendering)
Mobile performance is limited by RAM. We must prevent "Image Overdraw."

CachedNetworkImage: Use the cached_network_image package.

MemCache Dimensions: Explicitly set memCacheHeight and memCacheWidth in Flutter. This tells the phone to decode the image at the size itΓÇÖs displayed, saving massive amounts of RAM and preventing crashes on older Android devices.

CDN Switching: Detect the user's connection (Wi-Fi vs. LTE). If on a poor connection, the app should fetch the Thumbnail even for the detail view to save data.

≡ƒ¢á The Optimization Workflow
Upload: A vendor uploads a 5MB "Product Hero" image via React.

Process: NestJS receives it, sharp generates the WebP versions, and uploads all 3 files to AWS S3.

Distribute: The files are cached on AWS CloudFront edge locations (e.g., a server in Seattle/Vancouver).

Consume (Web): The React dashboard displays the WebP version with a blur-up effect.

Consume (Mobile): The Flutter app fetches the small Thumbnail for the catalog grid, ensuring smooth 60FPS scrolling.

Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (AC)
[ ] Data Reduction: The total payload of the "Product Listing" page in React must be at least 60% smaller than the raw original files.

[ ] WebP Adoption: Chrome, Edge, and Android/iOS browsers must receive WebP files, with a JPEG fallback for older Safari versions.

[ ] Scroll Performance: The Flutter app must maintain a consistent 60FPS while scrolling through a list of 100+ images.

[ ] S3 Security: Direct access to the S3 bucket is blocked; all images must be accessed via a secure CDN URL.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 138: Issue #138: Define Product & Service Entity Model`n`n[Description]`n

TypeScript
// src/modules/notifications/push-dispatcher.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class PushDispatcherService {
  async sendMarketAlert(userId: string, payload: { title: string; body: string; data: any }) {
    const tokens = await this.tokenService.getTokensForUser(userId);
    
    if (tokens.length === 0) return;

    const message = {
      notification: { title: payload.title, body: payload.body },
      data: { ...payload.data, click_action: 'FLUTTER_NOTIFICATION_CLICK' },
      tokens: tokens,
    };

    const response = await admin.messaging().sendMulticast(message);
    this.handleCleanup(response, tokens); // Remove expired tokens
  }
}

// lib/services/notification_handler.dart

void handleNotificationTap(RemoteMessage message) {
  final String type = message.data['type'];
  final String targetId = message.data['id'];

  if (type == 'CHAT_MESSAGE') {
    // Navigate to the Glassmorphism Chat UI (#136)
    navigatorKey.currentState?.pushNamed('/chat', arguments: targetId);
  } else if (type == 'NEW_LEAD') {
    // Navigate to the Procurement Request (#126)
    navigatorKey.currentState?.pushNamed('/leads', arguments: targetId);
  }
}

class GlassBubble extends StatelessWidget {
  final Widget child;
  final bool isMe;

  const GlassBubble({required this.child, required this.isMe});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return Container(
      decoration: BoxDecoration(
        boxShadow: [
          BoxShadow(color: Colors.black.withOpacity(0.1), blurRadius: 10, spreadRadius: 1)
        ],
      ),
      child: ClipRRect(
        borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(20),
        child: BackdropFilter(
          filter: ImageFilter.blur(sigmaX: 15.0, sigmaY: 15.0),
          child: Container(
            padding: const EdgeInsets.all(12),
            decoration: BoxDecoration(
              color: isMe 
                  ? Colors.blue.withOpacity(0.15) 
                  : Colors.white.withOpacity(0.05),
              border: Border.all(
                color: Colors.white.withOpacity(0.1),
                width: 1.5,
              ),
              borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(20),
            ),
            child: child,
          ),
        ),
      ),
    );
  }
}
