The Matching Algorithm (Weighted Score)Implement a scoring system ($0.0$ to $1.0$) to determine if two transactions are identical:Exact Amount: (Weight: $0.5$) ΓÇö If amounts differ by even $0.01, itΓÇÖs likely not a match.Date Proximity: (Weight: $0.3$) ΓÇö Bank dates and accounting dates often differ by 1ΓÇô3 days. Manual "Un-merge" CapabilityState Management: If the system makes a mistake, the user must be able to "Split" the transactions back into two separate entries from the Flutter UI.
≡ƒ¢á Deduplication & Merging WorkflowThe logic ensures that the "Unified Feed" stays clean and accurate by consolidating overlapping data streams.TypeScript// backend/src/transactions/deduplication.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class DeduplicationService {
  
  async findPotentialMatches(newTx: Transaction, businessId: string) {
    // 1. Query candidates within a 5-day window and +/- 0% amount difference
    const candidates = await this.db.findCandidates(businessId, {
      amount: newTx.amount,
      dateRange: { start: subDays(newTx.date, 3), end: addDays(newTx.date, 3) }
    });

    for (const candidate of candidates) {
      const score = this.calculateSimilarity(newTx, candidate);
      
      // 2. Auto-merge if high confidence (> 0.92)
      if (score > 0.92) {
        return this.mergeTransactions(candidate, newTx);
      }
    }
  }

  private calculateSimilarity(t1: any, t2: any): number {
    let score = 0;
    if (t1.amount === t2.amount) score += 0.5;
    
    const dayDiff = Math.abs(differenceInDays(t1.date, t2.date));
    if (dayDiff <= 1) score += 0.3;
    else if (dayDiff <= 3) score += 0.15;

    const merchantScore = stringSimilarity.compareTwoStrings(t1.name, t2.name);
    score += (merchantScore * 0.2);

    return score;
  }
}

≡ƒöÉ Security & ComplianceData Integrity: Never delete the original raw data. If a merge occurs, store the raw payloads in a history field so the action can be reversed.PIPA Compliance: Ensure that personal notes from the accounting software aren't accidentally linked to a "Shared" bank transaction if the business has multiple users (Issue #49).Audit Trail: Every merge must be logged with a system_action type to distinguish between AI-driven merges and user-driven merges.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 68: Issue #69: Build "Connected Accounts" Management UI`n`n[Description]`nAhmed, we are at Issue #69. ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

Route: POST /v1/webhooks/plaid

Payload Parsing: Extract the item_id and webhook_code.

≡ƒ¢á Webhook Security & Processing Flow
The webhook serves as the "Trigger" that moves the system from a passive state to an active sync state.

TypeScript
// backend/src/webhooks/plaid-webhook.controller.ts

@Controller('v1/webhooks/plaid')
export class PlaidWebhookController {
  
  @Post()
  async handleWebhook(@Headers('plaid-verification') signature: string, @Body() body: any) {
    // 1. Verify Webhook (Required for Bank-Grade Security)
    const isValid = await this.plaidService.verifyWebhook(signature, body);
    if (!isValid) throw new UnauthorizedException('Invalid Webhook Signature');

    // 2. Route based on Webhook Code
    const { webhook_code, item_id } = body;

    if (webhook_code === 'DEFAULT_UPDATE') {
      // Add a high-priority sync job to the queue (Issue #67)
      await this.plaidSyncQueue.add('delta-sync', { itemId: item_id }, { priority: 1 });
    }

    if (webhook_code === 'TRANSACTIONS_REMOVED') {
      await this.transactionService.removePlaidTransactions(body.removed_transactions);
    }

    return { received: true };
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria

Issue #67: Develop Transaction Fetching Worker
≡ƒô¥ Description
This issue involves creating a robust Sync Worker using NestJS and BullMQ to handle the retrieval of transactions from the Plaid API. ≡ƒæñ User Story

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements
1. The Sync Strategy (Cursor-Based)
Storage: Add a plaidSyncCursor field to the Business schema. This string acts as a "bookmark" in the transaction timeline.

Initial Sync: If the cursor is null, fetch the maximum allowable history (typically 2 years, but we'll start with 90 days for performance).

Delta Sync: Use the stored cursor to fetch only the "Added," "Modified," or "Removed" transactions since the last sync.

2. BullMQ Integration
Queue Name: plaid-sync-queue.

Job Data: Pass the businessId and accessToken.

Retry Logic: Configure exponential backoff (e.g., retry 3 times) to handle temporary Plaid API timeouts or rate limits.

3. Data Transformation & Deduplication
Normalizer: Map Plaid's Transaction object (amount, date, merchant_name, category) to our MongoDB schema.

Idempotency: Use Plaid's transaction_id as a unique index in MongoDB to prevent duplicate records if the worker runs twice.

≡ƒ¢á Background Sync Architecture
The worker operates independently of the UI, ensuring that large data volumes don't slow down the mobile experience.

TypeScript
// backend/src/integrations/plaid/plaid-sync.processor.ts

@Processor('plaid-sync-queue')
export class PlaidSyncProcessor {
  @Process('sync-transactions')
  async handleSync(job: Job<{ businessId: string }>) {
    const { businessId } = job.data;
    const business = await this.businessService.findOne(businessId);
    
    // 1. Decrypt the Access Token (Issue #57)
    const accessToken = this.cryptoService.decrypt(
      business.integrations.plaid.encryptedAccessToken,
      business.integrations.plaid.iv
    );

    let cursor = business.plaidSyncCursor;
    let added: any[] = [];
    let hasMore = true;

    // 2. Iterate through Plaid pagination
    while (hasMore) {
      const response = await this.plaidClient.transactionsSync({
        access_token: accessToken,
        cursor: cursor,
      });

      added = added.concat(response.data.added);
      hasMore = response.data.has_more;
      cursor = response.data.next_cursor;
    }

    // 3. Bulk Save to MongoDB (Issue #50 Schema)
    await this.transactionService.bulkUpsert(businessId, added);

    // 4. Save the new Cursor for the next Delta Sync
    await this.businessService.updateCursor(businessId, cursor);
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
[ ] The PlaidSyncProcessor successfully fetches transactions from the Plaid Sandbox/Development environment.

[ ] Transactions are correctly saved to the Transactions collection with the proper businessId.

[ ] The plaidSyncCursor is updated in the Business document after a successful run.

[ ] The worker handles "Removed" transactions from Plaid by marking them as isDeleted (Issue #54).

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Memory Management: For businesses with thousands of transactions, process data in batches of 100 to avoid Node.js OOM (Out of Memory) errors.

Data Sovereignty: Ensure all fetched financial data is stored in the Canada Central region.

PIPA Compliance: Only fetch the accounts authorized by the user. If the user only linked their "Business Chequing," ensure the worker doesn't attempt to pull from a personal "Savings" account on the same Plaid Item.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 65: Issue #66: Public Token Exchange & Secure Storage`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Description

≡ƒ¢á Public Token Exchange Architecture
The exchange turns a one-time-use token into a long-term credential, secured by your server-side encryption keys.

TypeScript
// backend/src/integrations/plaid/plaid.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class PlaidService {
  
  async exchangeAndSaveToken(businessId: string, publicToken: string, metadata: any) {
    try {
      // 1. Trade public_token for access_token
      const response = await this.plaidClient.itemPublicTokenExchange({
        public_token: publicToken,
      });

      const { access_token, item_id } = response.data;

      // 2. Encrypt the access_token (Issue #57 logic)
      const { encryptedData, iv } = this.cryptoService.encrypt(access_token);

      // 3. Update the Business record in MongoDB Atlas
      await this.businessModel.findByIdAndUpdate(businessId, {
        $set: {
          'integrations.plaid': {
            itemId: item_id,
            encryptedAccessToken: encryptedData,
            iv: iv,
            institutionName: metadata.institution.name,
            status: 'CONNECTED',
            linkedAt: new Date(),
          }
        }
      });

      return { success: true };
    } catch (error) {
      this.logger.error('Plaid Exchange Failed', error);
      throw new InternalServerErrorException('Failed to link bank account');
    }
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
[ ] POST /v1/plaid/exchange-public-token successfully returns a 200 OK after a valid exchange.

[ ] The access_token is never stored in plain text in MongoDB Atlas.

[ ] The itemId is correctly associated with the businessId.

[ ] A record is created in the SyncLogs collection to track the initial connection event.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
GCM Authentication Tag: Store the authentication tag from the AES-256-GCM encryption to ensure the token hasn't been tampered with.

No Client-Side Storage: The access_token must never be sent back to the Flutter app. It stays strictly on the NestJS server.

PIPA Compliance: If the user "Disconnects" their bank (Issue #62), call the Plaid /item/remove endpoint to invalidate the token before deleting the local encrypted record.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 64: Issue #65: Integrate Plaid Link SDK in Flutter`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Description

Dart
// frontend/lib/modules/bank/services/plaid_service.dart

class PlaidIntegrationService {
  
  void openPlaidLink(String linkToken) {
    LinkTokenConfiguration configuration = LinkTokenConfiguration(
      token: linkToken,
    );

    PlaidLink.onSuccess.listen((Success success) {
      // 1. Capture the public_token
      String publicToken = success.publicToken;
      
      // 2. Send to NestJS backend (Issue #66)
      _sendPublicTokenToBackend(publicToken, success.metadata);
    });

    PlaidLink.onExit.listen((Exit exit) {
      if (exit.error != null) {
        print("Plaid Link Error: ${exit.error?.displayMessage}");
      }
    });

    PlaidLink.open(configuration: configuration);
  }
}

Route: POST /v1/plaid/create-link-token

The link_token generation is the first step in a three-way communication between your server, the mobile app, and Plaid's secure vault.

TypeScript
// backend/src/integrations/plaid/plaid.controller.ts

@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard)
@Controller('plaid')
export class PlaidController {
  constructor(private readonly plaidService: PlaidService) {}

  @Post('create-link-token')
  async createLinkToken(@Req() req) {
    const user = req.user; // Extracted from JWT
    
    const configs: LinkTokenCreateRequest = {
      user: { client_user_id: user.sub },
      client_name: 'Ahmad B2B Marketplace',
      products: [Products.Transactions],
      country_codes: [CountryCode.Ca],
      language: 'en',
      // Required for Android/iOS redirects
      redirect_uri: process.env.PLAID_REDIRECT_URI, 
    };

    try {
      const createTokenResponse = await this.plaidClient.linkTokenCreate(configs);
      return createTokenResponse.data;
    } catch (error) {
      throw new InternalServerErrorException('Could not create link token');
    }
  }
}

export class PlaidProvider {

    const configuration = new Configuration({
      basePath: this.getEnvironmentPath(),
      baseOptions: {
        headers: {
          'PLAID-CLIENT-ID': this.configService.get('PLAID_CLIENT_ID'),
          'PLAID-SECRET': this.configService.get('PLAID_SECRET'),
        },
      },
    });
    this.plaidClient = new PlaidApi(configuration);
  }

  private getEnvironmentPath() {
    return this.configService.get('NODE_ENV') === 'production' 
      ? PlaidEnvironments.production 
      : PlaidEnvironments.sandbox;
  }
}

≡ƒ¢á Webhook Communication Workflow
The Webhook Listener acts as a "Reactive Trigger," allowing external systems to push updates to your backend infrastructure immediately.

TypeScript
// backend/src/webhooks/webhooks.controller.ts

@Controller('webhooks')
export class WebhooksController {
  
  @Post('plaid')
  async handlePlaidWebhook(
    @Headers('x-plaid-signature') signature: string,
    @Body() payload: any,
    @Req() req: RequestWithRawBody
  ) {
    // 1. Verify that this actually came from Plaid
    const isValid = this.verifyService.verifyPlaid(signature, req.rawBody);
    if (!isValid) throw new UnauthorizedException('Invalid Signature');

    // 2. Dispatch the event to the background worker
    switch (payload.webhook_code) {
      case 'DEFAULT_UPDATE':
        await this.syncQueue.add('fetch-new-transactions', { 
          itemId: payload.item_id 
        });
        break;
      case 'ITEM_ERROR':
        await this.notificationService.alertUser(payload.item_id);
        break;
    }

    return { received: true };
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
[ ] A public endpoint is available to receive POST requests from external providers.

[ ] Signature verification successfully blocks unauthorized or spoofed requests.

[ ] Incoming "New Transaction" webhooks from Plaid successfully trigger a BullMQ job to fetch data.

[ ] Changes made directly in the QuickBooks UI are reflected in our app's "Company Info" within minutes.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Secret Rotation: Store webhook secrets in AWS Secrets Manager and implement a way to rotate them without downtime.

Rate Limiting: Implement a specific rate-limiter for the webhook route to prevent DoS (Denial of Service) attacks from malicious actors.

PIPA Compliance: Webhook payloads usually contain IDs, not raw PII. Ensure that logs of these payloads are scrubbed of any sensitive information before being stored in Sentry or CloudWatch.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 59: Issue #60: Develop "Export to Accounting" Backend Service`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ DescriptionThis issue involves creating a robust Sync Worker in NestJS that pushes mapped transactions (from Issue #59) to the external Accounting APIs (QBO/Xero). ≡ƒ¢á Export-to-Accounting ArchitectureThe service acts as a "Translator" and "Dispatcher," converting our Unified Schema into the specific XML/JSON formats required by accounting giants.TypeScript// backend/src/integrations/export/export-worker.processor.ts

@Processor('accounting-export')
export class ExportWorker {
  
  @Process('push-transactions')
  async handleExport(job: Job<{ businessId: string, transactionIds: string[] }>) {
    const { businessId, transactionIds } = job.data;
    
    // 1. Get valid tokens (from Issue #57)
    const token = await this.tokenManager.getValidAccessToken(businessId);
    
    // 2. Fetch and format mapped transactions
    const transactions = await this.db.getMappedTransactions(transactionIds);
    
    for (const tx of transactions) {
      try {
        // 3. Dispatch to specific provider
        const externalId = await this.providerService.push(tx, token);
        
        // 4. Mark as 'SYNCED' and store the external reference
        await this.db.markAsSynced(tx._id, externalId);
      } catch (error) {
        // Log individual failures without stopping the entire batch
        this.logger.error(`Failed to push transaction {tx._id}: {error.message}`);
      }
    }
  }
}

The service will use a combination of Exact Match, Pattern Matching (Regex), and Historical Suggester logic to assign accounting categories, tax codes (GST/PST), and tracking categories to incoming bank data.

≡ƒæñ User Story

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

2. The Matching Engine Logic
Level 1: Saved Rules: Check the MappingRules collection for user-defined rules (e.g., "If Merchant contains 'Amazon', map to 'Office Supplies'").

Level 2: Fuzzy Matching: Use the string-similarity library to find the closest match between a Plaid category and a QuickBooks account name.

Level 3: Global Intelligence: (Optional) Suggest categories based on how other businesses in the Metro Vancouver area have mapped similar merchants.

3. Tax & Tracking Mapping

≡ƒ¢á Transaction Mapping Architecture
The mapper acts as a middleware that enriches raw transaction data before it is "Pushed" back to the accounting software or displayed on the dashboard.

TypeScript
// backend/src/integrations/mapping/mapper.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class MapperService {
  
  async mapTransaction(transaction: Transaction, businessId: string) {
    // 1. Check for custom user rules
    const rule = await this.ruleRepo.findMatchingRule(businessId, transaction.merchantName);
    if (rule) {
      return { ...transaction, accountId: rule.targetAccountId, confidence: 1.0 };
    }

    // 2. Fallback to fuzzy matching against Chart of Accounts
    const coa = await this.coaRepo.getAccountsForBusiness(businessId);
    const suggestedAccount = this.findBestMatch(transaction.rawCategory, coa);

    return {
      ...transaction,
      suggestedAccountId: suggestedAccount?.id,
      confidence: suggestedAccount ? 0.75 : 0.0,
      needsReview: !rule
    };
  }

  private findBestMatch(rawCat: string, accounts: Account[]) {
    // Logic to compare 'Food & Drink' with 'Meals & Entertainment'
    return stringSimilarity.findBestMatch(rawCat, accounts.map(a => a.name));
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
[ ] The service can successfully fetch and store the Chart of Accounts from QuickBooks and Xero.

[ ] Raw transactions are correctly updated with a suggestedAccountId based on existing rules.

[ ] Users can create, edit, and delete "Auto-Map Rules" from the Flutter UI.

[ ] Transactions with low confidence scores ( < 0.5) are flagged as "Needs Review" in the database.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Data Integrity: Mapping rules must be scoped strictly to the BusinessId (Issue #49) to prevent cross-tenant rule leakage.

Audit Trail: Log whenever an "Auto-Map" occurs, noting the ruleId or algorithm used, for financial transparency.

PIPA Compliance: Do not store sensitive merchant details in the "Global Intelligence" layer; only use anonymized merchant strings and category IDs (Issue #45).`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 56: Issue #57: Secure Token Management & Refresh Logic`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Description
This issue involves developing a centralized Token Management Service in NestJS to handle the lifecycle of OAuth 2.0 and API tokens for all third-party providers (Plaid, QBO, Xero). The service must proactively refresh tokens before they expire, handle "Token Revoked" errors gracefully, and ensure that all sensitive credentials are encrypted at rest using AES-256-GCM.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a Lead Developer, I want a background service that automatically refreshes our accounting and banking tokens 5 minutes before they expire, so that our data synchronization jobs (Issue #51) never fail due to "Unauthorized" errors.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements
1. Proactive Refresh Strategy
Expiry Tracking: Store a expiresAt timestamp (UTC) for every token in the Business schema.

The "Buffer" Rule: Implement a 5-minute "safety buffer." If currentTime > (expiresAt - 5 mins), trigger a refresh immediately.

Concurrency Control: Use a Redis Lock (or a simple database flag) to ensure that two parallel processes don't try to refresh the same token simultaneously.

2. Secure Storage (Encryption Layer)
Algorithm: Use crypto.createCipheriv with AES-256-GCM.

Key Management: The ENCRYPTION_KEY must be stored as a Secret in your environment (AWS Secrets Manager or Heroku Config Vars) and never committed to GitHub.

Salt/IV: Generate a unique Initialization Vector (IV) for every encryption operation and store it alongside the ciphertext.

3. Error Handling & Circuit Breaking
Invalid Grant: If a refresh_token is rejected (e.g., the user changed their password), catch the error, mark the integration as DISCONNECTED, and trigger a Sentry alert.

User Notification: Emit a "Re-authentication Required" event to the Flutter app via Socket.io (Issue #131) so the user sees a "Fix Connection" button.

≡ƒ¢á Secure Token Lifecycle Workflow
The service acts as an invisible layer between your business logic and the external APIs, ensuring "always-on" connectivity.

TypeScript
// backend/src/integrations/token-manager.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class TokenManagerService {
  
  async getValidAccessToken(businessId: string, provider: 'QBO' | 'XERO'): Promise<string> {
    const integration = await this.db.getIntegration(businessId, provider);
    
    // Check if token is about to expire
    if (this.isNearExpiry(integration.expiresAt)) {
      return await this.refreshAndSaveToken(businessId, integration);
    }
    
    // Decrypt and return the existing token
    return this.cryptoService.decrypt(integration.encryptedAccessToken, integration.iv);
  }

  private isNearExpiry(expiryDate: Date): boolean {
    const BUFFER_TIME = 5 * 60 * 1000; // 5 minutes
    return (expiryDate.getTime() - Date.now()) < BUFFER_TIME;
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
[ ] Tokens are successfully encrypted before being saved to MongoDB Atlas.

[ ] The refresh logic correctly updates the access_token and expiresAt fields in the database.

[ ] If a token refresh fails, the integration status is updated to ERROR.

The flow ensures that we identify the correct organization (Tenant) within the user's Xero account.

TypeScript
// backend/src/integrations/xero/xero-auth.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class XeroAuthService {
  private xero = new XeroClient({
    clientId: this.config.get('XERO_CLIENT_ID'),
    clientSecret: this.config.get('XERO_CLIENT_SECRET'),
    redirectUris: [this.config.get('XERO_REDIRECT_URI')],
    scopes: 'openid profile email accounting.transactions.read offline_access'.split(' '),
  });

  // 1. Get the Auth URL with PKCE
  async getAuthUrl() {
    return await this.xero.buildConsentUrl();
  }

  // 2. Handle Callback and Fetch Tenants
  async handleCallback(url: string) {
    const tokenSet = await this.xero.apiCallback(url);
    await this.xero.updateTenants();
    
    // Xero allows access to multiple organizations
    const tenants = this.xero.tenants; 
    
    return {
      tokens: tokenSet,
      availableTenants: tenants.map(t => ({ id: t.tenantId, name: t.tenantName }))
    };
  }
}

// backend/src/integrations/quickbooks/qbo-auth.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class QboAuthService {
  private oauthClient: OAuthClient;

  constructor(private config: ConfigService) {
    this.oauthClient = new OAuthClient({
      clientId: this.config.get('QBO_CLIENT_ID'),
      clientSecret: this.config.get('QBO_CLIENT_SECRET'),
      environment: this.config.get('QBO_ENV'), // 'sandbox' or 'production'
      redirectUri: this.config.get('QBO_REDIRECT_URI'),
    });
  }

  // Generate the "Connect to QuickBooks" URL
  getAuthUri(): string {
    return this.oauthClient.authorizeUri({
      scope: [OAuthClient.scopes.Accounting, OAuthClient.scopes.OpenId],
      state: 'random_secure_state_string',
    });
  }

  // Handle the callback and exchange code for tokens
  async exchangeCodeForToken(url: string) {
    const authResponse = await this.oauthClient.createToken(url);
    const tokenData = authResponse.getJson();
    
    // Save tokenData.access_token, tokenData.refresh_token, and realmId to DB
    return tokenData;
  }
}

This issue involves implementing a Soft Delete pattern across all core Mongoose schemas (Business, Transaction, Membership). ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

4. Automated Purge (Retention Worker)
Cron Job: Using @nestjs/schedule, run a nightly job that finds records where deletedAt is older than 30 days.

Hard Delete: Perform a permanent deleteMany() for these expired records to stay compliant with "Data Minimization" principles.

≡ƒ¢á Soft Delete & Data Retention Workflow
The system ensures a multi-stage lifecycle for data, moving from "Active" to "Soft-Deleted" (Recoverable) and finally to "Purged" (Permanent).

TypeScript
// backend/src/common/schemas/base.schema.ts

export class BaseSchema {
  @Prop({ default: false, index: true })
  isDeleted: boolean;

  @Prop({ type: Date, default: null })
  deletedAt: Date;
}

// backend/src/common/middleware/soft-delete.middleware.ts

export function SoftDeleteMiddleware(schema: Schema) {
  schema.pre('find', function() {
    this.where({ isDeleted: { $ne: true } });
  });

  schema.pre('findOne', function() {
    this.where({ isDeleted: { $ne: true } });
  });
  
  // Custom method to 'Soft Delete'
  schema.methods.softDelete = function() {
    this.isDeleted = true;
    this.deletedAt = new Date();
    return this.save();
  };
}

[ ] A PATCH request to the restore endpoint successfully brings the record back to the active list.

[ ] The automated purge job correctly identifies and hard-deletes records older than 30 days.

TypeScript
// backend/src/main.ts

async function bootstrap() {
  const app = await NestFactory.create(AppModule);
  
  app.useGlobalPipes(new ValidationPipe({
    whitelist: true,
    forbidNonWhitelisted: true,
    transform: true,
    exceptionFactory: (errors) => {
      // Map errors to a clean format for the Flutter frontend
      return new BadRequestException(errors.map(err => ({
        field: err.property,
        errors: Object.values(err.constraints),
      })));
    },
  }));
  
  await app.listen(3000);
}

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements

Extraction: Use zlib or a child process to extract the BSON/JSON files from the archive.

3. Database Ingestion (mongorestore)
Atomic Restore: Use the --drop flag with mongorestore to ensure the collection is cleared before the backup is injected, preventing "Duplicate Key" errors.

Target Mapping: Allow the script to take a TARGET_URI parameter so it can restore to a local Docker instance or an Atlas Staging cluster.

≡ƒ¢á Cloud-to-Local Restoration Workflow
The restoration process reverses the backup pipeline, moving data from the secure cloud vault back into the active application environment.

TypeScript
// backend/src/maintenance/restore.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class RestoreService {
  private readonly logger = new Logger(RestoreService.name);

  async restoreFromCloud(backupKey: string, targetUri: string) {
    this.logger.log(`Initiating restoration from: ${backupKey}`);

    try {
      // 1. Download from S3
      const localPath = await this.s3Service.downloadFile(backupKey);

      // 2. Decompress
      const extractedPath = await this.decompress(localPath);

      // 3. Execute mongorestore via Child Process
      const { stdout, stderr } = await exec(
        `mongorestore --uri="${targetUri}" --drop --archive="${extractedPath}" --gzip`
      );

      this.logger.log('Database restoration completed successfully.');
    } catch (error) {
      this.logger.error('Restoration FAILED!', error.stack);
      // This is a critical failure - manual intervention required
      throw new InternalServerErrorException('Disaster Recovery Failure');
    }
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
[ ] The script can successfully list available backups from the AWS S3 bucket.

[ ] A full restoration to a local MongoDB instance completes in under 5 minutes for a standard dev dataset.

[ ] The --drop functionality is verified (old data is replaced by backup data).

[ ] The process is restricted to Super-Admin credentials only.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Access Control: This service must NOT have a public API endpoint. It should be a CLI command or a heavily guarded internal admin route behind MFA.

Temporary File Purge: Ensure that all decrypted/uncompressed data is deleted from the server's local disk immediately after the restoration is finished.

Audit Logging: Every restoration attempt (successful or failed) must be logged with the adminId, backupTimestamp, and targetEnvironment for compliance audits.

Data Sovereignty: Since we are moving data between S3 and the DB, verify that all traffic stays within the Amazon Private Network to avoid public internet exposure.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 50: Issue #51: Implement Automated Backup Trigger (Local to Cloud)`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Description

// backend/src/maintenance/backup.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class BackupService {
  private readonly logger = new Logger(BackupService.name);

  @Cron(CronExpression.EVERY_DAY_AT_3AM, { timeZone: 'America/Vancouver' })
  async handleDailyBackup() {
    this.logger.log('Starting automated backup trigger...');
    
    try {
      // 1. Generate Backup (Local dump or Atlas Snapshot API)
      const filePath = await this.generateLocalDump();

      // 2. Upload to S3 (Canada-Central-1)
      const s3Key = `backups/${new Date().toISOString()}_prod_backup.tar.gz`;
      await this.s3Service.uploadFile(filePath, s3Key);

      // 3. Clean up local temp file
      await this.deleteLocalFile(filePath);
      
      this.logger.log(`Backup successfully uploaded to S3: ${s3Key}`);
    } catch (error) {
      // Log failure to Sentry (Issue #165)
      Sentry.captureException(error);
      this.logger.error('Backup failed! Check Sentry for details.');
    }
  }
}

})

This issue involves implementing a Signal Protocol-inspired E2EE layer for the B2B Chat System (Issue #131). Furthermore, sensitive MetadataΓÇösuch as the specific product being negotiated or the discount percentages mentionedΓÇömust be obfuscated to prevent server-side data mining of private business deals.

≡ƒæñ User Story

Public Key Bundle: Upload the Public Key to the NestJS backend so other users can initiate encrypted sessions.

2. The Encryption Pipeline (X3DH / Double Ratchet)
Pre-key Bundles: Implement a "Pre-key" system so users can start an encrypted chat even if the recipient is offline.

Message Payload: Before sending via WebSockets, the Flutter app must encrypt the message body and sensitive metadata (e.g., quotedPrice, discountCode) using the recipient's public key.

Shared Secret: Use Diffie-Hellman (ECDH) to derive a shared symmetric key for the actual message exchange.

3. Metadata Obfuscation (NestJS)
Server Blindness: The backend should only see routing metadata (Sender ID, Receiver ID, Timestamp).

Encrypted "Extra" Fields: Fields like negotiated_amount or item_reference must be stored as encrypted BLOBs in MongoDB.

≡ƒ¢á E2EE Communication Workflow
The server acts only as a "blind postman," delivering sealed envelopes it cannot open.

≡ƒô¥ Description
This issue involves building an Anonymization Pipeline within the NestJS backend. Before any transaction or invoice data is exported to long-term cloud storage (AWS S3) for "Big Data" analytics or archival, sensitive Personally Identifiable Information (PII) and Business Identifiable Information (BII) must be either masked, hashed, or removed. This ensures the platform's "Market Trends" engine can learn from data patterns without compromising individual user privacy.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a Security-Conscious User, I want to know that my specific company name and my employees' names are removed from the datasets you use for "Market Benchmarking," so that my competitors can't reverse-engineer my supply chain costs.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements
1. The Anonymization Engine (NestJS)
Field Mapping: Define a "Sensitivity Schema" that identifies which fields are:

Direct Identifiers: (Names, Emails, Phone Numbers, SINs) -> Must be Redacted/Removed.

Indirect Identifiers: (Postal Codes, Dates, Merchant Names) -> Must be Masked or Generalized.

Values: (Amounts, Quantities) -> Kept for Analytics.

Techniques to Implement:

Pseudonymization: Replace UserIDs with a unique, consistent cryptographic hash (SHA-256 + Salt).

Generalization: Convert specific timestamps to "Month/Year" and specific addresses to "City/District" (e.g., "V3J" instead of a full Coquitlam address).

2. Pre-Storage Middleware
S3 Interceptor: Create a service that intercepts any Upload command to the "Analytics" bucket.

Stream Transformation: Process the data stream "on-the-fly" using the Anonymizer service before it hits the S3 putObject command.

3. Key Management (KMS)
Salt Management: Store the cryptographic salts in AWS KMS or HashiCorp Vault.

Irreversibility: Ensure that the production environment does not have a "De-Anonymize" function enabled by default; it should be a one-way transformation for analytics.

≡ƒ¢á Anonymization Workflow
The pipeline acts as a filter, stripping identity while preserving the analytical utility of the data.

TypeScript
// backend/src/storage/anonymizer.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class AnonymizerService {
  
  // Transform a raw transaction into an anonymous analytical record
  transformForStorage(record: RawTransaction): AnonymousRecord {
    return {
      // Hashing the business ID so we can still group by 'Company A' 
      // without knowing who 'Company A' is.
      orgHash: this.crypto.hash(record.orgId, process.env.ANON_SALT),
      
      // Generalizing location to City level
      location: this.extractCity(record.address), 
      
      // Keeping the core business metrics
      category: record.category,
      amount: record.amount,
      currency: 'CAD',
      
      // Redacting sensitive strings
      memo: "[REDACTED]",
      vendorName: this.maskVendor(record.vendorName)
    };
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
[ ] Direct PII (Names, Emails) is 100% removed from all exports to the analytics-data bucket.

[ ] Cryptographic hashes are consistent (the same OrgID always produces the same hash) to allow for time-series analysis.

[ ] The "Generalization" logic correctly converts specific dates/locations into broader categories.

[ ] No raw, unmasked data is found in the S3 "Cold Storage" during a manual audit.

TypeScript
// backend/src/export/strategies/export.strategy.ts

export interface ExportStrategy {
  generate(data: any[], options: ExportOptions): Promise<Buffer | ReadableStream>;
}

// backend/src/export/export.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class ExportService {
  private strategies = new Map<string, ExportStrategy>();

  constructor(
    private csvStrategy: CsvExportStrategy,
    private pdfStrategy: PdfExportStrategy,
  ) {
    this.strategies.set('csv', csvStrategy);
    this.strategies.set('pdf', pdfStrategy);
  }

  async exportData(format: string, data: any[], options: ExportOptions) {
    const strategy = this.strategies.get(format.toLowerCase());
    if (!strategy) throw new BadRequestException('Unsupported format');
    
    return strategy.generate(data, options);
  }
}

provider: QUICKBOOKS or XERO.

accessToken: Encrypted (AES-256).

refreshToken: Encrypted (AES-256).

realmId: (For QBO) The specific Company ID.

expiryDate: When the current accessToken becomes invalid.

≡ƒ¢á OAuth 2.0 Sequence Workflow
The flow ensures that the user's credentials never touch our databaseΓÇöonly the resulting API tokens.
// backend/src/integrations/accounting-auth.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class AccountingAuthService {
  
  // 1. Generate the Authorization URL
  getAuthorizationUrl(provider: 'QBO' | 'XERO', userId: string): string {
    const state = this.cryptoService.generateState(userId);
    return this.providerSdk.getAuthUrl({
      clientId: process.env.QBO_CLIENT_ID,
      redirectUri: process.env.QBO_REDIRECT_URI,
      scope: ['com.intuit.quickbooks.accounting'],
      state: state
    });
  }

  // 2. Handle the Callback
  async handleCallback(code: string, state: string) {
    // Verify state to prevent CSRF
    const userId = await this.cryptoService.verifyState(state);
    
    // Exchange code for tokens
    const tokenResponse = await this.providerSdk.createToken(code);
    
    // Encrypt and save to MongoDB
    await this.saveTokens(userId, tokenResponse);
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
[ ] Users can trigger the OAuth flow from the Flutter settings page.

[ ] The app correctly handles the redirect back to the NestJS backend.

[ ] Tokens are successfully stored in an encrypted format in MongoDB.

[ ] The "Refresh Token" logic successfully extends a session without user intervention.

[ ] All failed auth attempts are logged to Sentry (Issue #165) with specific error codes.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Encryption at Rest: Tokens must be encrypted using a server-side ENCRYPTION_KEY that is never stored in the database.

TLS 1.2+: All communication with Intuit/Xero servers must happen over a secure TLS 1.2 or 1.3 connection.

Token Minimization: Only request the minimum scopes required to perform spending analysis; do not request "Write" permissions unless absolutely necessary for future features.

Audit Trail: Log every time an access token is refreshed or used to fetch data for compliance auditing.

≡ƒ¢á Transaction Sync Architecture
The service follows a robust synchronization loop to ensure the mobile dashboard is always up to date.
// backend/src/transactions/transaction-sync.service.ts

@Injectable()
export class TransactionSyncService {
  constructor(private readonly plaidClient: PlaidApi) {}

  async syncTransactions(item: ItemDocument) {
    let cursor = item.lastSyncCursor;
    let added: Transaction[] = [];
    let hasNextPage = true;

    while (hasNextPage) {
      const response = await this.plaidClient.transactionsSync({
        access_token: item.accessToken,
        cursor: cursor,
      });

      added = added.concat(response.data.added);
      cursor = response.data.next_cursor;
      hasNextPage = response.data.has_more;
    }

    // Process and Save to MongoDB
    await this.saveTransactionsToDb(item.userId, added);
    
    // Update the cursor for the next run
    await this.updateItemCursor(item._id, cursor);
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria
[ ] The service successfully fetches transactions from the Plaid Sandbox using a test access_token.

[ ] The sync logic handles "Deleted" transactions from the bank (removing them from our DB).

[ ] Duplicate transactions are prevented by checking the unique transactionId.

[ ] The process is wrapped in a try-catch block that logs failures to Sentry (Issue #165).

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Token Security: access_tokens must be encrypted at rest in MongoDB using AES-256.

Rate Limiting: Implement "Exponential Backoff" if the Plaid API returns a 429 Too Many Requests error.

Data Residency: Ensure the transaction data stays within the AWS Canada (Central) region to comply with local business data sovereignty preferences.

Webhook Handling: (Bonus) Implement a listener for TRANSACTIONS_REMOVED and INITIAL_UPDATE webhooks from Plaid to trigger immediate syncs.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 40: Issue #41: Integrate Plaid SDK for Canadian Bank Linking`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Context

// Flutter implementation snippet
void _openPlaidLink() async {
  Configuration configuration = LinkTokenConfiguration(
    token: "GENERATED_LINK_TOKEN_FROM_BACKEND",
  );

  PlaidLink.open(configuration: configuration);

  PlaidLink.onSuccess.listen((success) {
    // Send success.publicToken to NestJS backend
    _syncService.sendPublicToken(success.publicToken);
  });
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance

When a user edits a transaction offline on their phone and simultaneously another change is synced from a second device, a conflict occurs. We need a deterministic way to decide which version of the receipt data is the "Truth." We will implement a Last Write Wins (LWW) strategy using a lastModified timestamp to ensure the most recent update is preserved across the ObjectBox (Local) and MongoDB (Cloud) ecosystem.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a user, I want my latest changes to be reflected across all my devices without losing data or seeing duplicate receipts if I edit them while my internet is unstable.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements (Logic & Architecture)
1. The Metadata Layer
Field Addition: Add a lastModified (Int64/DateTime) field to both the ObjectBox TransactionEntity and the Mongoose TransactionSchema.

Auto-Update: Every time a user hits "Save" or "Edit" on the mobile UI, the lastModified timestamp must be updated to the current device time (UTC).

2. Conflict Resolution Logic (The "Merge" Rules)
When the Sync Service (Issue #39) runs, it must apply these rules:

Rule A (Cloud is Newer): If Cloud.lastModified > Local.lastModified, the local record is overwritten by the cloud version.

Rule B (Local is Newer): If Local.lastModified > Cloud.lastModified, the local version is pushed to the cloud (Upsert).

Rule C (Deleted Status): Implement a "Soft Delete" flag (isDeleted: true). If one side is marked deleted with a newer timestamp, the record is removed from both ends.

3. Flutter Sync Service Update
Update the SyncManager to fetch the "Server State" before pushing local changes.

Use a Repository Pattern to handle the swap between local storage and the sync result.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Workflow Guidance
The conflictΓÇæresolution process is based on timestamp comparison, ensuring that the most recent edit always prevails.
// Example of Conflict Check in Flutter (Client-Side)
void resolveConflict(TransactionEntity localTx, Map<String, dynamic> cloudData) {
  DateTime cloudLastModified = DateTime.parse(cloudData['lastModified']);
  
  if (cloudLastModified.isAfter(localTx.lastModified)) {
    // Cloud wins: Update local ObjectBox record
    localTx.amount = cloudData['amount'];
    localTx.vendor = cloudData['vendor'];
    localTx.lastModified = cloudLastModified;
    localTx.dbSyncStatus = SyncStatus.synced;
    
    objectbox.store.box<TransactionEntity>().put(localTx);
  } else {
    // Local wins: Mark for upload in the next sync cycle
    localTx.dbSyncStatus = SyncStatus.pending;
    objectbox.store.box<TransactionEntity>().put(localTx);
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
Timestamp Accuracy: Both the local and cloud databases store the lastModified field in UTC format.

Idempotency: Running the sync process repeatedly with identical data produces no changes in the database.

Race Condition Handling: If the server version is newer, local data is updated automatically based on the LWW (LastΓÇæWriteΓÇæWins) policy, without prompting the user.

Soft Delete: When a transaction is deleted offline, the deletion is correctly applied to the server once the device reconnects (using the isDeleted flag).

Performance: The comparison and merge operations for 100 records complete in under 50 milliseconds.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Data Integrity: During conflict resolution, the userId field must never be altered to prevent data from being reassigned between users.

Audit Trail (Optional): A lightweight revision history can be stored in MongoDB to enable rollback in case of issues.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 38: Issue #39: Develop Sync Controller for Transaction Upload`n`n[Description]`nContext
To ensure a "Zero-effort" cloud backup, the app needs a smart synchronization layer. Instead of a simple "Upload All," we will implement a Delta Sync mechanism. This prevents duplicate records if a sync is interrupted and retried.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Workflow Guidance
The Delta Sync ensures that only "dirty" data (new or modified) travels over the network.
// src/transactions/transactions.service.ts (NestJS Logic)
@Injectable()
export class TransactionsService {
  constructor(@InjectModel(Transaction.name) private model: Model<TransactionDocument>) {}

  async syncTransactions(userId: string, dtos: CreateTransactionDto[]) {
    const operations = dtos.map(dto => ({
      updateOne: {
        filter: { localId: dto.localId, userId: userId },
        update: { ...dto, userId: userId },
        upsert: true,
      }
    }));

    await this.model.bulkWrite(operations);
    return { syncedIds: dtos.map(d => d.localId) };
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] Bulk Processing: The endpoint can handle a batch of 50+ transactions in a single request.

[ ] User Isolation: Transactions are strictly saved under the authenticated user's ID (verified via FirebaseAuthGuard).

[ ] Idempotency: Re-sending the same transaction does not create a duplicate in MongoDB.

[ ] Performance: A sync of 20 items completes in under 200ms on the backend.

[ ] Status Mapping: The Flutter app correctly flips the "Synced" toggle in the local UI after receiving the 200 OK response.

// src/auth/firebase-auth.guard.ts (NestJS Example)
import { Injectable, CanActivate, ExecutionContext, UnauthorizedException } from '@nestjs/common';
import * as admin from 'firebase-admin';

@Injectable()
export class FirebaseAuthGuard implements CanActivate {
  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {
    final request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();
    final token = request.headers.authorization?.split('Bearer ')[1];

    if (!token) throw new UnauthorizedException('No token provided');

    try {
      // Verify token with Firebase Admin SDK
      const decodedToken = await admin.auth().verifyIdToken(token);
      request['user'] = decodedToken; // Attach user info to request
      return true;
    } catch (error) {
      throw new UnauthorizedException('Invalid or expired token');
    }
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
Google SignΓÇæIn: Users can successfully log in to the Flutter app using their Google account.

Token Exchange: The Flutter app correctly sends the Firebase ID token in the headers of API requests.

Backend Security: Protected routes in NestJS return a 401 Unauthorized error when accessed without a valid Firebase token.

User Sync: User information is stored in MongoDB Atlas after their first successful login.

Error Handling: Appropriate messages are shown to the user when phoneΓÇænumber verification fails (e.g., incorrect code).

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Secret Management: The Firebase serviceAccountKey.json private key must never be committed to GitHub; environment variables must be used instead.

Data Residency: In accordance with privacy regulations, only essential user data should be stored in the cloud profile.`n`n[Comments]`n- Smscodehub: For phone auth testing, remember Firebase's test verification codes only work with whitelisted numbersΓÇöfor real SMS delivery during development, you'll need a service. import { Module } from '@nestjs/common';
import { MongooseModule } from '@nestjs/mongoose';
import { ConfigModule, ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';

@Module({
  imports: [
    // Load environment variables
    ConfigModule.forRoot({ isGlobal: true }),
    // Connect to MongoDB Atlas asynchronously
    MongooseModule.forRootAsync({
      imports: [ConfigModule],
      useFactory: async (configService: ConfigService) => ({
        uri: configService.get<string>('MONGO_URI'),
      }),
      inject: [ConfigService],
    }),
  ],
})
export class AppModule {}

These could be legitimate major purchases or errors in OCR/manual entry. By applying the Standard Deviation (Z-Score) method, we can flag any day where spending exceeds the mathematical norm (Mean + 2x StdDev). This ensures the user's "Forecast" (Issue #31) isn't ruined by a single anomalous data point.
≡ƒæñ User StoryAs a user, I want to be notified if a transaction seems unusually high so that I can double-check for errors or investigate unexpected business costs.
ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)1. Statistical Outlier EngineImplement the "Z-Score" logic in AnalyticsUtils:Step 1: Calculate the Mean (\mu) of the last 30 days.Step 2: Calculate the Standard Deviation (\sigma) to measure volatility.Step 3: Set the Threshold: Any value x where x > (\mu + 2\sigma) or x < (\mu - 2\sigma) is flagged as an anomaly.2. Anomaly Signal SystemDetection: Run this check every time a new transaction is saved or synced.Action: If an anomaly is detected, create a "System Alert" object in the local database.UI Trigger: Add a "Warning" badge to the Transaction on the Dashboard and a notification in the app's Inbox.3. User Feedback LoopAllow the user to:Confirm: "Yes, this was a real expense." (This includes the outlier in future forecasts).Edit: "No, this was an error." (Correct the amount).
≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
Using standard deviation allows you to distinguish normal fluctuations from true anomalies.
class AnomalyDetector {
  /// Detects if the current spending is a statistical outlier
  static bool isOutlier(double currentAmount, List<double> history) {
    if (history.length < 10) return false; // Not enough data for a baseline

    // 1. Calculate Mean
    double mean = history.reduce((a, b) => a + b) / history.length;

    // 2. Calculate Standard Deviation
    double variance = history.map((x) => pow(x - mean, 2)).reduce((a, b) => a + b) / history.length;
    double stdDev = sqrt(variance);

    // 3. Check Z-Score (2.0 is the standard threshold for 95% confidence)
    double zScore = (currentAmount - mean).abs() / stdDev;
    
    return zScore > 2.0;
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
Mathematical Accuracy: Standard deviation calculations have been validated using sample test data.

Baseline Handling: The system does not trigger alerts until at least 10 transactions have been recorded (preventing early false positives).

UI Integration: Anomalous transactions are visually marked in the list with a warning icon (such as ΓÜá∩╕Å).

Inbox Signal: A system message is sent to the userΓÇÖs Inbox for manual review.

Performance: Anomaly detection calculations complete in under 5 milliseconds.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Privacy: All analysis is performed locally, and no information about the userΓÇÖs spending patterns is shared externally.

Audit Trail: Logging anomaly alerts helps the user provide reasonable explanations for sudden spending spikes during a financial or tax audit.
`n`n[Comments]`n- Whatsonyourmind: Z-score assumes your data is normally distributed. Financial transaction data almost never is ΓÇö it is heavily right-skewed (many small purchases, few large ones). This means z-score will under-flag large legitimate transactions and over-flag moderate ones.

**Three approaches ranked by robustness:**

**1. Modified Z-score (best for skewed financial data):**
```python
median = np.median(amounts)
mad = np.median(np.abs(amounts - median))  # median absolute deviation
modified_z = 0.6745 * (amounts - median) / mad
# Flag if |modified_z| > 3.5
```
MAD is robust to extreme outliers ΓÇö a single $50K purchase does not distort the baseline the way it would with standard deviation.

**2. IQR method (non-parametric):**
```python
Q1, Q3 = np.percentile(amounts, [25, 75])
IQR = Q3 - Q1
lower = Q1 - 1.5 * IQR
upper = Q3 + 1.5 * IQR
outliers = (amounts < lower) | (amounts > upper)
```
No distribution assumptions. Works well for detecting both unusually high and unusually low transactions.

**3. Adaptive per-user thresholds (learns over time):**
For each user, maintain a rolling 30-day Beta distribution of spending:
```python
user_mean = rolling_mean(user_transactions, window=30)
user_std = rolling_std(user_transactions, window=30)
z = (new_transaction - user_mean) / user_std
```
This distinguishes "unusual for THIS user" from "unusual overall." A $5K purchase is normal for a high-spender but anomalous for someone who usually spends $50.

**For your forecast protection** (Issue #31): Winsorize detected outliers instead of removing them. Replace outlier values with the 95th percentile value. This preserves the signal (there WAS a large transaction) without letting it distort your trend estimation.`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 33: Issue #34: Create Interactive "What-if" Dashboard Widgets`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Context

LineChartBarData _buildForecastLine(List<double> forecastData, int startIndex) {
  return LineChartBarData(
    spots: forecastData.asMap().entries.map((e) {
      return FlSpot((startIndex + e.key).toDouble(), e.value);
    }).toList(),
    isCurved: true,
    dashArray: [5, 5], // This creates the "Dashed" forecast effect
    color: AppColors.neonRed.withOpacity(0.5),
    barWidth: 3,
    dotData: FlDotData(show: false),
    belowBarData: BarAreaData(
      show: true,
      gradient: LinearGradient(
        colors: [AppColors.neonRed.withOpacity(0.1), Colors.transparent],
        begin: Alignment.topCenter,
        end: Alignment.bottomCenter,
      ),
    ),
  );
}

Raw financial data is volatile. A Simple Moving Average (SMA) calculates the average of the last 7 days to create a smooth trendline. To make the model even more responsive to sudden market changes (like a new subscription or a price hike), we will also implement a Weighted Moving Average (WMA), which gives higher importance to the most recent days.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a user, I want to see a smoothed version of my spending chart so that I can understand my "True Burn Rate" without being distracted by a single large purchase I made three days ago.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. Simple Moving Average (SMA)
Logic: For each point in the time series, calculate the mean of the current day and the 6 preceding days.

<img width="788" height="286" alt="Image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/ba1d85c4-4297-44d8-8d1b-4e55f04a20b4" />
3. Window Management
Ensure the algorithm handles the "Startup" period (the first 6 days of the dataset) by using a smaller window or padding with the first available value to prevent null results at the start of the chart.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
These functions should be added to your ForecastEngine or AnalyticsUtils.
class MovingAverageEngine {
  /// Calculates a 7-day Simple Moving Average
  static List<double> calculateSMA(List<double> data, int window) {
    List<double> sma = [];
    for (int i = 0; i < data.length; i++) {
      if (i < window - 1) {
        // Not enough data yet, use average of what we have
        double sum = data.sublist(0, i + 1).reduce((a, b) => a + b);
        sma.add(sum / (i + 1));
      } else {
        double sum = data.sublist(i - window + 1, i + 1).reduce((a, b) => a + b);
        sma.add(sum / window);
      }
    }
    return sma;
  }

  /// Calculates a Weighted Moving Average (recent days matter more)
  static List<double> calculateWMA(List<double> data, int window) {
    List<double> wma = [];
    int weightSum = (window * (window + 1)) ~/ 2;

    for (int i = 0; i < data.length; i++) {
      if (i < window - 1) {
        wma.add(data[i]); // Fallback for initial points
        continue;
      }

      double currentSum = 0;
      for (int j = 0; j < window; j++) {
        // Weight increases as we get closer to the current index 'i'
        currentSum += data[i - window + 1 + j] * (j + 1);
      }
      wma.add(currentSum / weightSum);
    }
    return wma;
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)[ ] Smoothing Effect: When plotted, the SMA/WMA lines significantly reduce the visual "spikes" compared to raw daily data.[ ] Responsiveness: The WMA reacts faster to a large spending change on "Day 7" than the SMA does.[ ] Mathematical Precision: Verified that the weighted sum correctly divides by the sum of weights (e.g., for a 3-day window, the divisor is $1+2+3=6$).[ ] Performance: Calculation for a 365-day dataset takes less than 2ms.[ ] Integration: The results are ready to be passed to the FlChart or CustomPainter for the Dashboard.≡ƒöÉ Security & ComplianceData Privacy: This is purely a mathematical transformation of existing ObjectBox data. No new sensitive identifiers are created.Context: The WMA is particularly useful for detecting a "High Burn Rate" early, which can be used to trigger local notifications (Issue #33) to help the business owner save money.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 30: Issue #31: Develop Simple Linear Regression Model`n`n[Description]`n
≡ƒô¥ ContextTo answer the user's question, "How much will I spend by the end of the week?", we need to find the underlying trend in their historical data. Linear Regression calculates a straight line (y = mx + b) that minimizes the distance between the line and the actual daily spending points. We then extend this line into the future (Extrapolation) to predict the next 7 days.

≡ƒæñ User StoryAs a business owner, I want to see a predicted spending line for the next week so I can adjust my budget before I run out of cash.ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)1. The Least Squares FormulaImplement the mathematical solver for the slope (m) and the intercept (b):

<img width="747" height="545" alt="Image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/23e496e4-dca8-41b6-8f7c-f6023243cd01" />
3. Trend AnalysisIf m > 0, the trend is increasing (Burn rate is rising).If m < 0, the trend is decreasing (Spending is cooling down).
4. ≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code GuidanceThe solver should live in your AnalyticsUtils to keep the logic separated from the UI.

class ForecastEngine {
  /// Predicts the next [steps] values based on [data]
  static List<double> predictNext(List<double> data, int steps) {
    int n = data.length;
    if (n < 2) return List.filled(steps, 0.0);

    double sumX = 0, sumY = 0, sumXY = 0, sumX2 = 0;

    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
      double x = i.toDouble();
      double y = data[i];
      sumX += x;
      sumY += y;
      sumXY += x * y;
      sumX2 += x * x;
    }

    // Calculate Slope (m) and Intercept (b)
    double m = (n * sumXY - sumX * sumY) / (n * sumX2 - sumX * sumX);
    double b = (sumY - m * sumX) / n;

    // Generate future points
    return List.generate(steps, (i) {
      double nextX = (n + i).toDouble();
      double prediction = (m * nextX) + b;
      // Financials can't be negative, so floor at 0.0
      return prediction < 0 ? 0.0 : double.parse(prediction.toStringAsFixed(2));
    });
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] Mathematical Accuracy: Verified against a manual calculation (e.g., if spending is exactly 10, 20, 30, the next value must be 40).

[ ] Negative Handling: Predicted values never fall below 0.00 (using max(0, prediction)).

[ ] Performance: Calculating a 30-day trend takes less than 1ms.

[ ] Trend Detection: The engine correctly identifies if spending is trending "Up" or "Down."

[ ] Unit Tests: Tests cover "flat" spending, "steep" growth, and "empty" data scenarios.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance

To perform financial forecasting or display trend charts, we cannot use raw, scattered transactions. We need to aggregate spending into a Time-Series format. This involves grouping multiple transactions from the same day into a single data point. This clean, normalized array will serve as the primary input for our linear regression and forecasting models.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a developer, I want to transform thousands of individual transactions into a daily spending sequence so that the AI engine can identify patterns and predict future cash flow.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. Daily Aggregation Logic
Input: List<TransactionEntity> (usually filtered by a date range, e.g., last 30 days).

Process: * Iterate through transactions.

Group them by the "Date" (ignoring the time/hours).

Sum the amount for each day.

Output: A Map<DateTime, double> or a list of DataPoint objects.

2. Filling the "Gaps" (Zero-Padding)
Financial data is often sparse (e.g., no spending on Sunday). A regression model requires a continuous sequence.

Requirement: If a day has no transactions, the aggregator must insert a data point with a value of 0.0.

Result: A perfectly continuous array where index i is Day 1 and index i+1 is Day 2.

3. Normalization for Math
Convert the DateTime keys into numerical "Time Steps" (e.g., Day 0, Day 1, Day 2) to simplify the linear regression math.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
The developer should implement this in AnalyticsUtils or a dedicated TimeSeriesService:
class TimeSeriesService {
  /// Converts raw transactions into a continuous daily total list
  List<double> prepareDailySeries(List<TransactionEntity> transactions, int daysBack) {
    final now = DateTime.now();
    final startDate = now.subtract(Duration(days: daysBack));
    
    // 1. Group existing data
    Map<String, double> dayTotals = {};
    for (var tx in transactions) {
      String dateKey = "${tx.date.year}-${tx.date.month}-${tx.date.day}";
      dayTotals[dateKey] = (dayTotals[dateKey] ?? 0) + tx.amount;
    }

    // 2. Fill gaps to create a continuous sequence
    List<double> series = [];
    for (int i = 0; i <= daysBack; i++) {
      final date = startDate.add(Duration(days: i));
      String dateKey = "${date.year}-${date.month}-${date.day}";
      series.add(dayTotals[dateKey] ?? 0.0);
    }

    return series;
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] Data Integrity: The sum of the daily aggregated series equals the sum of the raw transactions for that period.

[ ] Gap Handling: Days with no spending are correctly represented as 0.0 instead of being skipped.

[ ] Sorting: The resulting array is strictly chronological (earliest date to latest).

[ ] Performance: Aggregating 1,000 transactions over 90 days takes less than 3ms.

[ ] Edge Cases: Handle cases where the input list is empty (return a list of zeros).

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Data Anonymization: Since this is a mathematical transformation, ensure no PII (Vendor names or descriptions) is included in the resulting seriesΓÇöonly the numerical values.

Local Processing: As with all analytics, this stays entirely on the user's device, ensuring financial privacy remains a top priority.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 28: Issue #29: Implement Financial Math Utility Class (Analytics Engine)`n`n[Description]`nContext

class AnalyticsUtils {
  /// Calculates the percentage change between two periods.
  /// Useful for "Weekly Spending vs Last Week"
  static double calculateGrowthRate(double current, double previous) {
    if (previous == 0) return current > 0 ? 100.0 : 0.0;
    
    double growth = ((current - previous) / previous) * 100;
    return double.parse(growth.toStringAsFixed(2));
  }

  /// Calculates a simple moving average to smooth out volatility
  static List<double> movingAverage(List<double> data, int windowSize) {
    List<double> result = [];
    for (int i = 0; i <= data.length - windowSize; i++) {
      double sum = data.sublist(i, i + windowSize).reduce((a, b) => a + b);
      result.add(sum / windowSize);
    }
    return result;
  }
}

[ ] Division by Zero: The growth rate logic handles cases where the previous week's spending was $0 without crashing.

[ ] Precision: Calculations are verified to be accurate to 2 decimal places.

[ ] Efficiency: The utility can process a list of 1,000 transactions in under 10ms.

[ ] Unit Tests: A test suite is written to verify that a move from $100 to $150 returns exactly 50.0.

// Coordinate scaling logic is key here
class OCRPainter extends CustomPainter {
  final List<TextBlock> blocks;
  final Size imageSize; // Original image resolution

  OCRPainter(this.blocks, this.imageSize);

  @override
  void paint(Canvas canvas, Size size) {
    final double scaleX = size.width / imageSize.width;
    final double scaleY = size.height / imageSize.height;

    final paint = Paint()
      ..style = PaintingStyle.stroke
      ..strokeWidth = 2.0
      ..color = Colors.cyanAccent.withOpacity(0.8);

    for (var block in blocks) {
      final rect = Rect.fromLTRB(
        block.boundingBox.left * scaleX,
        block.boundingBox.top * scaleY,
        block.boundingBox.right * scaleX,
        block.boundingBox.bottom * scaleY,
      );
      canvas.drawRRect(RRect.fromRectAndRadius(rect, Radius.circular(4)), paint);
    }
  }

  @override
  bool shouldRepaint(covariant CustomPainter oldDelegate) => true;
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)

[ ] Accurate Mapping: Bounding boxes align perfectly with the text visible in the cropped image.

[ ] Visual Hierarchy: The boxes are subtle enough not to obscure the text but bright enough to be seen in Dark Mode.

import 'package:tflite_flutter/tflite_flutter.dart';

class DocumentClassifier {
  late Interpreter _interpreter;

  Future<void> loadModel() async {
    _interpreter = await Interpreter.fromAsset('AppAssets.receiptClassifierModel');
  }

  Future<bool> isReceipt(File imageFile) async {
    // 1. Convert File to Image and Resize (using 'image' package)
    // 2. Convert to Float32List (Tensor input)
    // 3. Run Inference
    var input = _preprocess(imageFile);
    var output = List.filled(2, 0).reshape([1, 2]); // Example for 2 classes
    
    _interpreter.run(input, output);
    
    // Check confidence for index 0 (Receipt)
    double confidence = output[0][0];
    return confidence > 0.75;
  }
}

≡ƒæñ User Story

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. Multi-Format Date Parser
The parser must handle the most common formats found on Canadian receipts:

ISO/Big-Endian: 2026-04-02 or 2026/04/02.

North American: 04/02/2026 or Apr 02, 2026.

Short Form: 04/02/26.

Logic: If multiple dates are found (e.g., "Transaction Date" vs "Print Date"), prioritize the one earlier in the text or closest to the "Total" keyword.

2. Currency Identification
Identify the currency based on text symbols and keywords:

CAD: $, CAD, CDN, or the presence of "HST/GST" (strong indicator of Canadian origin).

USD: USD, US$, or store names known to be US-only.

Default: Default to CAD for users with a Canadian locale.

3. Date Normalization
Convert all extracted strings into a standard Dart DateTime object. If the year is missing (e.g., Apr 02), assume the current year (2026).

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
The developer should use a list of RegExp patterns to "score" potential dates.
class FinancialParser {
  // Common Date Patterns
  static final List<RegExp> _datePatterns = [
    RegExp(r'\d{4}[-/]\d{2}[-/]\d{2}'), // YYYY-MM-DD
    RegExp(r'\d{2}[-/]\d{2}[-/]\d{4}'), // MM-DD-YYYY
    RegExp(r'(Jan|Feb|Mar|Apr|May|Jun|Jul|Aug|Sep|Oct|Nov|Dec)\s+\d{2},?\s+\d{4}', caseSensitive: false),
  ];

  static DateTime? extractDate(List<String> lines) {
    for (var line in lines) {
      for (var pattern in _datePatterns) {
        final match = pattern.firstMatch(line);
        if (match != null) {
          // Attempt to parse the matched string
          return DateTime.tryParse(match.group(0)!.replaceAll('/', '-'));
        }
      }
    }
    return null; // Fallback to DateTime.now() in the UI layer
  }

  static String detectCurrency(String rawText) {
    if (rawText.contains('USD') || rawText.contains('US\$')) return 'USD';
    return 'CAD'; // Default for your target market
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] Date Accuracy: Successfully parses 2026-04-02 and Apr 02, 2026 into the correct DateTime.

[ ] Currency Logic: Correctly flags a receipt as USD if the specific symbol/text is present.

[ ] Ambiguity Handling: For MM/DD vs DD/MM, the app uses a consistent "preferred" format based on the user's region settings.

[ ] Validation: Extracted dates cannot be in the future (e.g., if OCR misreads 2025 as 2029).

[ ] Integration: The extracted DateTime and Currency are passed to the TransactionEntity preview screen.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Privacy: No date or currency data is sent to external servers; parsing is 100% local.

Record Keeping: Accurate date extraction is vital for CRA (Canada Revenue Agency) compliance when the user exports data for tax season.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 23: Issue #24: Develop Regex-based Total Amount Extractor`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Context
After sorting text blocks (Issue #23), we are left with a list of strings. To automate the "Amount" field in the TransactionEntity, we need a robust parser that can identify currency strings, strip non-numeric characters (like $ or ,), and intelligently select the Grand Total from a list of sub-totals, taxes, and item prices.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a user, I want the app to automatically find the "Total" amount on my receipt so that I don't have to type the price manually after scanning.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. Canadian Currency Regex Patterns
The parser must recognize common formats used by Canadian retailers (e.g., Loblaws, Shoppers, Amazon.ca):

\$?\s?\d+[.,]\d{2} (Matches $12.99, 12.99, 1,250.00)

TOTAL\s?[:\-]?\s?\$?\d+[.,]\d{2} (Contextual match for "Total: $50.00")

2. The "Max-Value" Heuristic
Since a receipt contains many numbers (HST, Subtotal, Item prices), the algorithm should:

Extract all strings that match a price pattern.

Convert them to double.

Identify the maximum value: In 99% of cases, the largest number on a receipt is the Grand Total.

3. Cleanup Logic
Replace commas with dots for parsing (e.g., 1.200,50 -> 1200.50).

Remove currency symbols ($, CAD).

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
The developer should implement an AmountParser utility. Using a "Heuristic Score" improves accuracy: if a number is near the word "TOTAL" or "BALANCE," it gets a higher priority.
class AmountParser {
  // Regex to find digits formatted as currency
  static final RegExp _amountRegex = RegExp(r'\d+[\.,]\d{2}');

  static double? extractTotal(List<String> lines) {
    List<double> candidates = [];

    for (var line in lines) {
      // Find all matches in a single line
      final matches = _amountRegex.allMatches(line);
      for (var match in matches) {
        String cleaned = match.group(0)!.replaceAll(',', '.');
        double? value = double.tryParse(cleaned);
        if (value != null) candidates.add(value);
      }
    }

    if (candidates.isEmpty) return null;

    // Heuristic: The Grand Total is usually the largest number found
    candidates.sort((a, b) => b.compareTo(a));
    return candidates.first;
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] Accuracy: The parser correctly identifies the total on standard Canadian receipts (GST/HST included).

[ ] Comma/Dot Handling: Correctly parses both 1,200.50 and 1200.50.

[ ] Filtering: Ignores dates (e.g., 2026-04-02) or phone numbers that might look like large amounts.

[ ] Edge Cases: Handles receipts where the currency symbol is placed after the number (e.g., 15.00$).

[ ] Unit Test: Pass a mock list of strings (including subtotal, tax, and total) and verify the extractTotal returns the highest value.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
No Hardcoding: Ensure regex patterns are flexible to avoid missing "Total" if it's written as "Amount Due" or "Total CAD."

Precision: Use double for extraction but consider Decimal (from decimal package) if rounding errors occur in multi-currency scenarios.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 22: Issue #23: Implementation of Text Block Sorting Logic (Spatial Analysis)`n`n[Description]`n)
≡ƒô¥ Context
Raw OCR output from RecognizedText is often unordered. In a financial receipt, the relationship between a "Label" (e.g., Total) and its "Value" (e.g., $15.50) is defined by their spatial proximity on a horizontal line. This issue focuses on implementing a sorting algorithm that organizes detected text blocks by their Y-coordinates (rows) and then their X-coordinates (columns) to reconstruct the logical flow of the receipt.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a developer, I want the OCR text to be sorted logically by row and column so that my parsing heuristics (Issue #24) can accurately identify which numbers represent the "Total" versus the "Tax" or "Date."

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. Spatial Sorting Algorithm
The OCRService must process the TextBlock list before passing it to the parser.

Primary Sort (Vertical): Group blocks that share a similar top coordinate (within a small pixel threshold, e.g., 10-15px) into "Rows."

Secondary Sort (Horizontal): Within each row, sort blocks by their left coordinate.

2. Geometry Mapping
Utilize the Rect object provided by block.boundingBox for each TextLine or TextBlock.

Calculate the "Line Height" dynamically to determine if two blocks belong to the same logical line.

3. Normalization
Account for slight image rotation. If the user didn't crop perfectly (Issue #21), use the average slope of text lines to "de-skew" the coordinate logic.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
The developer should implement a sorting utility within the OCRService to clean the data:
class OCRScannerUtils {
  /// Sorts RecognizedText blocks into a logical reading order
  static List<TextBlock> sortBlocks(RecognizedText data) {
    List<TextBlock> blocks = List.from(data.blocks);

    // Sort primarily by Top (Y), then by Left (X)
    blocks.sort((a, b) {
      final rectA = a.boundingBox;
      final rectB = b.boundingBox;

      // If they are roughly on the same vertical line (15px threshold)
      if ((rectA.top - rectB.top).abs() < 15) {
        return rectA.left.compareTo(rectB.left);
      }
      return rectA.top.compareTo(rectB.top);
    });

    return blocks;
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] Row Integrity: When scanning a multi-column receipt, the "Item Name" and "Item Price" appear adjacent in the processed text list.

[ ] Top-to-Bottom Flow: The "Vendor Name" (usually at the top) consistently appears before the "Total Amount" (usually at the bottom) in the resulting array.

[ ] Tolerance Threshold: The sorting logic accounts for minor handheld camera shakes (blocks that are slightly "crooked" are still grouped into the same row).

[ ] Performance: Sorting 50+ text blocks adds less than 5ms to the total processing time.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Data Integrity: This logic is strictly structural; ensure no text content is altered or "auto-corrected" during the sorting phase to maintain the original evidence from the receipt.

Local Execution: This sorting happens entirely in memory (RAM) within the Flutter application, ensuring no sensitive financial coordinates are sent to external logs.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 21: Issue #22: Create Local OCR Service Controller`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Context

import 'dart:io';
import 'package:google_mlkit_text_recognition/google_mlkit_text_recognition.dart';

class OCRService {
  // Initialize with Latin script for Canadian receipts
  TextRecognizer? _textRecognizer;

  /// Ensures the recognizer is ready to use
  void _initialize() {
    _textRecognizer ??= TextRecognizer(script: TextRecognitionScript.latin);
  }

  /// The main processing bridge
  Future<RecognizedText> extractText(File imageFile) async {
    _initialize();
    
    final inputImage = InputImage.fromFilePath(imageFile.path);
    
    try {
      final recognizedText = await _textRecognizer!.processImage(inputImage);
      return recognizedText;
    } catch (e) {
      // Log error for debugging
      print('OCR Processing Error: $e');
      rethrow;
    }
  }

  /// MUST be called to prevent memory leaks
  void dispose() {
    _textRecognizer?.close();
    _textRecognizer = null;
  }
}

`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 18: Issue #19: Database Migration & Schema Versioning`n`n[Description]`nContext
As the application grows, the data models (Entities) will change. ObjectBox handles basic migrations (adding new entities or non-unique fields) automatically. However, complex changesΓÇösuch as renaming fields, changing types, or adding unique constraintsΓÇörequire a "Migration Strategy" using the objectbox-model.json file to maintain data integrity across app versions.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a developer, I want a reliable schema management system so that when I release an update with new data features, existing users can upgrade their app without losing their historical financial records.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. Schema Tracking (objectbox-model.json)
Strict Rule: The lib/objectbox-model.json file must be committed to Git. This file contains the UIDs (Unique Identifiers) for every entity and property.

Mechanism: ObjectBox uses these UIDs to map the compiled Dart code to the physical data on the user's disk.

2. Handling Renames & Deletions
Renaming: If a field is renamed (e.g., description to note), the developer must not simply change the name. They must use the @Property(uid: ...) annotation to tell ObjectBox that the new name refers to the old data.

Deletions: When a field is removed from the Dart class, ObjectBox will stop tracking it. However, the data remains in the database file until a manual cleanup or "Compact" operation is performed.

3. Versioning Strategy
ID Sync: Before every production release, run flutter pub run build_runner build to ensure the objectbox.g.dart and objectbox-model.json are perfectly synchronized.

Uid Conflict Resolution: If a "UID Conflict" occurs during development (common in team environments), use the ObjectBox CLI or manually revert the JSON to the last known stable state and re-generate.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Workflow Guidance
When adding a new field to an existing Entity, follow this Safe Migration Workflow:

Add the Field: Add String? newField; to your Entity class.

Generate: Run flutter pub run build_runner build. ObjectBox will automatically assign a new UID in the JSON file.

Default Values: If the new field is non-nullable, ensure the constructor provides a default value to handle old records that don't have this data.
@Entity()
class TransactionEntity {
  @Id()
  int id = 0;

  // New field added in Version 1.1.0
  String? memo; 

  // Renaming a field while keeping old data
  @Property(uid: 1234567890) // UID taken from objectbox-model.json
  String newDescriptionName; 
  
  // ...
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] objectbox-model.json is confirmed to be in the version control system (Git).

[ ] Migration Test: A test is performed where an entity is modified (field added), and the app is run over an existing database without crashing.

[ ] UID Stability: No manual tampering with UIDs has occurred without the use of the @Property annotation.

[ ] Documentation: A MIGRATION.md file is created in lib/core/data/ explaining the steps to take when changing a model.

[ ] Code Generation: build_runner completes successfully after the schema change.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Data Persistence: Ensure that migrations do not accidentally wipe the user's encrypted local database (Issue #17).

Backup: Encourage users to perform a cloud sync (once implemented) before major app updates as an extra safety measure for their financial history.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 17: Issue #18: Implementation of Change Listeners (Reactive UI)`n`n[Description]`nContext

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance

class TransactionRepository {
  final Box<TransactionEntity> _box;
  // ... constructor

  /// Provides a real-time stream of all transactions sorted by date.
  /// This automatically triggers whenever the Transaction box changes.
  Stream<List<TransactionEntity>> watchAll() {
    // Build the query
    final queryBuilder = _box.query()
      ..order(TransactionEntity_.date, flags: Order.descending);

    // .watch() returns a Stream of the query results
    // We use .map((query) => query.find()) to convert it to a List
    return queryBuilder.watch(triggerImmediately: true).map((query) {
      final list = query.find();
      // Important: Queries inside watchers don't need manual closing 
      // as the stream handles it, but verify version docs.
      return list;
    });
  }
}

[ ] Performance: The reactive stream does not block the UI thread during large database operations (ObjectBox handles this on a background thread).

[ ] Cleanup: All stream listeners are properly disposed of in the UI layer.
