Concurrency: ObjectBox is thread-safe; however, ensure`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 16: Issue #17: Local Data Encryption Strategy`n`n[Description]`nContext
Since this application stores sensitive financial data (transactions, vendor names, and potentially tax IDs), we must implement At-Rest Encryption. ObjectBox supports AES-256 encryption, but we must never hardcode the encryption key. Instead, we will generate a secure key and store it in the device's hardware-backed keychain.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a user, I want my financial data to be encrypted on my device so that even if my phone is lost or the file system is accessed by unauthorized apps, my private business transactions remain unreadable.

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. Secure Key Management
Package: Integrate flutter_secure_storage: ^9.0.0.

Logic: * On the first app launch, check if an encryption key exists in secure storage.

If not, generate a high-entropy 32-byte (256-bit) random key.

Store this key using FlutterSecureStorage (which uses Keychain on iOS and Keystore on Android).

2. ObjectBox Encryption Integration
Update the ObjectBox.create() method (from Issue #12) to accept the secret key:

Constraint: The key must be exactly 32 bytes for ObjectBox's AES-256 implementation.

Initialization: Pass the key to the openStore function via the password parameter (ObjectBox uses the term "password" for the encryption key).

3. Migration Handling
Critical: If encryption is enabled on an existing unencrypted database, the app will fail to open the store.

Strategy: For this phase, we assume encryption is enabled from Version 1.0.0. If implemented later, a manual data migration/export-import flow will be required.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
The developer should modify the ObjectBox provider to handle the secure key retrieval before opening the store:
import 'dart:convert';
import 'dart:typed_data';
import 'package:flutter_secure_storage/flutter_secure_storage.dart';

class ObjectBox {
  static const _storage = FlutterSecureStorage();
  static const _keyName = 'db_encryption_key';

  static Future<Uint8List> _getOrCreateKey() async {
    String? keyBase64 = await _storage.read(key: _keyName);
    
    if (keyBase64 == null) {
      // Generate a new 32-byte key
      final key = Uint8List.fromList(List.generate(32, (_) => Random().nextInt(256)));
      await _storage.write(key: _keyName, value: base64Encode(key));
      return key;
    }
    return base64Decode(keyBase64);
  }

  static Future<ObjectBox> create() async {
    final docsDir = await getApplicationDocumentsDirectory();
    final encryptionKey = await _getOrCreateKey();

    // Passing the 32-byte key to openStore
    final store = await openStore(
      directory: p.join(docsDir.path, "obx-secure-db"),
      macosApplicationGroup: "group.com.yourbusiness.app", // Required for Apple sandboxing
      password: encryptionKey, 
    );
    
    return ObjectBox._create(store);
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] flutter_secure_storage is correctly configured for both Android and iOS.

[ ] A 256-bit encryption key is generated and persisted in the hardware-backed storage.

[ ] The ObjectBox store is opened using the password parameter.

[ ] Verification: Attempting to open the data.mdb file (ObjectBox database file) using a hex editor or external tool results in encrypted, unreadable data.

[ ] Performance: The app remains responsive during database initialization (encryption adds a slight overhead to the initial open).

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
PIPEDA Compliance: Encryption at rest is a key requirement for protecting personal financial information in Canada.

Key Loss: Note that if the user wipes their device's "Secure Storage" or "Keychain," the database becomes permanently inaccessible. (This will be mitigated later with Cloud Sync/Backup).

Android Backup: Configure android:fullBackupContent="false" in AndroidManifest.xml to prevent the encryption key from being backed up to insecure cloud locations if not desired.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 15: Issue #16: Implement Advanced Querying & Filtering`n`n[Description]`nContext

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
ObjectBox queries are built using a fluent API. The developer should implement these in the TransactionRepository:
class TransactionRepository {
  final Box<TransactionEntity> _box;
  // ... constructor

  /// Get transactions for a specific period, sorted by date
  List<TransactionEntity> getTransactionsByDate(DateTime start, DateTime end) {
    final query = _box
        .query(TransactionEntity_.date.between(
          start.millisecondsSinceEpoch, 
          end.millisecondsSinceEpoch
        ))
        .order(TransactionEntity_.date, flags: Order.descending)
        .build();
    
    final results = query.find();
    query.close(); // Always close queries to free resources
    return results;
  }

  /// Search transactions by keyword in description
  List<TransactionEntity> searchTransactions(String text) {
    final query = _box
        .query(TransactionEntity_.description.contains(text, caseSensitive: false))
        .build();
    
    return query.find();
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] getByDateRange returns the correct records within the specified millisecond precision.

[ ] Performance: Searching through 1,000+ records completes in under 16ms (maintaining 60 FPS).

[ ] Sorting: All filtered results are returned in descending chronological order by default.

[ ] Resource Management: All Query objects are properly closed using .close() to prevent memory leaks.

[ ] Case Insensitivity: Searching for "coffee" also returns results containing "Coffee" or "COFFEE".

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance

@Entity()
class TransactionEntity {
  @Id()
  int id = 0;
  
  double amount;
  
  @Index()
  DateTime date;

  // Relations
  final category = ToOne<CategoryEntity>();
  final vendor = ToOne<VendorEntity>();

  TransactionEntity({required this.amount, required this.date});
}

@Entity()
class CategoryEntity {
  @Id()
  int id = 0;
  
  @Unique()
  String name;

  // Backlink: Allows finding all transactions for a category
  @Backlink('category')
  final transactions = ToMany<TransactionEntity>();

  CategoryEntity({required this.name});
}

@Entity()
class TransactionEntity {
  @Id()

  @Unique()

ObjectBox requires a single instance of Store to manage data operations across the application efficiently. This issue focuses on creating a robust wrapper class to handle the database lifecycle, including initialization at app startup and proper disposal. This ensures thread safety and prevents multiple database instances from locking the storage file.

≡ƒæñ User Story

ΓÜÖ∩╕Å Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. ObjectBox Manager Class

Implement the Singleton pattern to ensure only one Store exists.

Encapsulate the Store and Box instances.

2. Initialization Logic (init())
The init() method must be asynchronous and perform the following:

Locate the application's document directory using path_provider.

Join the directory path with a specific database folder name (e.g., objectbox-db).

Call openStore(directory: path) from the generated objectbox.g.dart file.

Provide a getter for the Store instance.

3. Lifecycle Management
Setup: Call the initialization in main.dart before runApp().

Teardown: Implement a close() method to safely shut down the store when the app process is terminated (primarily for testing and background tasks).

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
The developer should follow this structure for the ObjectBox provider:
import 'package:path/path.dart' as p;
import 'package:path_provider/path_provider.dart';
import '../../objectbox.g.dart'; // The generated file

class ObjectBox {
  late final Store store;

  // Private constructor
  ObjectBox._create(this.store);

  /// Create an instance of ObjectBox to use throughout the app.
  static Future<ObjectBox> create() async {
    final docsDir = await getApplicationDocumentsDirectory();
    final storePath = p.join(docsDir.path, "obx-fintech-db");

    // openStore is generated by ObjectBox
    final store = await openStore(directory: storePath);
    return ObjectBox._create(store);
  }
}
Integration in main.dart:
late ObjectBox objectbox;

Future<void> main() async {
  WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();
  
  // Initialize the singleton database
  objectbox = await ObjectBox.create();
  
  runApp(MyApp());
}

[ ] The application starts and reaches the Dashboard without any "Store already open" or "Failed to open store" errors.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
The developer should use a LayoutBuilder at the root of major screens to inject responsiveness:
class ResponsiveLayout extends StatelessWidget {
  final Widget mobile;
  final Widget tablet;

  const ResponsiveLayout({super.key, required this.mobile, required this.tablet});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return LayoutBuilder(
      builder: (context, constraints) {
        if (constraints.maxWidth < 600) {
          return mobile;
        } else {
          // Add a MaxWidth constraint for tablets to keep the UI centered
          return Center(
            child: ConstrainedBox(
              constraints: const BoxConstraints(maxWidth: 1024),
              child: tablet,
            ),
          );
        }
      },
    );
  }
}

      duration: const Duration(milliseconds: 600),

      switchOutCurve: Curves.easeOut,
      child: MaterialApp(

        darkTheme: darkTheme,

class ThemeToggle extends StatelessWidget {
  const ThemeToggle({super.key});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    final isDark = AdaptiveTheme.of(context).mode.isDark;

    return GestureDetector(
      onTap: () {

        HapticFeedback.mediumImpact();

      child: AnimatedContainer(
        duration: const Duration(milliseconds: 300),
        width: 70,
        height: 35,
        padding: const EdgeInsets.symmetric(horizontal: 4),
        decoration: BoxDecoration(
          borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(20),
          color: isDark 
              ? Colors.blueGrey.withOpacity(0.2) 
              : Colors.orangeAccent.withOpacity(0.2),
          border: Border.all(
            color: isDark ? Colors.blue.withOpacity(0.5) : Colors.orange.withOpacity(0.5),
          ),
        ),
        child: AnimatedAlign(
          duration: const Duration(milliseconds: 300),
          curve: Curves.easeInOutBack,
          alignment: isDark ? Alignment.centerRight : Alignment.centerLeft,
          child: Container(
            width: 28,
            height: 28,
            decoration: const BoxDecoration(
              shape: BoxShape.circle,
              color: Colors.white,
              boxShadow: [BoxShadow(color: Colors.black12, blurRadius: 4)],
            ),
            child: AnimatedSwitcher(
              duration: const Duration(milliseconds: 200),
              child: Icon(
                isDark ? Icons.nightlight_round : Icons.wb_sunny_rounded,
                key: ValueKey(isDark),
                size: 18,
                color: isDark ? Colors.indigo : Colors.orange,
              ),
            ),
          ),
        ),
      ),
    );
  }
}

To maximize screen real estate and maintain a modern look, the AppBar should not be a static, solid block. Instead, it should start as fully transparent (or semi-transparent) and gradually increase its Glassmorphism intensity (blur and background opacity) as the user scrolls down the content.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a user, I want the header to be unobtrusive while I am at the top of my dashboard, but I want it to become more defined and "solid glass" as I scroll, so that the navigation controls remain visible and legible over the scrolling content.

≡ƒÄ¿ Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. Scroll-Listener Logic
The developer must implement a ScrollController listener or use a SliverPersistentHeader to track the vertical offset (pixels).

Threshold: The transition should happen between 0 and 100 pixels of scroll.

Interpolation: Calculate the opacity value linearly: opacity = (scrollOffset / threshold).clamp(0.0, 1.0).

2. Visual Composition
Base: A PreferredSizeWidget or SliverAppBar.

Background: A BackdropFilter wrapped in an Opacity or AnimatedContainer.

Content: * Left: User Profile Avatar or Business Logo.

Center: Page Title (e.g., "Good Morning, Ahmad").

Right: Action Icons (Notifications/Search) using NeonIcon.

3. Glassmorphism Adaptation
At scroll = 0: Blur = 0, Opacity = 0.0, Border = Transparent.

At scroll > 100: Blur = 15.0, Opacity = 0.2, Border = Subtle White/Grey.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
Using a NotificationListener<ScrollNotification> is often more performant than a standard listener for simple opacity changes:
class DynamicAppBar extends StatelessWidget implements PreferredSizeWidget {
  final double scrollOffset;
  final String title;

  const DynamicAppBar({super.key, required this.scrollOffset, required this.title});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    // Calculate progress (0.0 to 1.0) based on 100px scroll
    final double opacityProgress = (scrollOffset / 100).clamp(0.0, 1.0);

    return ClipRRect(
      child: BackdropFilter(
        filter: ImageFilter.blur(
          sigmaX: opacityProgress * 15, 
          sigmaY: opacityProgress * 15
        ),
        child: AppBar(
          elevation: 0,
          backgroundColor: Theme.of(context)
              .colorScheme
              .surface
              .withOpacity(opacityProgress * 0.7),
          title: Text(
            title,
            style: TextStyle(
              color: Theme.of(context).colorScheme.onSurface.withOpacity(
                opacityProgress.clamp(0.5, 1.0) // Keep text visible but fade in intensity
              ),
            ),
          ),
          centerTitle: true,
          flexibleSpace: Container(
            decoration: BoxDecoration(
              border: Border(
                bottom: BorderSide(
                  color: Colors.white.withOpacity(opacityProgress * 0.1),
                  width: 1.0,
                ),
              ),
            ),
          ),
        ),
      ),
    );
  }

  @override
  Size get preferredSize => const Size.fromHeight(kToolbarHeight);
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] The AppBar is 100% transparent when the scroll position is at the very top.

[ ] Fluidity: The transition of blur and color is smooth (60 FPS) and does not "snap" abruptly.

[ ] Content Safety: The Title and Action icons remain legible throughout the entire scroll range.

[ ] Theme Support: The background color of the AppBar correctly pulls from Theme.of(context).colorScheme.surface for both Light and Dark modes.

[ ] System Status Bar: The status bar icons (clock, battery) remain visible and contrast correctly with the AppBar color.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Privacy: Ensure that sensitive information (like specific account balances) in the AppBar (if any) is obscured or simplified when the AppBar is in its "compact/scrolled" state.

Consistency: The behavior must be consistent across both iOS (bouncing scroll) and Android (clamped scroll) behaviors.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 5: Issue #5: Build Main BottomNavigationBar (Translucent Style)`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Context

Shape: Rounded corners (BorderRadius: 24.0) and a subtle horizontal margin from the screen edges (e.g., 16.0).

Icons: Use the NeonIcon widget from Issue #4 for the active state.

2. Navigation Logic
State Management: Use a PageIndex provider (Cubit/Provider/Riverpod) to track the active tab.

Page Controller: Implement a PageView (with physics disabled) or an IndexedStack to preserve the state of each page (Home, Inbox, etc.) as the user switches tabs.

Animated Transitions: Use AnimatedContainer or AnimatedOpacity for the icon "active" indicator (e.g., a glowing dot or a sliding pill background).

3. Navigation Items
Home: Financial Dashboard & OCR Access.

Inbox: Smart notifications and AI insights.

Marketplace (B2B): Product showcase and supplier search.

Settings: Profile, Theme Toggle (Issue #2), and Business Info.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
To allow the content to scroll behind the navigation bar, set extendBody: true in the Scaffold.
// Suggested Structure for Floating Glass Nav Bar

To complement the Glassmorphism UI, icons must not appear "flat." We need a specialized icon wrapper that applies a Neon Glow (Bloom) effect. In Dark Mode, these icons should appear as light sources, while in Light Mode, they should maintain a glossy, high-contrast professional look.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a user, I want the navigation and action icons to be visually distinct and "glowing," making the interface feel futuristic, interactive, and premium.

≡ƒÄ¿ Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. The "Neon Glow" Logic
The developer must create a reusable widget NeonIcon that wraps a standard IconData. The "Glow" is achieved by stacking a blurred version of the icon behind the primary icon.

2. Styling Parameters
Primary Color: Use Theme.of(context).colorScheme.primary or secondary.

Glow Intensity: blurRadius should be between 8.0 and 15.0.

Spread: The shadow color should be the same as the icon color but with a lower opacity (e.g., 0.5).

Theme Mapping: * Dark Mode: High intensity glow (Neon effect).

Light Mode: Subtle soft shadow (Glossy effect) to maintain professional legibility.

3. Component Architecture
The widget should utilize the BoxShadow property or a Stack with ImageFiltered for the most realistic bloom effect.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
The developer should implement the NeonIcon using a ShaderMask or a custom Shadow list for performance:
class NeonIcon extends StatelessWidget {
  final IconData icon;
  final double size;
  final Color? color;

  const NeonIcon({
    super.key,
    required this.icon,
    this.size = 28.0,
    this.color,
  });

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    final themeColor = color ?? Theme.of(context).colorScheme.primary;
    final isDark = Theme.of(context).brightness == Brightness.dark;

    return Icon(
      icon,
      size: size,
      color: themeColor,
      shadows: [
        // The Neon Glow Effect
        Shadow(
          color: themeColor.withOpacity(isDark ? 0.8 : 0.3),
          blurRadius: isDark ? 15.0 : 5.0,
        ),
        if (isDark)
          Shadow(
            color: themeColor.withOpacity(0.6),
            blurRadius: 30.0,
          ),
      ],
    );
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] NeonIcon widget created in lib/core/widgets/neon_icon.dart.

[ ] Performance: The glow effect must use standard Shadow properties to avoid the heavy performance cost of multiple BackdropFilter layers.

[ ] Visual Consistency: The glow color must dynamically match the IconData color passed to the widget.

[ ] Legibility: Icons must remain sharp; the glow should appear around and behind the icon, not distorting the icon shape itself.

[ ] Theme Switching: The glow intensity must automatically reduce in Light Mode to prevent a "washed out" look on white backgrounds.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Accessibility: Ensure that even with the glow effect, the core icon shape is recognizable for users with visual impairments.

Contrast: Maintain a minimum contrast of 3:1 for graphical objects against the background surface as per WCAG 2.1 guidelines.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 3: Issue #3: Create Reusable GlassCard Widget (Glassmorphism Core)`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Context
To achieve a high-end, modern Fintech UI, we are implementing a Glassmorphism design language. This reusable widget will serve as the primary container for all financial data cards, providing a semi-transparent, frosted-glass effect that works across both Light and Dark themes.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a user, I want a visually distinct and modern interface where data cards feel layered and depth-oriented, making the financial information easy to read and aesthetically pleasing.

≡ƒÄ¿ Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. Core Visual Parameters
The developer must create a stateless widget GlassCard with the following configurable properties:

double blur: The sigma value for the Gaussian blur (Default: 10.0).

double opacity: The background color alpha (Default: 0.1 for Light, 0.05 for Dark).

BorderRadius borderRadius: Corner smoothness (Default: 16.0).

Widget child: The content to be hosted inside the card.

EdgeInsets padding: Internal spacing for the content.

2. Implementation Layers
The widget must be constructed using a Stack or BackdropFilter approach to ensure the background is blurred correctly:

ClipRRect: To ensure the blur effect doesn't "leak" outside the rounded corners.

BackdropFilter: Using ImageFilter.blur(sigmaX: blur, sigmaY: blur).

Container (The Glass Surface): * Fill: Theme.of(context).colorScheme.surface.withOpacity(opacity).

Border: A subtle, semi-transparent border to simulate the "glass edge" (e.g., onSurface.withOpacity(0.12)).

Gradient Border (Optional/Advanced): A LinearGradient stroke from top-left to bottom-right to simulate light reflection.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
The developer should follow this architectural pattern for the GlassCard:
class GlassCard extends StatelessWidget {
  final Widget child;
  final double blur;
  final double opacity;
  final double borderRadius;

  const GlassCard({
    super.key,
    required this.child,
    this.blur = 15.0,
    this.opacity = 0.2,
    this.borderRadius = 20.0,
  });

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return ClipRRect(
      borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(borderRadius),
      child: BackdropFilter(
        filter: ImageFilter.blur(sigmaX: blur, sigmaY: blur),
        child: Container(
          decoration: BoxDecoration(
            color: Theme.of(context).colorScheme.surface.withOpacity(opacity),
            borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(borderRadius),
            border: Border.all(
              width: 1.5,
              color: Theme.of(context).colorScheme.onSurface.withOpacity(0.1),
            ),
          ),
          child: child,
        ),
      ),
    );
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] The GlassCard widget is created in lib/core/widgets/glass_card.dart.

[ ] Performance: The blur effect does not cause frame drops on mid-range Android/iOS devices (avoid over-nesting BackdropFilter).

[ ] Adaptability: The card automatically looks correct in both Light and Dark modes (using onSurface for the border color).

[ ] Geometry: The clipping (ClipRRect) perfectly matches the BorderRadius to prevent sharp corners on the blur layer.

[ ] Flexibility: The widget accepts any Widget as a child and expands to fit its content or parent constraints.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Visual Accessibility: Ensure that text placed inside the GlassCard maintains a contrast ratio of 4.5:1 against the blurred background. If the background is too busy, the opacity of the card surface should be programmatically increased.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 2: Issue #2: Implement AdaptiveTheme Manager (Theme Persistence)`n`n[Description]`n≡ƒô¥ Context
To provide a seamless user experience, the application must remember the user's theme preference (Light, Dark, or System Default) across sessions. This issue focuses on integrating a robust theme management system that handles real-time switching and local persistence.

≡ƒæñ User Story
As a user, I want the app to remember my preferred theme (e.g., Dark Mode) every time I reopen the application, so I don't have to manually re-adjust the settings.

≡ƒÄ¿ Technical Requirements (Flutter Implementation)
1. Dependency Integration
Add the following package to pubspec.yaml:

adaptive_theme: ^3.4.0 (or the latest stable version).

Note: While Flutter has built-in ThemeMode, adaptive_theme is preferred for this project as it simplifies the boilerplate for SharedPreferences integration and provides smooth transitions.

2. Root Wrapper Setup
The developer must wrap the root MaterialApp with AdaptiveTheme in main.dart:

Initial Theme: Retrieve the saved theme from local storage; if null, default to AdaptiveThemeMode.system.

Light Theme: Link to AppTheme.light() from Issue #1.

Dark Theme: Link to AppTheme.dark() from Issue #1.

3. State Management & Persistence
Storage: Use SharedPreferences (managed internally by the package) to store the theme_mode index.

Service Layer: Create a simple ThemeService or ThemeController to encapsulate the toggle logic:

void toggleTheme(): Switches between Light and Dark.

void setSystemTheme(): Reverts to following the OS settings.

≡ƒ¢á Logic & Code Guidance
The implementation should follow this structure in the main entry point:
void main() async {
  WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();
  final savedThemeMode = await AdaptiveTheme.getThemeMode();
  runApp(MyApp(savedThemeMode: savedThemeMode));
}

class MyApp extends StatelessWidget {
  final AdaptiveThemeMode? savedThemeMode;
  const MyApp({super.key, this.savedThemeMode});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return AdaptiveTheme(
      light: AppTheme.light(),
      dark: AppTheme.dark(),
      initial: savedThemeMode ?? AdaptiveThemeMode.system,
      builder: (theme, darkTheme) => MaterialApp(
        theme: theme,
        darkTheme: darkTheme,
        home: const DashboardScreen(),
      ),
    );
  }
}
Γ£à Acceptance Criteria (Definition of Done)
[ ] The app correctly detects and applies the system theme on the first launch.

[ ] Users can manually switch between Light and Dark modes via a settings toggle.

[ ] Persistence: Closing and killing the app process does not reset the theme selection.

[ ] No "Flash of Unstyled Content" (FOUC) ΓÇô the correct theme must be applied before the first frame is drawn (handled in main).

[ ] The theme transition animation is smooth and does not cause UI jank.

≡ƒöÉ Security & Compliance
Data Privacy: Ensure no personal identifiable information (PII) is stored alongside the theme preference in local storage.

Compliance: The implementation must respect the "System-wide" dark mode settings as per Apple and Google accessibility guidelines for Canada.`n`n[Comments]`n`n---------------------------------------------------`n# Issue 1: Issue #1: Define Design System & Brand Palette (Light/Dark Mode)`n`n[Description]`nIssue #1: Define Design System & Brand Palette (Light/Dark Mode)
